# auth — behaviour specification

This document says what the `auth` plugin does, feature by feature, and what it
must never do: the security, consistency, performance and failure properties an
application built on it relies on. It is the reference the plugin's tests and
the example projects are measured against. A property listed here without a
proof is a gap, not a promise.

It covers plugin version 0.1.0-rc.24.

## How to read it

- Every **invariant** is one MUST or MUST NOT statement about observable
  behaviour, with a stable id (`<feature>/<name>`, `interaction/<a>-<b>-<name>`,
  `auth/<name>`). Ids do not change when the wording does, so tests and
  examples can cite them.
- **Categories:** Security · Consistency & concurrency · Performance · Failure &
  configuration.
- **Proof** names the unit tests in `auth/src/handlers` (or `auth/src/lib/…`)
  that demonstrate the invariant. Those tests run the handlers against
  in-memory fakes of the generated storage clients. They prove sequencing and
  wire shape, not what only the database can enforce (a unique index, a
  conditional `UPDATE … RETURNING` under concurrency, a `NOW()`
  comparison, a transaction rollback, column encryption). Such rows say
  **unit (mock) only** and name what a live test would have to show. `none`
  means nothing proves it yet.
- **public** means the REST preset endpoint takes no credential
  (`exclude_auth`). **failed_only** means the gateway's per-address limiter
  charges such an endpoint only for a failed request; a public endpoint
  without it is charged for every request.
- **Gaps / suspected defects** after each feature are what the code does not
  enforce although the invariant requires it, found by reading the code. The
  ones re-checked against the code are collected under
  [Verified findings](#verified-findings).

## Verified findings

Gaps confirmed by re-reading the handlers and the DQL. Each is a defect to fix
before an example may claim the invariant; the invariant id says which. Row 7
is the exception: it is read from the DQL and the manifest, and stays a
suspected defect until a build of that feature combination confirms it.

| # | finding | invariants | where |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Fixed.** The OAuth `state` was an HMAC over a nonce, the provider and the redirect, and nothing else: no expiry, not single-use, not bound to the browser that started the flow, and the callback carried the session. A callback URL captured by one person signed whoever opened it into that person's account (login CSRF). Now the browser sends a PKCE challenge at authorize, the state expires, the callback hands out only a single-use login code bound to that challenge, and `OAuthExchange` redeems it with the verifier. PKCE and an OIDC nonce go to the provider too. | `oauth/*` (state), `interaction/oauth-*` | `src/handlers/oauth.go` (`signOAuthState`, `verifyOAuthState`) |
| 2 | **Fixed.** `CliAuthorize` accepted any authenticated caller, whatever its realm, and `CliToken` then minted a full SESSION token with a fresh lifetime. A session could renew itself indefinitely, and an API token whose permissions include the CLI endpoints could become an unnarrowed session. Since rc.25 `CliAuthorize` refuses any caller that did not authenticate with a session (`SESSION_REQUIRED`). The renewal is closed by inheritance: `CliAuthorize` reads the approving session's `expires_at` (`GetLiveSessionExpiry`, live rows of the caller only; a missing or expired row refuses) and binds it to the code (`CliAuthCode.session_expires_at`), and `CliToken` mints with min(normal session TTL from now, that expiry), with no floor. A non-expiring approving session gives the normal TTL. | `interaction/api_token-cli_login-realm-escalation`, `cli_login/*` | `src/handlers/cli_login.go` (`CliAuthorize`, `CliToken`) |
| 3 | **Fixed in rc.25.** `CreateApiToken` used only the caller's user id: it checked neither the caller's realm nor its permission subset, so a narrowed API token that may call it minted a token with every API-realm permission of its holder. `CreateApiToken` now requires a session caller and refuses an API token (`PERMISSION_DENIED`, `SESSION_REQUIRED`). | `api_token/*` (narrow-only) | `src/handlers/apitoken.go` (`CreateApiToken`) |
| 4 | **Fixed in rc.25.** Token authentication never read `disabled_at`; disabling relied on deleting the tokens. `IssueBotToken` did not check it either, so a working token could be minted for a disabled machine account, and a sign-in racing a disable could leave a live session. Both resolvers now run the disabled gate on the token's owner, and `IssueBotToken` refuses a disabled machine account. | `interaction/user_admin-service_account-disabled-bot-token`, `user_admin/*` | `src/handlers/service_account.go` (`IssueBotToken`), token lookups in `proto/queries/auth_query.proto` |
| 5 | **Fixed in rc.25 (the race).** The first-user role decision (`CountUsers == 0` → `first_user` roles) was read before the sign-up transaction and nothing serialised it: concurrent first sign-ups could each become a bootstrap administrator. The first_user set is now assigned only to the sign-up that writes the `BootstrapClaim` row inside its transaction; the others get the default roles, and a claim once written is never reopened. Only a sign-up writes the claim: a seeded first account, or a deployment that had users before rc.25, holds none, so deleting every account there reopens the bootstrap. That the first stranger to register on an open deployment becomes the administrator is documented, not changed: seed the first account before opening sign-up. | `interaction/rbac-sign_up-first-user-race` | `src/handlers/signup_roles.go` (`resolveSignupRoleIDs`) |
| 6 | **Fixed.** A successful password reset consumed only the token used; every other live reset link of the account still worked afterwards, also after a password change. Both now spend every live link of the account (`SpendPasswordResetTokens`) in the same transaction as the new password. | `password_reset/*` | `src/handlers/reset.go`, `ConsumePasswordResetToken` |
| 7 | **Fixed.** `RevokeOtherSessionTokens` (then `DeleteOtherSessionTokens`, `password_change`) filtered on `token_type`, a column that exists only with `api_token`, without a feature guard, so `password_change` without `api_token` could not generate. The filter is now `?feature(api_token) token_type = 1`, and `make check-stage` reports any statement that names another feature's column ungated. | `interaction/password_change-without-api_token-build` | `proto/mutations/auth_mutation.proto` (`DeleteOtherSessionTokens`) |
| 8 | **Fixed (trust); Decided (OAuth second factor).** An OAuth sign-in skips the second factor and, with `devices`, used to mark the device trusted; under `two_factor_scope=new_devices` later password sign-ins from that device then skipped the second factor too. The OAuth exchange now binds the session to the device without trusting it. A password change and disabling an account used to leave device trust in place; both now stamp `User.device_trust_revoked_at` in the statement that changes the password or disables the account, and a device trusted at or before that instant counts as untrusted at sign-in and in `ListDevices`, the caller's own device included. A reset keeps trust by design (`interaction/credential-change-device-trust`). **Decided:** an OAuth sign-in does not ask for this plugin's second factor, also for an account that has one confirmed: the provider already verified the identity, and asking for a second factor at the provider and again here is not wanted. | `interaction/oauth-devices-two_factor-trust-laundering`, `oauth/second-factor-policy`, `interaction/credential-change-device-trust` | `src/handlers/oauth.go`, `src/handlers/devices.go`, `UpdateUserPasswordIfUnchanged`, `DisableUsers` |
| 9 | **Fixed.** A session was a bearer token that worked from anywhere; the `devices` feature named a device by a client-chosen `X-Device-Id` (or a UA+IP fingerprint), so two clients could claim one device, "revoke others" kept whatever matched the claimed id, and SignUp, ConfirmSignUp and CliToken minted sessions with no device at all. Now a device is the key its client signs DPoP proofs with: every mint requires the gateway's possession thumbprint (`x-w17-dpop-jkt`) and binds the token to the (user, thumbprint) Device, Authenticate refuses any request whose proof is not by the bound key, a revoked device is refused for good, and trust belongs to the (user, device) pair. Sessions minted before this are refused once. API tokens (`CreateApiToken`, `IssueBotToken`) are bound to the public key their request names — the program's, never the creating browser's — after a version that refused to issue any rather than mint unbound. | `device/*` | `src/handlers/device.go`, `auth_service.go` (`authenticate`), every mint path, `RegisterDevice`, the token lookups, `apitoken.go` (`registerKeyDevice`), `lib/devicekey` |

## Per feature

### (base tier)

- **Purpose.** The identity record and the bearer-token store every other feature builds on. It is always present.
- **Adds.** Tables `User` (`email` unique, `password_hash` PASSWORD-typed) and `UserToken` (`token_hash` unique, sha256 hex; legacy `token` nullable and unique; `user_id` indexed; `expires_at` nullable). Queries `GetUserByEmail`, `GetUserByToken`, `GetUserById`. Mutations `CreateUser` (emits `UserCreated`), `IssueToken` (emits `TokenIssued`, never carrying the token), `RevokeToken`, `UpgradeTokenHash`, `UpdateUserPassword`. No REST endpoints. Token revocation columns `UserToken.revoked_at`, `revoked_by`, `revoke_reason` (enum `RevokeReason`) — see device identity: nothing in the auth tables is deleted.
- **Env knobs.** `password_hash_algo` (argon2id | bcrypt | scrypt | pbkdf2; an unknown value fails `RegisterPlugin`), `session_token_ttl_seconds` (default 30 days; `<= 0` uses the default).
- **Requires / conflicts / combined files.** None.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| `base/token-stored-as-hash` | A minted bearer token MUST be stored only as its sha256 digest. The plaintext MUST be returned to the caller exactly once and MUST NOT appear in any column or event. | Security | `TestNewBearerToken` (digest ≠ token, two mints differ). Whether `IssueToken` binds only `token_hash` is visible in the DQL, not tested. |
| `base/token-entropy` | A bearer token MUST come from a CSPRNG with at least 256 bits of entropy (64 characters from `randstr.Token`). | Security | `TestNewBearerToken` (length and non-repetition only). |
| `base/stored-digest-is-not-a-credential` | Presenting a stored `token_hash` value as a bearer MUST NOT authenticate. The lookup hashes the presented value, and the legacy column is NULL on every hashed row. | Security | `TestAuthenticate_AStoredDigestIsNotACredential` (both resolvers, session and API token; the store matches with the statement's own predicate), `TestNoMintWritesTheLegacyTokenColumn` (no INSERT into `UserToken` assigns `token`) |
| `base/legacy-token-migrates-once` | A legacy row (plaintext token, no hash) resolved by a lookup MUST be moved to `token_hash`, with `token` cleared, by a statement guarded on `token_hash IS NULL`. A concurrent second upgrade MUST change nothing. A failed upgrade MUST NOT fail the request. The move MUST be written only for a request that passed every check, the device binding included: a refused request (a stolen legacy token with the wrong key, no proof) MUST write nothing. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestUpgradeLegacyToken`, `TestResolvePrincipalSession_LegacyRowIsMovedToTheHash`, `TestResolvePrincipalRealmAware_LegacyRowIsMovedToTheHash`, `TestAuthenticate_ARefusedRequestNeverUpgradesALegacyToken`. **Unit (mock) only:** a live test would race two requests carrying one legacy token and assert that exactly one `UPDATE` matched and the row ends with the correct hash and a NULL `token`. |
| `base/session-ttl-stamped` | Every session token MUST be minted with `expires_at = now + session_token_ttl_seconds`, falling back to 30 days when the value is `<= 0`. It MUST NOT be minted with a zero or NULL expiry. The one exception is earlier, never later: a CLI session ends no later than the session that approved it (`cli_login/no-lifetime-extension`). | Security | `TestSessionTokenExpiry_ConfiguredTTL`, `TestSessionTokenExpiry_DefaultWhenUnset`, `TestIssueSession_StampsExpiry`, `TestCliToken_ANonExpiringApproverGivesTheNormalTTL` |
| `base/expired-token-refused` | A token with `expires_at <= NOW()` MUST NOT resolve in any token lookup. | Security | none. A live test would mint a token with a past `expires_at` and assert `Authenticate` refuses it. The predicate is SQL-only. |
| `base/expired-token-says-so` | A bearer that EXISTS and is past `expires_at`, presented with a proof by the key it is bound to, MUST be refused with Unauthenticated carrying `ErrorDetail{code: SESSION_EXPIRED}` (translated sentence naming the expiry) and `google.rpc.ErrorInfo{reason: SESSION_EXPIRED, domain: auth.w17, metadata.expires_at: RFC 3339 UTC}`. An unknown token, a failed expiry probe, an expired token presented with another key or bound to none (the thief of a token learns nothing from it, `device/proof-required`), and an outage MUST keep their previous answers (opaque refusal with no detail; Unavailable), and the probe runs only on the live lookup's NotFound. An expired row MUST NOT be moved to the hash or stamped as used. | Security | `TestAuthenticate_ExpiredSessionSaysSo`, `TestAuthenticate_ExpiredApiTokenSaysSo`, `TestAuthenticate_UnknownTokenStaysOpaque`, `TestAuthenticate_LookupOutageIsNotProbedForExpiry`, `TestResolvePrincipalSession_ExpiredVsUnknown`, `TestAuthenticate_AThiefLearnsNothingAboutTheToken` (expired, another key), `TestSessionExpired_StatesUTC`, `TestSessionExpiryStatements` (`GetExpiredToken`, `GetExpiredTokenWithType` match expired rows only). **Unit (mock) only:** the `expires_at <= NOW()` predicate is SQL. |
| `base/expired-sessions-swept-at-mint` | **Retired:** minting deleted the user's expired sessions. Nothing in the auth tables is deleted any more (`device/revocation-is-a-state`): `IssueToken` is one INSERT, and an expired token stays as history, refused by its `expires_at`. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestIssueToken_SweepsNothing`, `TestSessionExpiryStatements` (one INSERT, no DELETE) |
| `base/password-hash-at-rest` | `User.password_hash` MUST hold only a salted, PHC-encoded hash from the configured algorithm. An empty plaintext MUST NOT be hashed. | Security | `TestHashVerify_RoundTrip`, `TestHash_DifferentSaltPerCall`, `TestHash_RejectsEmptyPlain` (`lib/passwordhash`) |
| `base/email-canonical` | Every write and lookup of `User.email` MUST use one canonical form (trimmed, lower-cased), so that two spellings cannot become two accounts. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestSignUpThenSignIn_EmailSpellingDoesNotSplitTheAccount`, `TestSignIn_NormalizesEmailForLookup` (handler paths only; see gaps) |
| `base/email-unique` | At most one `User` row MUST exist per address. The guarantee MUST come from the unique index, not from a read-then-insert. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestSignUp_ATakenAddressSaysSignInInstead`. **Unit (mock) only:** a live test would run two concurrent `CreateUser` calls for one address and assert one row plus one `EMAIL_TAKEN`. |
| `base/token-lookup-indexed` | Every token lookup MUST be served by a unique index (`token_hash`, or legacy `token`). Role resolution MUST be served by the `UserRole.user_id` and `RolePermission.role_id` indexes. | Performance | none (schema only) |
| `base/unknown-hash-algo-fails-boot` | An unrecognised `password_hash_algo` MUST fail `RegisterPlugin`. It MUST NOT fall back silently. | Failure & configuration | `TestHash_RejectsUnknownAlgo` (library), `TestRegisterPlugin_UnknownPasswordHashAlgoFailsBoot` (the boot path) |

**Gaps / suspected defects**

1. **Canonical email is a handler convention, not a storage rule.** `normalizeEmail` runs on every handler path that writes or looks up an address: SignUp, SignIn, password reset, OAuth, and (fixed in rc.44) the admin's `UpdateAccount` and `CreateBot`. The statements themselves (`AuthMutation.CreateUser`, `UpdateUser`) still write `:email` as given and the unique index on `User.email` is case-sensitive, so a fixture or hand-written code that calls them directly with upper-case letters writes an address `SignIn` can never find.
2. **Fixed: a weak password hash is upgraded at sign-in.** `SignIn` passes `VerifyAndRotate` an update (`rehashOnSignIn`): when the stored hash is weaker than the current settings (another algorithm, a lower cost), the same password is stored rehashed through `RehashUserPassword` — a compare-and-swap on the hash that was verified, so a password changed or reset meanwhile is never overwritten, and a write that touches the hash only (no trust ended, nothing revoked: the credential is the same). A failed or lost write never fails the sign-in; the next one tries again. Only after the right password, so it can be neither triggered nor timed without it (`TestSignIn_RehashesAWeakHash`, `TestSignIn_RehashesOnlyAWeakHashAfterTheRightPassword`, `TestSignIn_ARehashThatCannotBeWrittenStillSignsIn`, `TestRehashUserPassword_IsAGuardedHashOnlyWrite`).
3. **Unhashed legacy tokens persist until a later release drops the column.** A legacy token that is never presented stays in plaintext at rest. This is documented, but nothing enforces or reports how many remain.
4. **No password policy.** The only plugin-level rule is "non-empty" (the PASSWORD field is required), and there is no maximum length either. Fixed: under `password_hash_algo=bcrypt` a password longer than 72 bytes is refused on its field (`PASSWORD_TOO_LONG`, InvalidArgument) by sign-up, confirmation, reset and change, instead of surfacing as an internal error.

---

### device identity (base tier)

- **Purpose.** Every token is bound to the device it was issued to, and a device is a key pair its client holds. The id of a device is the RFC 7638 thumbprint of its public key; the client proves possession on every request with a DPoP proof (RFC 9449). The gateway verifies the proof and hands this plugin only the thumbprint: as gRPC metadata `x-w17-dpop-jkt` on a mint request, and as the `AuthReq.headers` entry of the same name on Authenticate. Both are in the gateway-owned `x-w17-` namespace that every surface strips from client input.
- **Adds.** Table `Device` (unique `(user_id, thumbprint)`; `thumbprint` CHAR(43), NULL only on legacy rows kept as history; `label`, `ip`, `trusted_at`, `last_seen_at`, `created_at`, `revoked_at`); columns `UserToken.device_id` (nullable for rows minted before binding, indexed) and `User.device_trust_revoked_at`. Query `GetDeviceTrustRevokedAt`; the token lookups (`GetUserByTokenWithPermissions`, `GetTokenWithType`) read the binding through `LEFT JOIN Device`. Mutations `RegisterDevice` (one UPSERT on the pair) and `TrustDevice`; `IssueToken` requires `device_id`. `AuthResp.device_id`. Refusal codes `DEVICE_PROOF_REQUIRED` (no proof, before any lookup), `DEVICE_REVOKED` (to the bound key only) (ErrorDetail + `google.rpc.ErrorInfo`, domain `auth.w17`), and, for the key an API-token request names, `DEVICE_KEY_REQUIRED`, `DEVICE_KEY_INVALID`, `DEVICE_KEY_REVOKED` and `BOT_KEY_MISMATCH` (ErrorDetail only, never Unauthenticated). `Device.api_key` (a program's key, named for an API token; `RegisterDeviceReq.api_key`, written on the INSERT only). `lib/devicekey`: the strict public-JWK reader and RFC 7638 thumbprint (stdlib only). The sign-in history: table `SignInEvent` (append-only; `id`, `created_at`, `user_id` and `device_id` nullable, `address` CHAR(320), `thumbprint` CHAR(43) nullable, `ip`, `user_agent`, `method` (enum `SignInMethod`), `outcome` (enum `SignInOutcome`); indexes `(user_id, created_at)`, `(address, created_at)`, `created_at`. **`SignInEvent.device_id` is the Device ROW's UUID**, like `UserToken.device_id`; the key thumbprint — what the events and `AuthResp.device_id` call `device_id` — is the `thumbprint` column, which the admin list shows to match rows to events), mutation `RecordSignInEvent`, query `ListUserSignInEvents` and service method `ListMySignInEvents` (REST `GET /auth/sign-ins`; both `authenticate_turnkey`). The token, sign-in and MFA events carry `device_id`; their mutations take an event-only `thumbprint`. The public computer: a `public_computer` flag on `SignInReq`, `OAuthExchangeReq`, `SignUpReq` and `ConfirmSignUpReq` (VerifyMfa takes it from its challenge), echoed on their responses and `VerifyMfaResp`, recorded as `UserToken.public_computer` and `SignInEvent.public_computer` (and `MfaChallenge.public_computer` under `two_factor`); `IssueTokenReq` / `RecordSignInEventReq` / `CreateMfaChallengeReq` carry it. The CLI approver: `UserToken.approved_by_device_id` (the Device row of the browser session that approved a `cli_login` login; `IssueTokenReq.approved_by_device_id`). `RevokeDeviceTokens` and `MarkDeviceRevoked` are base tier — the `devices` calls and the `user_admin` Devices page both use them — and `RevokeDeviceTokens` records the `revoked_by` it is given.
- **Env knobs.** `public_session_ttl_seconds` (manifest default 0 = 8 hours, silently capped at the session lifetime): the lifetime of a public-computer session. Only an explicit value > 0 longer than `session_token_ttl_seconds` fails the boot (`auth/boot-public-session-ttl`); the default never does, because the bundle fills an unset knob with its manifest default. `two_factor_scope=new_devices` reads the trust kept here.
- **Requires / conflicts / combined files.** None. The `devices` feature adds only the management endpoints on top.

Rows marked *later* belong to the next steps of the device-identity work (streams, the client half of the public computer, the SDK half of the private key) and are not proved yet.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| `device/id-is-thumbprint` | A device MUST be identified only by the thumbprint the gateway passes (`x-w17-dpop-jkt`); a value that is not 43 base64url characters MUST be refused as no proof. No header, body field or envelope value a client sends may name a device. | Security | `TestMintThumbprint_RefusesAMissingOrMalformedProof`, `TestAuthenticate_NoProofIsRefusedBeforeTheLookup` (malformed proof). The `x-w17-` stripping is the gateway's. |
| `device/no-unbound-credential` | No mint path (SignIn, SignUp when it issues a session, ConfirmSignUp, VerifyMfa, OAuthExchange, CliToken) MUST issue a token without a proof; each MUST refuse with `DEVICE_PROOF_REQUIRED` before any password check, attempt count or single-use code is spent, and MUST bind the token it issues to the request's (user, thumbprint) Device. `IssueToken` MUST require `device_id`. CreateApiToken MUST bind every token to the (owner, thumbprint) Device of the key its request names (`device/api-token-own-key`) and MUST mint nothing without one (`DEVICE_KEY_REQUIRED`); IssueBotToken MUST do the same when its request names a key, and without one MUST mint only a machine account's bearer token (`device/bot-bearer`): `issueApiTokenTx` MUST refuse a mint without a device, so only the bearer path writes `IssueApiTokenReq` without `device_id`. | Security | `TestEveryMintPath_RefusesWithoutAProofBeforeTouchingStorage`, `TestSignIn_WithoutAProofIsRefusedBeforeTheLookup`, `TestSignIn_BindsTheSessionToTheProofsDevice`, `TestSignUpThenSignIn_TheSamePasswordWorks`, `TestSignUpConfirmation_SignUpParksAndConfirmCreates`, `TestVerifyMfa_TrustIsGuardedFromTheChallengeOn`, `TestOAuthExchange_DoesNotTrustTheDevice`, `TestCliLogin_EndToEnd`, `TestIssueSession_BindsAndTrustsOnlyWithACutoff`, `TestMintDevice_AStoreThatAnswersNoRowBindsNothing`, `TestIssueTokenReq_RequiresTheDevice`, `TestIssueApiTokenTx_MintsNothingWithoutADevice`, `TestCreateApiToken_BindsTheTokenToTheGivenKey`, `TestCreateApiToken_WithoutAKeyMintsNothing`, `TestIssueBotToken_OneKeyPerMachineAccount` |
| `device/proof-required` | A request that proves no key (no `x-w17-dpop-jkt`, a malformed one, or the token sent as `Bearer`) MUST be refused with `DEVICE_PROOF_REQUIRED` BEFORE the token is looked up, so the refusal says nothing about the token; the two exceptions are the MCP surface (`device/mcp-surface`) and, off the admin plane, a token sent under the configured scheme that carries the bot-bearer prefix (`device/bot-bearer`). With a proof, an unknown token, a token bound to another key or to none, and a revoked or expired token presented with another key MUST all get the SAME opaque refusal with no detail: whoever holds a stolen token without its key cannot tell it is live, or watch it be revoked. Signature, nonce and replay checks are the gateway's. | Security | `TestAuthenticate_NoProofIsRefusedBeforeTheLookup` (zero lookups), `TestAuthenticate_AThiefLearnsNothingAboutTheToken` (both resolvers), `TestAuthenticate_ABoundTokenWithItsKeyPassesAndNamesTheDevice` (the `DPoP` scheme case-insensitively, RFC 9110) |
| `device/mcp-surface` | MCP keeps its OAuth 2.1 flow without DPoP (its clients cannot sign yet). A proofless request MUST be accepted only when the gateway marks it `x-w17-surface: mcp` (gateway-owned, never client-set) AND the token is `mcp_only` (minted by the MCP OAuth flow); it then carries no device. On the MCP surface any other token without a proof MUST get the opaque refusal; an `mcp_only` token MUST be refused on every other surface (without a proof: `DEVICE_PROOF_REQUIRED`; with one: opaque, it is bound to no key). Cross-checked once against the SDK's `dpop.Thumbprint` on 1000 generated keys (not a committed test: the plugin does not import go-jose). |
| `device/private-key-never-leaves` (plugin side) | No API of the plugin MAY accept, return or store a private key: no request, response, table or event field is named like one, and the public key a request names is never a stored column (only its thumbprint is). A JWK with any private member (`d`, `p`, `q`, `dp`, `dq`, `qi`, `oth`, `k`, in any case) MUST be refused before anything else is read, with its own sentence, and MUST appear in no row, no answer and no log line — the history row of the refusal carries no key, and a refusal repeats no value longer than 16 bytes. No log call takes the key. | Security | `TestCreateApiToken_APrivateKeyIsRefusedNeitherStoredNorLogged` (also with the history write failing and logged), `TestParse_RefusesAnyPrivateMember`, `TestParse_ErrorsDoNotEchoLongValues`, `TestNoAPICarriesAPrivateKey` (field names across the five protos; `public_key` in no mutation or table), `TestNoLogCallTakesTheKey`. The browser key's non-extractability is the SDK's. |
| `device/bot-one-key` | A machine account MUST have exactly one live key (its bearer tokens, `device/bot-bearer`, have none and do not count): `IssueBotToken` locks the account row (`LockMachineAccount`, SELECT … FOR UPDATE) inside its transaction, reads the account's live keyed devices (`ListMachineKeys`) and MUST refuse another key (`BOT_KEY_MISMATCH`, FailedPrecondition, nothing minted); the same key MUST reuse the one device (its replicas share it). Once that device is revoked (the admin Devices page, which ends its tokens), its key is refused for good and a new key becomes the account's one key. The key is read after every authorization check, so a caller who may not mint for the account hears that whatever key it sent. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestIssueBotToken_OneKeyPerMachineAccount` (first key, same key, another key, revoke then a new key; the lock before the read), `TestIssueBotToken_RefusesABadKeyAfterTheChecks`. **Unit (mock) only** for the race: two parallel first tokens with different keys need a live database to show the lock serialising them. |
| `device/bot-bearer` | `IssueBotToken` without `public_key` MUST mint a BEARER token, and only for an account of kind BOT, after every check the keyed path makes (the target's organization, kind, visibility, the operator's ceiling, a disabled account): the token is `w17bot_` followed by a token of the usual shape, stored as its sha256 only, bound to no device, flagged `UserToken.bearer` (written through `IssueApiToken`'s `bearer`, field-gated `service_account`, set by service_account.go only, so `ApiTokenCreated` keeps one emit site), with its narrowing and one BOT_TOKEN history row (no device) in one transaction and the operator's device as approver. It MUST expire: the request's `expires_at`, else the deployment's default — 90 days when that is "never" — and never more than 366 days ahead (a request beyond is `TOKEN_EXPIRY_TOO_FAR` on `expires_at`; a longer default is cut to the ceiling). Authenticate MUST let a proofless request reach the lookup ONLY when the build has machine accounts (`serviceAccountStaged`), the token is sent under the configured scheme (`Bearer` by default; never `DPoP`), carries the prefix, and the request is neither on the MCP surface nor on the admin plane (`AuthenticateAdmin`: a credential without a key does not open the admin console); every other proofless request keeps `DEVICE_PROOF_REQUIRED` before the lookup. Such a request MUST pass only for a live row flagged `bearer`, of an API token, owned by a BOT account, bound to no device and not `mcp_only`; anything else — the prefix on a token without the flag included — MUST be the opaque refusal, recording nothing. The prefix authorizes nothing. A bearer token MUST NOT pass under `DPoP` (it is bound to no key) or on the MCP surface. An ended bearer token MUST answer `SESSION_EXPIRED` without a proof (its holder is whoever presents it); an ended prefixed token that is not one MUST stay opaque. AuthResp names no device for it. | Security | `TestIssueBotToken_WithoutAKeyMintsAnExpiringBearerToken`, `TestIssueBotToken_ABearerTokenExpiresWithinAYear`, `TestIssueBotToken_ABearerTokenIsForAMachineAccountOnly`, `TestAuthenticate_ABotBearerTokenPassesWithoutAProof`, `TestAuthenticate_OnlyAFlaggedBotTokenPassesWithoutAProof` (both resolvers), `TestAuthenticate_ABearerTokenTakesNoOtherPath` (unprefixed: zero lookups), `TestAuthenticate_ABearerTokenIsRefusedOnTheAdminPlaneAndWithoutMachineAccounts` (zero lookups), `TestAuthenticate_ADisabledBotsBearerTokenIsRefused`, the keyless cases of `TestIssueBotToken_AnAPITokenCallerIsRefused`, `TestIssueBotToken_RefusesADisabledBot`, `TestIssueBotToken_RefusesABotFromAnotherOrganization`, `TestIssueBotToken_RefusesABotAboveTheOperator`, `TestAuthenticate_AnEndedBearerTokenSaysSo`, `TestIsBotBearerToken`, `TestBotBearerStatements`. **Unit (mock) only:** the flag's SQL and the `?feature(service_account)` projection need a live database. |

**Sign-in history: the outcome of each refusal.** What the caller is told is unchanged; most of these reach the wire as the same opaque refusal.

| outcome | when |
|---|---|
| `OK` | a session or an API token was issued |
| `MFA_REQUIRED` | SignIn: the password was right, a second factor was asked for |
| `UNKNOWN_ACCOUNT` | SignIn: no account has the address (`user_id` NULL) |
| `WRONG_PASSWORD` | SignIn: wrong password (`errAuthnPasswordMismatch`) |
| `ACCOUNT_DISABLED` | a disabled or erased account (`errAuthnUserDisabled`, `ACCOUNT_DISABLED`), at SignIn, VerifyMfa, OAuthExchange, CliToken |
| `MACHINE_ACCOUNT` | SignIn by a `service_account` bot (`errAuthnBotNoPassword`) |
| `DEVICE_PROOF_REQUIRED` | any path, no proof |
| `DEVICE_REVOKED` | any path, a revoked (account, device) — the row names the revoked device; for an API token, the key its request names (`DEVICE_KEY_REVOKED`) |
| `KEY_REFUSED` | CreateApiToken, IssueBotToken: no key (`DEVICE_KEY_REQUIRED`), a key that is not a public P-256 or Ed25519 JWK, a private one or a signed-in device's (`DEVICE_KEY_INVALID`), another key than the machine account's (`BOT_KEY_MISMATCH`) |
| `THROTTLED` | SignIn: the guess budget was spent — the account's, from a key that never signed in to it, or the known device's own (`sign_in_turnkey/failure-budget`) |
| `WRONG_CODE` | a wrong, unknown, expired or spent code: VerifyMfa (`errMfaChallengeInvalid`), ConfirmSignUp (`errSignUpConfirmInvalid`), OAuthExchange (`errOAuthCodeInvalid`, `errOAuthVerifierInvalid`), CliToken (`errCliCodeInvalid`, `errCliVerifierInvalid`, `errCliRedirectMismatch`) |
| `TOO_MANY_ATTEMPTS` | VerifyMfa past five attempts (`errMfaAttemptsSpent`), ConfirmSignUp past five |
| `OTHER_DEVICE` | VerifyMfa signed by another key than the challenged SignIn (`errMfaChallengeOtherDevice`) |
| `RATE_LIMITED` | SignIn: the account's challenge budget is spent (`errMfaChallengeBudget`) |
| `EMAIL_TAKEN` | SignUp, ConfirmSignUp, OAuth account creation: `EMAIL_TAKEN` |
| `SIGN_UP_REFUSED` | a sign-up gate: `EMAIL_INVALID` (reserved domain), `SIGNUP_CLOSED`, `SIGNUP_NOT_INVITED`, `INVITE_OTHER_ADDRESS`, `TENANT_UNKNOWN` |
| `UNVERIFIED_EMAIL` | OAuthExchange: `OAUTH_ACCOUNT_UNVERIFIED`, `OAUTH_EMAIL_UNVERIFIED` |
| `CLIENT_REFUSED` | CliToken: a registered client failed its authentication (`errCliClientAuthFailed`) or is revoked (`CLIENT_REVOKED`) |
| `ERROR` | an outage (`AUTH_UNAVAILABLE`, Unavailable, Internal, …; also ConfirmSignUp's attempt counter and VerifyMfa's challenge read or attempt claim failing, which the caller is told as a wrong code, and an opaque refusal whose cause is an outage code the wire does not call one, such as Unknown) or an unexpected error |
| `REFUSED` | any other refusal, for example a CLI exchange after the approving session ended (`errCliSessionNotLive`), or after the approving device was revoked (`errCliApproverRevoked`), a sign-in with a program's key (`errDeviceIsAProgramKey`), or an API-token request by a caller that may not mint it |

The outcome is what the path set explicitly (an unknown address, a parked second factor, ConfirmSignUp's spent budget), else read off the error the caller gets: the sentinels each feature registers in `signInOutcomeRules`, then the wire `ErrorDetail` code, then the status code.

**Gaps / suspected defects**

1. **Residual windows of `device/credential-change-ends-trust`.** VerifyMfa's cutoff is the challenge's `created_at`, inserted after SignIn's password check, so a password change or disable committing between that check and the challenge insert is not caught; a renewing SignIn has the same window between its password check and its read of the stamp. Both last milliseconds. On Postgres `NOW()` is the transaction's start, so two overlapping transactions can order their stamps against their commit order.
2. **Fixed: VerifyMfa bound the device of its own request**, which need not be the one SignIn challenged. The challenge now records SignIn's key (`MfaChallenge.thumbprint`) and VerifyMfa signed by another key is refused opaquely, spending an attempt (bounded probing) without consuming the challenge (`device/second-factor-same-key`).
3. **Rows are never bounded.** Every new key that passes the password makes a Device row; nothing caps or purges them.
4. **The sign-in history grows with every attempt, failures included**, and nothing prunes it (nothing in the auth tables is deleted). An unauthenticated flood — every proofless or wrong-password request writes a row — is bounded only by the gateway's per-address limit (the mint endpoints are `failed_only`: every refusal is charged). The admin list is paged; the person's own list is the newest 100.
5. **A sign-up refused under `sign_up_confirmation` is not recorded.** SignUp then parks the registration and signs nobody in, so neither its gates' refusals nor the parking are sign-in attempts; ConfirmSignUp records the attempt that would sign in.

---

### rbac

- **Purpose.** Role-based permissions: roles, their permission grants, and user-to-role links. It also stages the declarative sign-up role assignment (`first_user` / `default`).
- **Adds.** Tables `Role` (unique `(name, ended_key)`; `preset_key` unique and nullable; flags `all_permissions`, `assign_first_user`, `assign_default`; `deleted_at`), `RolePermission` (unique `(role_id, permission_id)`; `permission_id` `CHECK`-constrained to the deployed `AclPermission` ids; `removed_at`, `removed_by`), `RolePermissionChange` (append-only log of permission adds and removals), `UserRole` (unique `(user_id, role_id, ended_key)`, or two partial uniques with `ended_key` under `org_membership`; `revoked_at`, `revoked_by`), `BootstrapClaim` (one row on the constant key `slot`). Nothing in them is deleted (`auth/nothing-is-deleted`). Queries `GetUserPermissions`, `GetUserByTokenWithPermissions`, `GetUserRoleGrants`, `ListRoleGrants`, `CountUsers`, `GetFirstUserRoles`, `GetDefaultRoles`, `ListRoles`, `GetRole`. Mutations `CreateRole`, `UpdateRole`, `DeleteRole`, `AssignRoleToUser`, `RevokeRoleFromUser`, `AddPermissionToRole`, `RemovePermissionFromRole`, each emitting its lifecycle event, and `ClaimBootstrapAdmin` (no event). Admin preset: the Roles page and the `auth_user_count` widget. No REST endpoints.
- **Requires / conflicts / combined files.** None. Default on. Go file `handlers/signup_roles.go`.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| `rbac/permission-id-in-catalogue` | A `RolePermission.permission_id` outside the deployed permission catalogue MUST be refused by the database. | Security | none. A live test would insert an id that is not in the lock and expect a check violation mapped to InvalidArgument. |
| `rbac/no-duplicate-grants` | A user MUST NOT hold the same live realm-wide role twice, and a role MUST NOT carry the same permission twice. A duplicate live grant MUST be refused by the unique indexes (live rows share `ended_key` = ''), including realm-wide rows (`org_id IS NULL`) under `org_membership`. Adding a permission a role already carries is refused by a guard ahead of the UPSERT (`AlreadyExists` / `PERMISSION_ALREADY_GRANTED`), as the INSERT's index refused it before. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestUniqueIndexesLetAnEndedKeyBeReused` (the index text); the refusal itself is schema |
| `rbac/wildcard-from-deployed-lock` | A role with `all_permissions` MUST expand to the full deployed lock at authentication time. It MUST NOT expand to `RolePermission` rows. It MUST expand only if the role survived every narrowing axis. | Security | `TestResolvePrincipalRealmAware_Wildcard_GrantsAll`, `TestNarrowGrants_WildcardRoleMustPassTheOrgAxis` (both through other features' resolvers; the session-only resolver has no wildcard test) |
| `rbac/role-update-cannot-widen` | `UpdateRole` MUST change only `name` and `description`. The privilege flags MUST stay declarative, so the admin edit form cannot widen a grant. | Security | none (DQL only) |
| `rbac/deleted-role-grants-nothing` | After `DeleteRole`, no query MUST report any permission that role carried. `DeleteRole` revokes every live grant of the role in the same transaction, and every role and permission reader (`GetUserPermissions` included, which now joins `Role`) skips a deleted role, a revoked grant and a removed permission. | Security | `TestEveryFormerDeleteIsAGuardedStateChange` (DeleteRole's two legs), `TestEveryReaderSkipsEndedRows` (every reader; the LEFT JOIN filters in their ON; GetUserPermissions joins the role). **Unit (mock) only:** a live test would delete a role and show `Authenticate` and `GetUserPermissions` no longer report its permissions. |
| `rbac/removal-is-a-state` | Retiring a role, revoking a grant and removing a permission MUST NOT delete a row. `DeleteRole` stamps `Role.deleted_at` and releases the name (`ended_key` = id), a preset role (`preset_key` set) included, and MUST keep `preset_key` on the deleted row: the platform's role seed (the Role row's `upsert_on: [preset_key]` and its declared `fields`, applied by the console's fixture apply) matches the row by that key and rewrites only the columns it declares — never `deleted_at` or `ended_key` — so a deleted preset STAYS deleted across re-seeds and no second row is written that could collide on the seed's deterministic id; the seed never retires an undeclared role, so this delete is the only way to end one; `RevokeRoleFromUser` stamps `UserRole.revoked_at` / `revoked_by` and releases the grant's key; `RemovePermissionFromRole` stamps `RolePermission.removed_at` / `removed_by`. Each is guarded on its state column being NULL: a second one matches nothing (NotFound), as the delete of a missing row did. A released key MAY be taken again by a new row (a new role with the name, the same grant again); a removed permission is REVIVED by `AddPermissionToRole` (the one table that keeps one row per pair — its unique index is the role seed's conflict target), and both permission calls append to `RolePermissionChange` in the same transaction, so the revive loses no history. `AddPermissionToRole` and `AssignRoleToUser` MUST refuse a deleted or unknown role (`NotFound` / `ROLE_NOT_FOUND`), and `AddPermissionToRole` a permission the role already carries (`AlreadyExists` / `PERMISSION_ALREADY_GRANTED`), before writing: only a real change is logged, and `RolePermissionAdded` fires only for one. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestEveryFormerDeleteIsAGuardedStateChange`, `TestRoleWritesRefuseADeletedRoleAndANoOp`, `TestDeleteRoleKeepsAPresetsKey`, `TestUniqueIndexesLetAnEndedKeyBeReused`, `TestNoStatementDeletesAnything`. **Unit (mock) only:** these mutations have no handler; a live test would revoke and re-grant a role and remove and re-add a permission and count the rows. |
| `rbac/first-user-bootstrap-once` | The `first_user` role set (typically a wildcard superadmin) MUST be granted to at most one account per deployment, even when the first registrations arrive concurrently. | Security | `TestAssignSignupRoles_FirstUserGetsBootstrapRoles`, `TestAssignSignupRoles_LaterUserGetsDefaultRoles`, `TestAssignSignupRoles_ConcurrentFirstSignUpsGetOneBootstrapAdmin`, `TestAssignSignupRoles_ATakenClaimIsNotReopened`, `TestBootstrapSlot_FitsItsColumn` (the claim key passes the column's validation). **Unit (mock) only**, with a scripted `CountUsers` and a mocked claim. A live test would fire N concurrent SignUps against an empty table and assert exactly one holder of the `first_user` roles. |
| `rbac/signup-roles-atomic` | The sign-up role links MUST commit in the same transaction as the `User` row and its first token. A failed assignment MUST leave no account. | Consistency & concurrency | none. No test drives SignUp or ConfirmSignUp through the transaction fake with a failing `AssignRoleToUser`. |
| `rbac/role-resolution-cost` | Authenticating a request MUST cost a bounded number of indexed queries: one token lookup and one role-grant query (an API token adds its subset query), plus the staged decorators' and narrowers' own fixed reads (`auth/authenticate-read-only`). It MUST NOT grow with the number of roles or permissions. | Performance | `TestAuthenticate_QueryCountIsFixedWhateverTheRoleCount` (1 and 40 roles cost the same; session 2 queries, API token 3, +1 per decorator). That the queries are index-served is schema (`auth/lookup-columns-indexed`). |

**Gaps / suspected defects**

1. **The first stranger to register becomes the bootstrap admin.** The race is fixed in rc.25: the `first_user` set goes only to the sign-up that writes the `BootstrapClaim` row inside its transaction (`assignSignupRoleIDs`). What remains is by design and documented in the README: `sign_up_turnkey` is on by default and public, so the first person to reach `/auth/register` on an empty deployment becomes superadmin unless the operator seeds an account first. A seeded account writes no claim, so on such a deployment deleting every account reopens the bootstrap. Without `DistTx` the claim commits on its own, ahead of the role links; a failure after it leaves the claim taken for good (see `sign_up_turnkey` gap 2). The blocked sign-up gets the default roles only under READ COMMITTED (PostgreSQL's default) or MySQL; a PostgreSQL server defaulting to REPEATABLE READ or SERIALIZABLE answers it with a serialization failure instead.
2. **Fixed: `GetUserPermissions` ignored deleted roles.** It joins `Role` now and skips a deleted role, a revoked grant and a removed permission; `DeleteRole` also revokes the role's grants (`rbac/deleted-role-grants-nothing`).
3. **Retired: orphan link rows.** A retired role's grants are revoked with it, and its `RolePermission` rows are kept on purpose as the record of what it granted; no reader counts either.
5. **Changed: a preset role deleted at runtime stays deleted.** It used to come back on the next seed (as a new row, without its holders). Now the deleted row keeps its `preset_key`, and the seed, which matches on that key and never writes `deleted_at`, rewrites its declared columns and leaves it deleted — like a seeded permission removed at runtime (gap 6). To bring a preset back, declare it under a new key. The seed's own updates of the deleted row (label, flags) are harmless: no reader counts a deleted role; a seed that knows `deleted_at` leaves a deleted role's `RolePermission` rows alone.
6. **The platform's role seed: ends and revives, once it knows this release.** A role seed that sees `RolePermission.removed_at` ends the rows a declaration no longer selects (`removed_at`, never a `DELETE`), revives a declared pair `RemovePermissionFromRole` removed — so a seeded permission removed at runtime comes back on the next seed; only a change to the declaration removes it for good — and logs each change in `RolePermissionChange` (`changed_by` NULL) in the same statement as the change. An older platform still prunes with a `DELETE` and cannot revive. Both behaviours belong to the platform, not to a statement of this plugin.
4. **Fixed: a stale contract comment.** `GetUserByTokenWithPermissions` said it returned the permissions in one round trip; it returns the token, user and device, and says that the permissions are `GetUserRoleGrants`.
