### authenticate_turnkey

- **Purpose.** The gateway's bearer resolver (`Authenticate`), plus `WhoAmI` and `Logout`, and the same resolver from a user id (`ResolveUser`) for the domain's own code acting for a person.
- **Adds.** Mutation `RevokeSessionToken` (sole emitter of `TokenRevoked`). Service methods `Authenticate`, `AuthenticateAdmin`, `ResolveUser` (no preset surfaces it), `WhoAmI`, `Logout`. REST endpoints `GET /auth/whoami` and `POST /auth/logout` (both bearer). MCP tool `WhoAmI`. Admin `user_lookup` (`AuthenticateAdmin`).
- **Requires.** `rbac`. No conflicts or combined files.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| `authenticate_turnkey/opaque-refusal` | Every refusal (missing header, wrong scheme, empty token, unknown, expired or revoked token, failed decorator or narrower) MUST produce one identical `Unauthenticated` wire status whose message never carries the cause. | Security | `TestUnauthenticated_WireStatusIsAlwaysOpaque`, `TestUnauthenticated_TwoCallsWithDifferentCauses_WireResponseIdentical`, `TestAuthnError_EveryRefusalIsStillTheSameOpaqueAnswer`, `TestAuthnError_NotFoundNeverBecomesOperational` (constructor level; `Authenticate`'s header parsing has no direct test) |
| `authenticate_turnkey/outage-is-not-refusal` | A storage outage (Unavailable, DeadlineExceeded, ResourceExhausted, Aborted, Internal) MUST surface as `Unavailable` with `AUTH_UNAVAILABLE`. It MUST NOT surface as bad credentials. | Failure & configuration | `TestAuthnError_AnOutageIsNotReportedAsBadCredentials`, `TestUnauthenticated_OutageCarriesItsOwnCode` |
| `authenticate_turnkey/scopes-server-derived` | `scopes["user_id"]` and every decorator-added scope MUST come from the resolved token, never from client input. A decorator or narrower error MUST fail the request closed. | Security | `TestResolveTenantScope_LookupErrorPropagates` (decorator half only) |
| `authenticate_turnkey/session-id-only-for-sessions` | `AuthResp.session_token_id` MUST be set only when the bearer is a session token. | Security | `TestResolvePrincipalRealmAware_ReturnsTokenID`, `TestResolvePrincipalRealmAware_ApiTokenHasNoSessionID` |
| `authenticate_turnkey/logout-own-session-only` | `Logout` MUST revoke exactly the caller's current session (reason LOGOUT, the caller as `revoked_by`), guarded on both `id` and `user_id`, and keep the row. It MUST be idempotent for an already-revoked session. | Security | `TestLogout_RevokesCurrentSession`, `TestLogout_AlreadyRevoked_Idempotent`, `TestLogout_MutationError_Propagates`, `TestLogout_RevokesTheSessionAndKeepsTheRow`. **Unit (mock) only** for the ownership guard: a live test would call `RevokeSessionToken` with another user's token id and assert the row stays live. |
| `authenticate_turnkey/logout-refuses-unknown-session` | `Logout` without a resolvable session id (API-token bearer, or an envelope missing field 4) MUST be refused with `FailedPrecondition`. It MUST NOT return a false success. | Failure & configuration | `TestLogout_NoSessionToken_Refused`, `TestLogout_NoCaller_Unauthenticated` |
| `authenticate_turnkey/whoami-fails-closed` | `WhoAmI` and every self-service handler MUST refuse a call that carries no gateway-stamped principal. | Security | `TestLogout_NoCaller_Unauthenticated` (shared `callerUserID` path); no `WhoAmI`-specific test |
| `authenticate_turnkey/resolve-user-is-authenticate` | `ResolveUser` MUST answer a person through the same resolution as `Authenticate` after the credential (`principalResp`: decorators, narrowers, the wildcard expanded from the deployed lock, the enrichers), reading the person's session-realm roles and no other realm's. It MUST refuse a request naming nobody (`InvalidArgument`), an unknown account (`NotFound`) and a disabled one, answer no session or device, and set `acted_for`, which a re-read of the grants under an organization's lock MUST count as a session (it names no session token); `Authenticate` MUST never set it. The organization MUST come from the request's `headers` alone: an incoming `w17-org` is dropped before the decorators run. No preset MUST surface it, and it MUST carry `(w17.contrib.plugin_internal_rpc)`, which makes codegen refuse every surface naming it: it trusts its caller with any user. | Security | `TestResolveUser_AnswersWhatTheirSessionHolds`, `TestResolveUser_TheOrganizationComesFromTheRequest`, `TestResolveUser_Refuses`, `TestResolveUser_TheCeilingJudgesThePersonActedFor` (stamped as the envelope, the role ceiling judges the person; a bare user id holds nothing — control), `TestResolveUser_UnderTheOrganizationsLockThePersonIsTheirSession`, `TestResolveUser_AnswersWhatAuthenticateAnswersForTheirSession` (parity, every person × organization, the real organization decorator and narrower), `TestResolveUser_HoldsTheirSessionRolesInTheOrganizationAlone`, `TestResolveUser_TheCeilingJudgesThePersonInAndOutOfTheLock`, `TestResolveUser_IgnoresThePrincipalTheRequestCarries`, `TestResolveUser_ADisabledAccountIsRefusedEverywhere`, `TestResolveUser_InvitationsMadeActingForAPersonOutliveTheLockGrowing`, `TestResolveUser_IsMarkedInternal`; the surfaces' refusal is codegen's (its gateway parser refuses a surface naming an internal RPC); live: `product-roles-proof` step 22 |

**Gaps / suspected defects**

1. **Fixed: the scheme prefix was case-sensitive.** `authorization: bearer <t>` was refused; the configured scheme is now matched case-insensitively (RFC 9110 §11.1), as `DPoP` already was.
2. **No direct test of `Authenticate`.** Header lookup, scheme stripping, decorator and narrower ordering, and the final `AuthResp` are untested end to end at unit level. Tests reach only the resolvers and the error constructor.
3. **`kind` is not re-checked per request.** `Authenticate` refuses a disabled account on every request (under `user_admin`, both resolvers run the disabled gate on the token's owner), but it does not read `kind`: a machine account authenticates with its API tokens by design.

---

### sign_in_turnkey

- **Purpose.** Email and password sign-in that issues a session bearer.
- **Adds.** Service method `SignIn`. REST endpoint `POST /auth/login` (**public, failed_only**). Admin `login_method`. The guess budget (base tier, so every activation has it): columns `User.sign_in_failures` / `sign_in_window_at` and `Device.sign_in_failures` / `sign_in_window_at`, mutations `CountSignInAttempt`, `CountDeviceSignInAttempt`, `ResetSignInAttempts`, history outcome `THROTTLED`.
- **Env knobs.** `sign_in_failure_budget` (default 20), `sign_in_device_failure_budget` (default 10), `sign_in_failure_window_seconds` (default 900).
- **Requires / conflicts / combined files.** None. Default on. Go file `handlers/signin_budget.go`.
- **Trade-off of the budget.** A spent budget does not lock the account: a lockout would let anyone who knows an address keep its owner out. Instead, only a device that has signed in to the account before (a live, signed-in `Device` row of the proof's key, which only a client that knew the password could have made) may still try, within a budget of its own. During an attack the owner keeps signing in on every device they have used; on a NEW device they wait out the window. Guessing spread over many addresses is bounded per account all the same. Where that wait is not acceptable, put the gateway's captcha on `SignIn` too.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| `sign_in_turnkey/indistinguishable-refusals` | Unknown address, wrong password, a spent guess budget, machine account and disabled account MUST produce byte-identical wire refusals (code, message and user-facing detail). | Security | `TestSignIn_TheFourRefusalsAreIndistinguishable`, `TestSignIn_EveryRefusalReachesTheWireWithAMessage`, `TestUnauthenticatedSignIn_AllGatesRenderIdentically` |
| `sign_in_turnkey/constant-time-miss` | An unknown address MUST cost one password-hash verification with the configured parameters, so that timing does not reveal whether an account exists. | Security | `TestSignIn_UserNotFound_RunsDummyVerify` (asserts the dummy verify runs, not the elapsed time) |
| `sign_in_turnkey/gates-after-password` | The machine-account and disabled-account gates MUST run only after a successful password verification. | Security | none for the ordering. `TestSignIn_RejectsDisabledAccount` and `TestSignIn_RefusesAMachineAccountThatHasAPassword` show the gates refuse with the correct password; no test shows they are not consulted before it. |
| `sign_in_turnkey/no-token-before-all-gates` | No token MUST be issued until every gate (password, machine, disabled, device, second factor) has passed. | Security | `TestSignIn_RejectsDisabledAccount`, `TestSignUpThenSignIn_AWrongPasswordIsStillRefused` |
| `sign_in_turnkey/canonical-lookup` | The address MUST be normalised before lookup. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestSignIn_NormalizesEmailForLookup` |
| `sign_in_turnkey/failed-attempts-limited` | Failed sign-ins MUST be charged to the gateway's per-address limiter (`public_limit: failed_only`). Successful ones MUST NOT be charged. | Security | none (manifest only; the enforcement is in the gateway) |
| `sign_in_turnkey/failure-budget` | Every password attempt at an existing account MUST be counted against it before its password is checked, in one statement (`CountSignInAttempt`), so concurrent guesses get distinct counts. Past `sign_in_failure_budget` attempts in the window, an attempt MUST be refused — the right password included — unless its key has a live signed-in device of the account, whose own count (`CountDeviceSignInAttempt`) MUST stay within `sign_in_device_failure_budget`. An attempt that could not be counted MUST be refused. A refusal MUST run the throwaway verify and answer as a wrong password; the history records it `THROTTLED`. A correct password MUST reset both counts. | Security | `TestSignIn_BudgetSpent_AnUnknownDeviceIsRefused`, `TestSignIn_BudgetSpent_AKnownDeviceStillSignsIn`, `TestSignIn_BudgetSpent_AKnownDeviceHasItsOwnBudget`, `TestSignIn_BudgetWindowStartsOver`, `TestSignIn_UncountedAttemptIsRefused`, `TestSignInOutcome_Throttled`. **Unit (mock) only:** a live test would run concurrent wrong guesses past the budget and assert exactly `sign_in_failure_budget` reach the password check. |
| `sign_in_turnkey/outage-not-wrong-password` | A lookup outage MUST answer `Unavailable`. It MUST NOT answer "Wrong email or password." | Failure & configuration | `TestAuthnError_AnOutageIsNotReportedAsBadCredentials` (constructor level) |

**Gaps / suspected defects**

1. **No event for a failed attempt.** Failed attempts are recorded (`SignInEvent`) and bounded per account (`failure-budget`), but nothing is published for them: a subscriber that wants to alert on an account under attack reads the history.
2. **Fixed: weak hashes are upgraded at sign-in.** See base gap 2.

---

### sign_up_turnkey

- **Purpose.** Self-service registration that creates the account and its first session in one call.
- **Adds.** Service method `SignUp`. REST endpoint `POST /auth/register` (**public**, charged on every request). Gates on `sign_up_allowlist` (and `invite_only` when `org_invite` is staged).
- **Env knobs.** `sign_up_allowlist` (addresses and `@domain` entries; a malformed entry fails boot).
- **Requires / conflicts / combined files.** None. Default on. Go file `handlers/signup.go`.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| `sign_up_turnkey/gates-before-work` | The allowlist and invite gates MUST run before any password hashing and before any write. An address outside the allowlist MUST receive one fixed answer, whatever else is true of it. | Security | `TestSignUpAllowlist_GatesThePlainSignUp`, `TestSignUpAllowlistGate` |
| `sign_up_turnkey/allowlist-fails-boot` | A malformed `sign_up_allowlist` entry MUST fail boot. At request time an unparsable list MUST refuse everyone. | Failure & configuration | `TestParseSignUpAllowlist` |
| `sign_up_turnkey/atomic-registration` | The user row, its role links, an open-invitation claim and the first token MUST commit together or not at all. | Consistency & concurrency | none (no transaction-fake test for SignUp) |
| `sign_up_turnkey/taken-address-answer` | A taken address MUST be refused with `AlreadyExists` / `EMAIL_TAKEN`, derived from the unique index. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestSignUp_ATakenAddressSaysSignInInstead`. **Unit (mock) only:** see `base/email-unique`. |
| `sign_up_turnkey/session-ttl` | The token SignUp mints MUST carry the session TTL. | Security | none (SignUp's own mint is untested; `TestIssueSession_StampsExpiry` covers the SignIn path) |
| `sign_up_turnkey/every-request-limited` | Every SignUp request MUST be charged to the gateway's per-address limiter. | Performance | none (manifest only) |

**Gaps / suspected defects**

1. **Account enumeration by design.** Without `sign_up_confirmation`, `SignUp` answers `EMAIL_TAKEN` for a registered address, so the endpoint reveals which addresses have accounts, at the gateway limiter's rate. This is accepted, but should be stated in the consumer documentation next to `sign_in_turnkey/indistinguishable-refusals`.
2. **The transaction silently degrades.** When `DistTx` is nil, SignUp runs its writes as separate statements (`signup.go:111`). That is intended for tests, but a mis-wired bundle would degrade to non-atomic registration without any error. A wired handler should refuse to boot without a coordinator. Under `rbac` it also commits the `BootstrapClaim` ahead of the role links, so a failure between them leaves the claim taken by an account without the `first_user` roles, and the claim is never reopened.
3. **Fixed: an over-long password was a server error.** See base gap 4. An empty one is refused by the required PASSWORD field before it reaches the handler.
4. **Bootstrap admin.** See `rbac` gap 1.

---

### sign_up_confirmation

- **Purpose.** Prove the address before the account exists. SignUp parks a pending registration and mails a code. `ConfirmSignUp` creates the account.
- **Adds.** Table `PendingSignUp` (`email` indexed; `password_hash` and `code_hash` both hashed with the password hasher; `attempts`; `expires_at`; `claimed_at`; `superseded_at`; `tenant_id` under `tenant_scope`). Mutations `CreatePendingSignUp` (emits `SignUpConfirmationRequested` with the plaintext code), `RecordPendingSignUpAttempt`, `ClaimPendingSignUp`, `SupersedePendingSignUpsForEmail`. No pending row is deleted: claimed, superseded and expired rows stay. Service method `ConfirmSignUp`. REST endpoint `POST /auth/register/confirm` (**public, failed_only**).
- **Env knobs.** `sign_up_confirmation_ttl_seconds` (default 900).
- **Requires.** `sign_up_turnkey`. Combined file `handlers/signup_confirm_tenant.go` with `tenant_scope`. Default off.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| `sign_up_confirmation/no-account-before-proof` | SignUp MUST create no `User` row and issue no token. It MUST return only `confirmation_required` and `pending_id`. | Security | `TestSignUpConfirmation_SignUpParksAndConfirmCreates` |
| `sign_up_confirmation/secrets-hashed` | The pending row MUST store only hashes of the password and the code. The plaintext code MUST travel only in `SignUpConfirmationRequested`. | Security | `TestSignUpConfirmation_SignUpParksAndConfirmCreates` |
| `sign_up_confirmation/code-entropy` | The code MUST be 8 uniformly random Crockford base32 characters from a CSPRNG (about 2^40 possibilities). | Security | `TestSignUpConfirmation_SignUpParksAndConfirmCreates` (alphabet and length only) |
| `sign_up_confirmation/signup-does-not-enumerate` | SignUp MUST answer a taken address exactly as a new one, with no lookup and the same work. Only ConfirmSignUp, given the mailed code, MAY reveal that the account exists. | Security | `TestSignUpConfirmation_ATakenAddressIsNotRevealedBySignUp`, `TestConfirmSignUp_ATakenAddressSaysSignInInstead` |
| `sign_up_confirmation/abandoned-blocks-nobody` | Any number of pending rows MAY exist for one address. None of them MUST block the owner's registration, and confirming one MUST end every other open pending row for that address (`superseded_at`) in the same transaction; the rows stay, and none of them can be confirmed afterwards. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestSignUpConfirmation_AnAbandonedRegistrationBlocksNobody` (the stranger's row is kept superseded, the owner's claimed), `TestSignUpConfirmation_NoNumberOfStrangersRefusesTheOwner` (all 51 rows kept, none live), `TestEveryFormerDeleteIsAGuardedStateChange` |
| `sign_up_confirmation/registration-ends-as-a-state` | Nothing MUST delete a pending row. The claim is `UPDATE … SET claimed_at = NOW() … RETURNING`, the others are superseded, an expired row stays (the per-address purge is gone), and every statement that reads a row as live (`RecordPendingSignUpAttempt`, `ClaimPendingSignUp`) requires `claimed_at IS NULL AND superseded_at IS NULL AND expires_at > NOW()`. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestEveryFormerDeleteIsAGuardedStateChange`, `TestEveryWriteThatUsesARowSkipsAnEndedOne`, `TestSignUpConfirmation_ACodeIsSingleUse` (the claimed row stays), `TestSignUpConfirmation_AnExpiredRegistrationStaysAndIsRefused` 
| `sign_up_confirmation/attempt-budget-atomic` | Each pending row MUST accept at most 5 code checks. The attempt MUST be counted by the same statement that returns the hash, before the check, so concurrent guesses cannot share one budget. | Security | `TestSignUpConfirmation_WrongCodesAreRefusedAndExhaustTheRow`. **Unit (mock) only:** a live test would send more than 5 concurrent wrong guesses and assert that `attempts` ends above 5 while at most 5 were evaluated. |
| `sign_up_confirmation/single-use` | Two confirmations of one pending row MUST yield at most one account. `UPDATE … SET claimed_at = NOW() WHERE … claimed_at IS NULL … RETURNING` is the guard. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestSignUpConfirmation_ACodeIsSingleUse`. **Unit (mock) only:** a live test would race two correct confirmations and assert one user and one opaque refusal. |
| `sign_up_confirmation/expiry-in-statement` | An expired pending row MUST behave exactly like a missing one in every read and claim, through `expires_at > NOW()` in the WHERE clause. | Security | `TestSignUpConfirmation_AnExpiredRegistrationIsRefused`. **Unit (mock) only:** the predicate is SQL. |
| `sign_up_confirmation/uniform-refusal` | A wrong code, a spent, expired or exhausted row and an unknown id MUST all produce one identical refusal. | Security | `TestSignUpConfirmation_WrongCodesAreRefusedAndExhaustTheRow`, `TestSignUpConfirmation_ACodeIsSingleUse`, `TestSignUpConfirmation_AnExpiredRegistrationIsRefused` |
| `sign_up_confirmation/allowlist-rechecked` | The allowlist MUST be re-checked at confirmation, against the stored address. | Security | `TestSignUpAllowlist_GatesBothSteps` |
| `sign_up_confirmation/confirm-is-proof` | A confirmed account MUST be recorded as `SELF_CONFIRMED` (when `email_verification` is staged) in the same transaction that creates it. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestSignUpConfirmation_SignUpParksAndConfirmCreates` |
| `sign_up_confirmation/tenant-from-request-time` | Under `tenant_scope`, the account MUST be created in the tenant resolved at SignUp. It MUST NOT be created in whichever tenant the confirmation request names. | Security | `TestConfirmSignUp_CreatesTheAccountInTheTenantSignUpResolved` (confirmation arriving on another tenant's host; no tenant lookup at confirmation) |

**Gaps / suspected defects**

1. **The plaintext code rides the event bus.** `SignUpConfirmationRequested.code` (and likewise the reset and verification tokens) is published on the plugin's `events` channel. Every auth event is internal (`public: false`), so none reaches the gateway's client stream (`/w17-events`); what remains is the transport: a durable channel keeps the code in the broker for its retention, and a hand-written consumer that republishes the event exposes it. "Never persisted" holds only for the plugin's own tables. See also `password_reset` gap 1.
2. **Fixed: the invite gate is run again at confirmation.** ConfirmSignUp asks `signupInviteGate` for the parked address inside its transaction, so an invitation revoked or expired between SignUp and ConfirmSignUp refuses the confirmation and no account is created. An open link was bound to the address at SignUp, so the address finds it; a registration an organization's open policy took (org_signup) came through no invitation and is not asked. The open-invitation claim made at SignUp persists (`TestSignUpConfirmation_TheInviteGateRunsAgainAtConfirmation`).
3. **Fixed: an outage read as a wrong code.** `ConfirmSignUp` (`RecordPendingSignUpAttempt`) and `VerifyMfa` (`GetMfaChallenge`, `RecordMfaAttempt`) still fail closed, but answer an outage as `Unavailable`, the way `Authenticate` and the consume paths do, so the person retries instead of registering again or retyping a right code. An unknown or spent registration or challenge is still the one opaque refusal.
4. **Pending rows are kept for good.** Nothing deletes one (`auth/nothing-is-deleted`): claimed, superseded and expired rows stay, and the table grows with every registration. Each keeps its password and code hashes (a claimed row's password hash is the account's).
5. **Every SignUp costs two password-hash derivations.** One is for the password and one for an 8-character code. On a public endpoint guarded only by the per-address limiter, that is a deliberate CPU and memory cost per request. A fast keyed hash of the code would carry the same security given the attempt budget.

---

### tenant_scope

- **Purpose.** Single-tenant isolation. Every user belongs to exactly one `Tenant`, and `Authenticate` publishes `tenant_id` as both a data scope and an event label.
- **Adds.** Table `Tenant` (`name` slug unique; `domain` unique, nullable and indexed). Column `User.tenant_id` (NOT NULL, indexed). Queries `GetUserTenant`, `GetTenantBySlug`, `GetTenantByDomain`. A scope decorator, and the tenant-aware SignUp create path. Forwarded header `Host`.
- **Requires.** `sign_up_turnkey`. **Conflicts with** `oauth`. Combined files: `service_account_tenant.go` (with `service_account`) and `signup_confirm_tenant.go` (with `sign_up_confirmation`). Default off.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tenant_scope/scope-from-user-row` | `scopes["tenant_id"]` and `labels["tenant_id"]` MUST come from the authenticated user's stored tenant. They MUST NOT come from a header or a request field. | Security | `TestResolveTenantScope_PublishesBothKeys` |
| `tenant_scope/lookup-error-fails-closed` | A tenant lookup failure MUST fail `Authenticate`. | Failure & configuration | `TestResolveTenantScope_LookupErrorPropagates` |
| `tenant_scope/no-tenantless-principal` | A principal whose tenant cannot be resolved MUST NOT be served unscoped. | Security | `TestResolveTenantScope_NoTenantStampsNeither` asserts the opposite: an empty tenant stamps neither key and the request proceeds (see gap 3) |
| `tenant_scope/signup-requires-tenant` | SignUp MUST resolve a tenant from the URL slug (preferred) or the forwarded Host, port stripped. When neither names an existing tenant, it MUST refuse with `TENANT_UNKNOWN` (InvalidArgument) and write nothing. | Failure & configuration | `TestResolveSignupTenant_ByForwardedHost` (the success path only) |
| `tenant_scope/oauth-excluded` | Activating `tenant_scope` together with `oauth` MUST fail codegen. | Failure & configuration | none in this repository (enforced by the manifest's `conflicts_with`) |
| `tenant_scope/tenant-lookup-indexed` | Tenant resolution MUST be served by the unique indexes on `Tenant.name` and `Tenant.domain`, and `GetUserTenant` by the primary key. | Performance | none (schema only) |

**Gaps / suspected defects**

1. **Addresses are unique across all tenants.** `User.email` is globally unique (`models.proto:72`), and `GetUserByEmail` has no tenant predicate. As a result:
   - the same person cannot hold accounts in two tenants;
   - SignUp in tenant B reveals that an address is registered in tenant A (`EMAIL_TAKEN`);
   - `SignIn` arriving on tenant B's host signs in a tenant-A account, and the session is scoped to A.

   Either make uniqueness `(tenant_id, email)` and scope the lookup by the request's tenant, or document that a tenant does not partition identities.
2. **Any caller can register into any tenant.** The tenant is chosen by a client-supplied slug or Host. Without `sign_up_allowlist` or `invite_only`, every tenant accepts open self-registration. That should be stated as a property of the feature.
3. **Fixed: an empty tenant fails closed.** When `GetUserTenant` answers no tenant, `resolveTenantScope` refuses (`errNoTenant`), and `Authenticate` answers Unauthenticated with no principal, instead of an unscoped principal in a project that scopes by tenant (`TestResolveTenantScope_NoTenantRefuses`, `TestRunScopeDecorators_NoTenantIsUnauthenticated`, `TestAuthenticate_AnAccountWithNoTenantIsRefused`).
4. **Bootstrap roles are global.** `CountUsers` counts every tenant, so the bootstrap-admin roles go to the first user of the deployment. Each tenant's first user receives only the default roles. That is defensible, but undocumented.

---

### marketing_consent

- **Purpose.** The user's own consent to marketing messages, kept as a value of the identity and answered by Authenticate, so the gateway's request stream carries it to the tool that sends the messages. It changes nothing about what is recorded.
- **Adds.** Columns `User.marketing_consent` (bool, FALSE) and `User.marketing_consent_at` (NULL = never asked). Query `GetUserMarketingConsent`. Mutations `SetUserMarketingConsent` and the conditional `WithdrawUserMarketingConsent`, which emit `MarketingConsentChanged` (`auth.user.marketing_consent_changed`: `user_id`, `consent`, `decided_at`, `device_id`). Service methods `GetMarketingConsent`, `SetMarketingConsent` and the internal `WithdrawMarketingConsent` (a withdrawal the user made in the marketing tool, for the domain's own code). REST endpoints `GET` and `PUT /auth/marketing-consent` (bearer). `AuthResp.marketing_consent` (7).
- **Requires / conflicts / combined files.** `authenticate_turnkey`. Default off.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| `marketing_consent/caller-only` | `GetMarketingConsent` and `SetMarketingConsent` MUST act on the authenticated caller. Their requests MUST NOT be able to name another user, and no admin path MUST write it (the internal `WithdrawMarketingConsent` names the user and can only withdraw: `marketing_consent/withdraw-only`). | Security | `TestSetMarketingConsent_RecordsTheCallersDecision`, `TestGetMarketingConsent_NeverAskedHasNoDecisionTime`, `TestMarketingConsent_NeedsACaller` |
| `marketing_consent/one-statement` | A decision MUST be stored with its time and announced (`MarketingConsentChanged`) in one statement. A withdrawal MUST be recorded like a consent. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestSetUserMarketingConsent_StampsAndAnnouncesInOneStatement` |
| `marketing_consent/withdraw-only` | `WithdrawMarketingConsent` — the user unsubscribed in the marketing tool at `withdrawn_at` — MUST only ever record a withdrawal, in ONE conditional statement (`WithdrawUserMarketingConsent`) that writes false at `withdrawn_at` only over a yes decided before it or no decision, and announces it as `MarketingConsentChanged` only when it wrote. A newer decision — a consent the user gives at that very instant included — and a withdrawal already on record MUST stand and be answered as they are, so a late or redelivered unsubscribe cancels nothing. A request without a user or `withdrawn_at` MUST be refused (`InvalidArgument`), an unknown user MUST answer `NotFound` with nothing written. It names the user, so it MUST carry `(w17.contrib.plugin_internal_rpc)` and no preset MUST surface it. | Security | `TestWithdrawMarketingConsent_OnlyWithdraws`, `TestWithdrawMarketingConsent_ANewerConsentStands`, `TestWithdrawUserMarketingConsent_IsOneConditionalStatement`, `TestWithdrawMarketingConsent_IsMarkedInternal` |
| `marketing_consent/answered-by-authenticate` | `AuthResp.marketing_consent` MUST be the stored consent, through either token lookup. An account never asked MUST answer false. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestAuthenticate_AnswersTheMarketingConsent`, `TestTokenLookupsProjectTheMarketingConsent` |

**Gaps / suspected defects**

1. **No admin view.** The admin `Users` page cannot gate a column per feature, so an operator does not see the consent. A support case reads it from the database.

---

### step_up

- **Purpose.** The signed-in person confirms their password in their current session, so a sensitive action can require a recent proof that the person is themselves. A valid token alone does not show that: a token and its device key can be copied off a machine, and the password is not on it. The feature records the confirmation and does not decide what it guards. The console's secret write grants are its first consumer, and they read `UserToken.stepped_up_at` of the caller's session.
- **Adds.** Columns `UserToken.stepped_up_at` (NULL until the first confirmation), `UserToken.step_up_attempts` (COUNTER) and `UserToken.step_up_locked_until`. Mutations `MarkSessionSteppedUp`, `RecordPasswordStepUpAttempt` and `LockPasswordStepUp`. Service method `ConfirmPassword`. REST endpoint `POST /auth/password/confirm` (bearer).
- **Requires / conflicts / combined files.** None. Default off.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| `step_up/own-session` | The confirmation MUST stamp the caller's own session token, keyed on the authenticated user. The request MUST NOT be able to name another session. | Security | `TestConfirmPassword_StampsTheCallersSession` |
| `step_up/session-only` | An API token MUST be refused before the password is checked: it has no session to step up, and it must not vouch for a person. | Security | `TestConfirmPassword_RefusesAWrongPasswordAndAnAPIToken` |
| `step_up/wrong-password-stamps-nothing` | A wrong password MUST stamp nothing. | Security | `TestConfirmPassword_RefusesAWrongPasswordAndAnAPIToken` |
| `step_up/guess-budget` | Every guess MUST be counted before it is checked, in one statement. Five wrong ones in a row MUST lock the session's step-up for fifteen minutes, the right password included. A confirmation MUST reset the count. | Security | `TestConfirmPassword_LocksAfterFiveWrongPasswords`. **Unit (mock) only:** a live test would run six concurrent wrong guesses and assert one is refused as locked. |
| `step_up/api-token-asks-for-it` | With `api_token_step_up_seconds` > 0, `CreateApiToken` MUST refuse (`API_TOKEN_NEEDS_STEP_UP`) unless the caller's own session stepped up no longer ago than that. A token outlives the session that mints it, so minting one is what a stolen session would do. 0 MUST ask nothing. Without `step_up` staged, a value > 0 MUST fail the boot: no session could step up, so no person could create a token. | Security | `TestRequireRecentStepUp`, `TestCheckAPITokenConfig` |

---

### step_up_code

- **Purpose.** A session steps up with the second factor instead of the password: the person's authenticator when one is confirmed, otherwise a code mailed to the account's address. The stamp is `step_up`'s (`UserToken.stepped_up_at`), so a consumer asking for a recent step-up does not care which proof gave it. With `step_up_password=false` the second factor is the ONLY step-up, and a stolen session plus a phished password is not enough.
- **Adds.** Columns `UserToken.step_up_code_hash` and `UserToken.step_up_code_sent_at`. Query `GetSessionStepUp`. Mutations `SetStepUpCode` (emits `StepUpCodeRequested`, topic `auth.step_up.code_requested`, carrying the plaintext code for a hand-written delivery channel, like `MfaChallengeRequested`) and `ConsumeStepUpCode`. Service methods `RequestStepUpCode` and `ConfirmStepUp`. REST `POST /auth/step-up/code` and `POST /auth/step-up/confirm` (bearer). Env `step_up_password` (default true).
- **Requires / conflicts / combined files.** Requires `step_up` and `two_factor`. Go file: `handlers/step_up_code.go`.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| `step_up_code/proof-is-the-sign-ins` | `RequestStepUpCode` MUST name the authenticator, and mail nothing, for a person with a confirmed one (built-in TOTP on); otherwise it MUST mail a six-digit code. An unreadable enrolment MUST refuse rather than mail. | Security | `TestRequestStepUpCode_AuthenticatorOrMailedCode` |
| `step_up_code/own-session` | A mailed code's hash MUST be stamped on the caller's own session row, and only that session MUST be able to spend it. The plaintext MUST travel only in the event, never stored. | Security | `TestRequestStepUpCode_AuthenticatorOrMailedCode`, `TestConfirmStepUp_MailedCode` |
| `step_up_code/no-flood` | A session MUST get a new mailed code at most once a minute; asking again within it MUST answer the code already out, with its expiry. | Security | `TestRequestStepUpCode_DoesNotResendWithinAMinute` |
| `step_up_code/session-only` | An API token MUST be refused by both methods before anything is mailed or counted. | Security | `TestStepUpCode_RefusesAnAPIToken` |
| `step_up_code/single-use-and-expiry` | A mailed code MUST stamp the session once (spent in the same statement, and only while it is still the live code) and MUST be refused after `mfa_code_ttl_seconds`. An authenticator code MUST be claimed (its time step), so it too works once. | Security | `TestConfirmStepUp_MailedCode`, `TestConfirmStepUp_ExpiredMailedCode`, `TestConfirmStepUp_Authenticator` |
| `step_up_code/one-budget` | Every guess MUST be counted before it is checked, in the session budget `ConfirmPassword` spends: five wrong in a row lock the session's step-up for fifteen minutes, the right code included. | Security | `TestConfirmStepUp_LocksAfterFiveWrongCodes` |
| `step_up_code/password-off` | With `step_up_password=false`, `ConfirmPassword` MUST refuse (`STEP_UP_PASSWORD_OFF`) before it counts or checks anything. | Security | `TestConfirmPassword_OffWhenTheSecondFactorIsTheOnlyProof` |

---

### password_change

- **Purpose.** An authenticated principal replaces its own password, proving the current one. No mail channel is needed.
- **Adds.** Mutations `UpdateUserPasswordIfUnchanged` (compare-and-swap on the verified hash; it also stamps `User.device_trust_revoked_at`) and `RevokeOtherSessionTokens`. Service method `ChangePassword`. REST endpoint `POST /auth/password/change` (bearer).
- **Requires / conflicts / combined files.** None. Default off.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| `password_change/caller-only` | The account changed MUST be the authenticated principal's, read by user id. It MUST NOT be an address from the request. | Security | `TestChangePassword_WritesUnderTheVerifiedHash` |
| `password_change/current-password-required` | A wrong current password MUST write nothing and end no session. | Security | `TestChangePassword_WrongCurrentPasswordWritesNothing` |
| `password_change/compare-and-swap` | The write MUST apply only while the stored hash still equals the one just verified. A concurrent change MUST turn the later request into the wrong-password refusal. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestChangePassword_RefusesWhenTheHashMovedUnderneath`. **Unit (mock) only:** a live test would run two concurrent changes from one old password and assert one success, one refusal, and a final hash matching the winner. |
| `password_change/other-sessions-end-atomically` | Every other SESSION token MUST be revoked (reason PASSWORD_CHANGED, the person as `revoked_by`; the rows stay) in the same transaction as the new hash. The caller's own session and all API tokens MUST be kept. A failed sweep MUST roll back the password. | Security | `TestChangePassword_EndsEveryOtherSession`, `TestChangePassword_RevokesTheOtherSessions`, `TestChangePassword_AFailedSignOutFailsTheChange` (the failure is returned; no test wires `DistTx`, so the rollback itself is **unit (mock) only**: a live test would make the sweep fail and assert the old password still signs in) |
| `password_change/ends-device-trust` | A successful change MUST end the trust of every device of the account, the caller's own included, in the same statement as the new hash. The caller's session keeps working; its device passes the second factor again on the next sign-in under `new_devices`. | Security | `TestChangePassword_RevokesDeviceTrustInTheSameStatement` (the statement), `TestMintDevice_TrustRevokedByACredentialChange_NotTrusted` (the read side). **Unit (mock) only:** a live test would change the password and show the same device key is challenged again. |
| `password_change/errors-not-disguised` | A non-NotFound write failure MUST surface as itself. It MUST NOT surface as a wrong password. | Failure & configuration | `TestChangePassword_OtherWriteErrorsAreNotDisguised` |
| `password_change/hash-outside-tx` | The new hash MUST be derived before the transaction opens, so no row lock is held during the derivation. | Performance | `TestChangePassword_HashesBeforeTheTransactionOpens` |
| `password_change/guess-budget` | Verifying the current password MUST be bounded per account, because a stolen session must not become an unlimited offline-speed oracle for the password. | Security | `TestChangePassword_TheCurrentPasswordSpendsTheSignInBudget` |

**Gaps / suspected defects**

1. **Fixed: build failure without `api_token`.** The session-realm filter of `RevokeOtherSessionTokens` (then `DeleteOtherSessionTokens`) is now gated `?feature(api_token)`. Without `api_token`, every token is a session.
2. **Fixed: no attempt limit on the current-password check.** `ChangePassword` is bearer-authenticated, so the gateway's `failed_only` limiter does not apply. Its current password now spends the account's sign-in budget (`sign_in_turnkey/failure-budget`), counted before it is checked; past it the request is `PASSWORD_ATTEMPTS_SPENT` (ResourceExhausted), the right password included, and a right password within it starts the budget over.
3. **Fixed: outstanding reset tokens survived a change.** `ChangePassword` spends every live reset link in its transaction (see `password_reset` gap 2).
4. **Fixed: the wrong-password refusal looked like an expired session.** It was `Unauthenticated`, which clients answer by signing the person out. It is now `PermissionDenied` with `PASSWORD_NOT_CONFIRMED` on `current_password`, as `ConfirmPassword` answers a wrong password; a concurrent change (the compare-and-swap missing) answers the same.

---

### password_reset

- **Purpose.** The forgotten-password flow. A single-use, time-bounded token is delivered by a hand-written mail subscriber and then redeemed for a new password.
- **Adds.** Table `PasswordResetToken` (`token_hash` unique sha256; `user_id` indexed; `expires_at`; `consumed_at`). Mutations `CreatePasswordResetToken` (emits `PasswordResetRequested` with the plaintext token), `ConsumePasswordResetToken`, `RevokeTokensForReset`. Service methods `RequestPasswordReset` and `ResetPassword`. REST endpoints `POST /auth/password/reset-request` (**public**, charged on every request) and `POST /auth/password/reset` (**public, failed_only**).
- **Env knobs.** `password_reset_ttl_seconds` (default 3600).
- **Requires / conflicts / combined files.** None. Default off.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| `password_reset/request-constant-answer` | `RequestPasswordReset` MUST return the same empty success whether or not the address has an account, including when the lookup fails. | Security | `TestRequestPasswordReset_UnknownEmail_NoOp`, `TestRequestPasswordReset_KnownEmail_StoresHashEmitsPlaintext`. **Violated** on the error path: a `CreatePasswordResetToken` failure is returned, and only for an existing account (gap 3). |
| `password_reset/token-hashed-high-entropy` | The token MUST be 256 bits from a CSPRNG. Only its sha256 MUST be stored, and the plaintext MUST travel only in `PasswordResetRequested`. | Security | `TestRequestPasswordReset_KnownEmail_StoresHashEmitsPlaintext` |
| `password_reset/consume-atomic` | Single use and expiry MUST be enforced by one conditional `UPDATE` (`consumed_at IS NULL AND expires_at > NOW()`). Two concurrent redemptions MUST set at most one password. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestResetPassword_InvalidToken_Rejected`. **Unit (mock) only:** a live test would race two `ResetPassword` calls with one token and assert exactly one success and one opaque refusal. |
| `password_reset/consume-and-write-together` | Spending the token and writing the password MUST commit together. A failure after the consume MUST leave the token unspent. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestConsumePaths_TheTokenAndItsWriteDoNotSeparate`, `TestResetPassword_FailureAfterConsume_RollsBack`, `TestResetPassword_Success_CommitsOnTheHandlersConnection`, `TestResetPassword_BeginRefused_SpendsNothing` |
| `password_reset/opaque-refusal` | An unknown, expired or used token MUST produce one identical `Unauthenticated` answer. An outage MUST surface as `Unavailable`. | Security | `TestConsumePaths_ABadTokenIsAlwaysTheSameOpaqueRefusal`, `TestConsumePaths_AnOutageIsStillAnOutage` |
| `password_reset/all-tokens-revoked` | A successful reset MUST revoke every token of the account, sessions and API tokens alike (reason PASSWORD_RESET, no actor; the rows stay), and MUST report whether that sweep ran. An account with no live token reports it ran (the statement has no RETURNING). | Security | `TestResetPassword_ValidToken_UpdatesAndRevokes`, `TestResetPassword_ReportsWhenTheSessionSweepDidNotRun`, `TestResetPassword_ReportsASweepThatSucceeded`, `TestResetPassword_RevokesEveryToken` |
| `password_reset/one-live-token-per-account` | Redeeming a token, or changing the password by any route, MUST invalidate every other outstanding reset token of that account, in the same transaction as the new password. | Security | TestResetPassword_SpendsEveryOtherResetLink, TestChangePassword_SpendsTheAccountsResetLinks (**unit (mock) only**: a live test requests two links, redeems one, and asserts the other is refused) |
| `password_reset/no-hash-for-bogus-token` | No password-hash derivation MUST run for a token that fails the consume. | Performance | none (ordering in `consumeAndSetPassword`, untested) |
| `password_reset/request-limited` | Every reset request MUST be charged to the gateway's per-address limiter. | Performance | none (manifest only) |

**Gaps / suspected defects**

1. **Fixed: credentials travel sealed.** `PasswordResetRequested`, `EmailVerificationRequested`, `SignUpConfirmationRequested`, `StepUpCodeRequested` and `MfaChallengeRequested` carry their token or code sealed to `delivery_recipient` (age) in `sealed_token` / `sealed_code`, and the clear field empty: the event bus, its dead-letter queue and its backups hold ciphertext only. See `delivery/sealed`.
2. **Fixed: sibling tokens stayed valid.** A reset and a password change now spend every live link of the account in their transaction. These are the only paths that set the password of an existing account: `user_admin` has no password write, and sign-up and OAuth create a new account, which has no links yet. Still open: an admin email change (`UpdateUser`, `user_admin`) leaves the links sent to the old address alive until they expire, and so does disabling an account.
3. **Fixed: the request endpoint no longer enumerates accounts by time or by error.** Both paths do the same work before answering — the address lookup — and a registered address's link is minted after the answer, on a context the request no longer cancels (`mintResetLink` through `runDetached`). A mint that fails reaches nobody: the answer is the unknown address's (`TestRequestPasswordReset_AnswersBeforeTheLinkIsMinted`, `TestRequestPasswordReset_AFailedMintAnswersLikeAnUnknownAddress`).
4. **Fixed: the session sweep runs inside the reset's transaction.** `RevokeTokensForReset` runs before the commit, so a reset and the end of every old session land together; a sweep that fails rolls the reset back (the password unchanged, the link not spent) and the person retries, instead of a thief's session outliving the reset. Without a transaction coordinator (a hand-built handler) the statements commit one by one and the sweep's outcome is reported in `sessions_revoked` (`TestResetPassword_SweepsSessionsInsideTheTransaction`, `TestResetPassword_AFailedSweepRollsTheResetBack`, `TestResetPassword_ReportsWhenTheSessionSweepDidNotRun`).
5. **No per-account issuance limit.** Mail flooding of one victim is bounded only per requesting client address.

---

### email_verification

- **Purpose.** Address-ownership verification through a single-use link. It is also the home of `User.email_proof`, the floor that `email_proof_required` (in `org_invite`) reads.
- **Adds.** Table `EmailVerificationToken` (`token_hash` unique sha256; `user_id` indexed; `expires_at`; `consumed_at`). Columns `User.email_verified_at` and `User.email_proof` (UNPROVEN, DERIVED_FROM_INVITE, SELF_CONFIRMED). Mutations `CreateEmailVerificationToken` (emits `EmailVerificationRequested` with the plaintext token), `ConsumeEmailVerificationToken`, `MarkEmailVerified`, `MarkEmailDerivedFromInvite`. Service methods `RequestEmailVerification` and `VerifyEmail`. REST endpoints `POST /auth/email/verify-request` (bearer) and `POST /auth/email/verify` (**public, failed_only**). Seams installed: the invite email-proof gate, `markEmailDerived`, `markEmailConfirmed`.
- **Env knobs.** `email_verification_ttl_seconds` (default 86400). `email_proof_required` is read through this feature's gate.
- **Requires / conflicts / combined files.** None (`oauth` requires it). Default off.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| `email_verification/own-address-only` | `RequestEmailVerification` MUST mint a token only for the authenticated caller's stored address. | Security | `TestRequestEmailVerification_StoresHash` |
| `email_verification/token-hashed-high-entropy` | The token MUST be 256 bits from a CSPRNG and stored only as sha256. | Security | `TestRequestEmailVerification_StoresHash` |
| `email_verification/consume-atomic` | Single use and expiry MUST be enforced in one conditional `UPDATE`, and the consume and the stamp MUST commit together. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestVerifyEmail_ValidToken_MarksVerified`, `TestVerifyEmail_InvalidToken_Rejected`, `TestConsumePaths_TheTokenAndItsWriteDoNotSeparate`. **Unit (mock) only** for the conditional `UPDATE`: a live test would race two redemptions and replay an expired token. |
| `email_verification/opaque-refusal` | An unknown, expired or used token MUST produce one identical `Unauthenticated` answer. | Security | `TestConsumePaths_ABadTokenIsAlwaysTheSameOpaqueRefusal` |
| `email_verification/stamp-and-proof-agree` | A verification MUST set `email_verified_at` and `email_proof = SELF_CONFIRMED` in one statement. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestVerifyEmail_ValidToken_MarksVerified` (asserts that `MarkEmailVerified` is called, not what the DQL writes) |
| `email_verification/derived-never-downgrades` | Marking an address `DERIVED_FROM_INVITE` MUST NOT lower a `SELF_CONFIRMED` address. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestAcceptOrgInvite_BoundRecordsDerivedAndOpenDoesNot` (caller side). The guard sits in the DQL WHERE clause and is **unit (mock) only**. |
| `email_verification/proof-bound-to-address` | A proof MUST certify the address it was delivered to. Changing `User.email` MUST reset both `email_verified_at` and `email_proof`, and MUST void every outstanding verification token. | Security | none (see gaps 1 and 2) |
| `email_verification/floor-enforced` | When `email_proof_required` is `derived` or `full`, an acceptance below the floor MUST be refused with `EMAIL_NOT_VERIFIED`, without revealing whether the invitation exists and without consuming it. | Security | `TestEmailProofGate_FullRefusesAnUnprovenAccount`, `TestEmailProofGate_FullAllowsASelfConfirmedAccount`, `TestEmailProofGate_NoneChangesNothing`, `TestEmailProofGate_RefusalIsActionableAndLeaksNothing`, `TestEmailProofGate_DerivedFloorSeparatesBoundFromOpen`, `TestEmailProofGate_AlreadyDerivedSatisfiesDerivedButNotFull`, `TestAcceptOrgInvite_ARefusedAcceptanceDoesNotConsumeTheInvitation` |
| `email_verification/floor-config-fails-boot` | An `email_proof_required` value other than `none`, `derived` or `full`, or a value above `none` in an activation without this feature, MUST fail boot. | Failure & configuration | none (see gap 3) |

**Gaps / suspected defects**

1. **Fixed: a link mailed to the old address verified the new one.** `EmailVerificationToken` still has no email column, but the only path that changes an address, the admin's `UpdateAccount`, spends every live verification link (and every reset link, which would let the old mailbox set the password) in the transaction that changes it. Saving the address the account already has writes nothing, so it keeps its verification.
2. **Fixed: an address change left `email_proof` stale.** `UpdateUser` and `BeginUserErasure` reset it with `email_verified_at` (see `user_admin` gap 3).
3. **Fixed: the floor failed open on configuration.** An unknown `email_proof_required`, or one above `none` without `email_verification`, refuses to boot (`checkEmailProofFloor`, see `org_invite` gap 3).
4. **Requests are not limited.** `RequestEmailVerification` is bearer-authenticated, so no public limiter applies. One session can mint unlimited tokens and mails, including for an already-verified address, and every token stays redeemable for its TTL.
5. **Verification tokens on the events channel.** See `password_reset` gap 1.

---

### two_factor

**Purpose.** Adds a second factor to password sign-in. Built-in TOTP (RFC 6238) is on by default. An event-delivered numeric code is the fallback, and one-time recovery codes stand in for a lost authenticator. When a challenge fires, SignIn returns `mfa_required` + `challenge_id` instead of a token, and VerifyMfa completes the sign-in.

**Adds.**
- Tables: `UserTotpSecret` (one LIVE per user, unique `(user_id, ended_key)`; `seed` CRYPTED_SECRET; `last_step` replay guard; `stepup_attempts` / `stepup_locked_until`; `revoked_at`), `UserRecoveryCode` (SHA-256 of the normalised code, `used_at`, `revoked_at`), `MfaChallenge` (`code_hash` for the fallback path, `attempts`, `consumed_at`, `public_computer` — the challenged SignIn's choice, which VerifyMfa honours; expiry is computed as `created_at + mfa_code_ttl_seconds` and is not stored).
- Business RPCs: `EnrollTotp`, `ConfirmTotp`, `DisableTotp`, `GetMfaStatus`, `GenerateRecoveryCodes`, `VerifyMfa`. Also a SignIn seam (the challenge gate).
- REST (`/auth/mfa`): `POST /verify` (**public, failed_only**), `POST /totp`, `POST /totp/confirm`, `DELETE /totp`, `GET /status`, `POST /recovery-codes`. All endpoints except `/verify` need a bearer.
- Storage RPCs: `GetTotpSecret`, `CountRecoveryCodes`, `GetMfaChallenge`, `LockUserForIssuance` (SELECT … FOR UPDATE on the user row), `CountRecentMfaChallenges`; `CreateTotpSecret`, `ConfirmTotpSecret`, `MoveTotpSeed`, `ClaimTotpStep`, `RecordStepUpAttempt`, `LockStepUp`, `ResetStepUp`, `CreateRecoveryCode`, `RevokeRecoveryCodes`, `ConsumeRecoveryCode`, `RevokeTotpSecret`, `CreateMfaChallenge`, `ConsumeMfaChallenge`, `RecordMfaAttempt`, `SupersedePendingMfaChallenges`.
- Events: `MfaChallengeRequested` (carries `totp_enrolled`, `email`, the **plaintext** fallback `code`, `expires_at`), `MfaVerified`, `TotpEnrolled`, `TotpConfirmed`, `TotpDisabled`, `RecoveryCodeUsed`.
- Env knobs: `two_factor_totp` (default true), `two_factor_scope` (`all_logins` | `new_devices`), `two_factor_policy` (`required` | `optional` | `disabled`, default `required`; who is challenged at all — with `org_two_factor_policy`, per organization and kind), `mfa_code_ttl_seconds` (300), `mfa_max_challenges_per_window` (10), `mfa_challenge_window_seconds` (900), `totp_issuer`, `two_factor_secret_key` (legacy; only used to open seeds sealed by earlier releases).
- Constants: 5 attempts per challenge; ±1 time step of skew; 6 digits; 10 recovery codes of 10 characters from a 31-symbol alphabet (about 49.5 bits each); step-up locks for 15 min after 5 wrong codes.

**Requires:** `sign_in_turnkey`. **Conflicts:** none. **Combined files:** none. `two_factor_scope=new_devices` needs no other feature (device identity is base tier).

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| `two_factor/no-session-while-challenged` | When the gate raises a challenge, SignIn MUST return `mfa_required` + `challenge_id` and MUST NOT issue a session token. | Security | TestMfaGate_AllLogins_NoTotp_GeneratesCode, TestMfaGate_TotpEnrolled_NoCode, TestMfaGate_NewDevices_ChallengesUntrusted |
| `two_factor/new-devices-needs-proven-trust` | Under `new_devices`, a challenge MUST be skipped only for a device whose key signed the request (the gateway-verified thumbprint) and whose (user, device) row holds trust later than the account's last trust revocation. Nothing a client sends besides the proof MUST count. | Security | TestMfaGate_NewDevices_SkipsTrusted, TestMfaGate_NewDevices_ChallengesUntrusted, TestSignIn_TrustIsPerUserAndDeviceOnASharedDevice, TestMintDevice_TrustRevokedByACredentialChange_NotTrusted |
| `two_factor/scope-off-is-dev-only` | `two_factor_scope=off` MUST spare the challenge only with `W17_ENV=dev`. Anywhere else the boot MUST refuse it, and a handler that skipped the boot check MUST challenge anyway. | Security | TestTwoFactorScopeOff_DevelopmentOnly |
| `two_factor/seed-encrypted-at-rest` | The TOTP seed MUST be stored only encrypted with the project field keyring. It MUST NOT be stored or logged in plaintext. | Security | none. Encryption is done by the storage layer. A live test must read the raw `seed` column and show ciphertext that decrypts only with the keyring. |
| `two_factor/legacy-seed-moved-once` | A seed sealed under `two_factor_secret_key` MUST verify with that key, then be moved into `seed` and cleared from the legacy column. If the key is unusable, verification MUST be refused (FailedPrecondition), never accepted. | Security | TestLegacySeed_VerifiesAndMoves, TestLegacySeed_KeyGone_Refuses |
| `two_factor/totp-step-single-use` | A TOTP code MUST be accepted at most once: only for a time step later than `last_step`, claimed by one conditional UPDATE. When two requests present the same code at once, exactly one MUST succeed. | Security | TestTotp_ACodeIsAcceptedOnce; TestTotp_ConcurrentReuseIsAcceptedOnce is **unit (mock) only**. A live test must fire N parallel VerifyMfa calls with one code against the database and show exactly one `ClaimTotpStep` row returned. |
| `two_factor/challenge-attempt-budget` | A challenge MUST allow at most 5 verification attempts. Each attempt MUST be counted (atomic increment with RETURNING) *before* the code is checked. After the 5th, even the correct code MUST be refused. | Security | TestVerifyMfa_LocksOutAfterMaxAttempts; TestVerifyMfa_ConcurrentGuessesShareOneBudget is **unit (mock) only**. A live test must show that 20 parallel wrong guesses produce attempts 1..20 and at most 5 code checks. |
| `two_factor/issuance-cap` | At most `mfa_max_challenges_per_window` challenges MUST be issued per user per window. The count and the insert MUST be one section serialised by a row lock on the user. | Security | TestMfaIssuance_CapRefusesOnceTheWindowIsSpent, TestMfaIssuance_CountsOverTheConfiguredWindow; TestMfaIssuance_CapHoldsUnderConcurrency is **unit (mock) only**. A live test must send concurrent sign-ins for one user and show no more than the cap of `MfaChallenge` rows (proves the FOR UPDATE plus the distributed transaction). |
| `two_factor/one-live-challenge` | Issuing a challenge MUST retire every pending challenge of the user in the same transaction, so at most one code is live. If retiring fails, nothing MUST be minted. | Security | TestMfaIssuance_NewChallengeRetiresThePendingOnes, TestMfaIssuance_SupersedeAndCreateAreOneSeam, TestMfaIssuance_AFailedSupersedeMintsNothing |
| `two_factor/challenge-single-use` | A consumed or superseded challenge MUST NOT yield a session. When two concurrent verifications both pass the code check, only the one whose conditional consume returns a row MUST get a session. | Security | TestVerifyMfa_Consumed_Unauthenticated; the consume race is **unit (mock) only**. A live test needs two parallel correct fallback-code verifications and must show one session. |
| `two_factor/challenge-expires` | A challenge older than `mfa_code_ttl_seconds` MUST be refused, and so MUST one read without its creation time (no proof of age). | Security | TestVerifyMfa_Expired_Unauthenticated, TestVerifyMfa_AChallengeWithoutACreationTimeIsRefused |
| `two_factor/verify-is-one-transaction` | Checking the code, the account check and the consume MUST be one transaction: a lost consume or a refused account MUST roll back what the check spent (a recovery code, a TOTP step), and a refused account MUST NOT consume the challenge (no `MfaVerified`). | Consistency & concurrency | TestVerifyMfa_ALostConsumeRollsTheCodeBack, TestVerifyMfa_ADisabledAccountDoesNotConsumeTheChallenge, TestVerifyMfa_ASuccessCommitsOnce |
| `two_factor/fallback-code-hashed` | The fallback code MUST be persisted only as a slow password hash. The plaintext MUST travel only in `MfaChallengeRequested`. A TOTP-path challenge MUST carry no code. | Security | TestMfaGate_AllLogins_NoTotp_GeneratesCode, TestMfaGate_TotpEnrolled_NoCode |
| `two_factor/fallback-code-not-client-visible` | `MfaChallengeRequested` (plaintext code) MUST NOT be delivered to any client-facing event stream. It is for a server-side delivery subscriber only. | Security | none. The event declaration carries no visibility restriction. |
| `two_factor/opaque-verify` | Every VerifyMfa refusal (unknown, expired, consumed, over budget, wrong code) MUST be the same Unauthenticated. | Security | TestVerifyMfa_WrongCode_Unauthenticated, TestVerifyMfa_Expired_Unauthenticated, TestVerifyMfa_Consumed_Unauthenticated, TestVerifyMfa_LocksOutAfterMaxAttempts |
| `two_factor/no-weaker-path-on-read-failure` | A failed read of the enrolment MUST refuse. It MUST NOT skip the challenge, fall back to the fallback-code path, report "not enrolled", or let DisableTotp proceed without a code. | Security | TestMfaGate_ReadFailure_RefusesRatherThanSkipTheChallenge, TestVerifyMfa_ReadFailure_DoesNotFallToTheWeakerPath, TestGetMfaStatus_ReadFailure_Errors, TestDisableTotp_ReadFailure_DoesNotDisableWithoutACode |
| `two_factor/step-up-for-confirmed-changes` | Replacing (EnrollTotp) or removing (DisableTotp) a confirmed authenticator, or regenerating recovery codes, MUST require a current TOTP code or an unused recovery code. A session alone MUST NOT be enough. | Security | TestEnrollTotp_ReplacingAConfirmedAuthenticatorTakesACode, TestDisableTotp_ValidCode_Deletes, TestDisableTotp_WrongCode_Rejected, TestDisableTotp_EmptyCode_Rejected, TestGenerateRecoveryCodes_ReplacesTheSetBehindTheStepUp, TestStepUp_ARecoveryCodeNeedsNoSeed |
| `two_factor/step-up-budget` | After 5 wrong step-up codes, step-up MUST be locked for 15 minutes, and even a correct code MUST be refused while locked. A success MUST reset the count. Counting MUST be one statement. | Security | TestStepUp_FiveWrongCodesLockIt, TestStepUp_ASuccessResetsTheCount; the single-statement race is **unit (mock) only**. |
| `two_factor/pending-cancel-without-code` | An unconfirmed enrolment MUST be cancellable without a code. | Security | TestDisableTotp_Unconfirmed_NoCodeNeeded |
| `two_factor/first-enrolment-reauth` | Enrolling the first authenticator on an account MUST require fresh proof of the primary credential, not only a bearer session. | Security | none. Not enforced (see gaps). |
| `two_factor/recovery-codes-hashed-single-use` | Recovery codes MUST be shown once, stored only as hashes, and each MUST be spendable exactly once (conditional UPDATE on `used_at IS NULL`). | Security | TestConfirmTotp_HandsOutTenRecoveryCodesStoredHashed, TestVerifyMfa_ARecoveryCodeSignsInOnce; the concurrent spend is **unit (mock) only**. |
| `two_factor/recovery-codes-die-with-authenticator` | Disabling the authenticator MUST revoke its unused recovery codes in the same transaction. | Security | TestDisableTotp_TakesARecoveryCodeAndDropsTheSet, TestDisableTotp_KeepsTheRowsRevokedAndNothingVerifies |
| `two_factor/disable-is-a-state` | Disabling or replacing an authenticator and replacing a recovery-code set MUST NOT delete a row. `RevokeTotpSecret` stamps `revoked_at`, clears `seed` and the legacy `secret` (a revoked authenticator's seed is not kept at rest, as erasure clears it) and releases the user for a new enrolment (`ended_key` = id); `RevokeRecoveryCodes` stamps `revoked_at` on every unused code (a used one keeps `used_at`). Every statement that verifies or counts reads live rows only: `GetTotpSecret`, `ConfirmTotpSecret`, `MoveTotpSeed`, `ClaimTotpStep` and `RecordStepUpAttempt` skip a revoked authenticator, `ConsumeRecoveryCode` and `CountRecoveryCodes` a revoked code — so a disabled authenticator verifies nothing and a replaced code is refused like a used one. | Security | TestDisableTotp_KeepsTheRowsRevokedAndNothingVerifies, TestEnrollTotp_TheReplacedAuthenticatorStaysRevoked, TestGenerateRecoveryCodes_ReplacesTheSetBehindTheStepUp (the old set kept revoked and refused), TestEveryFormerDeleteIsAGuardedStateChange, TestEveryWriteThatUsesARowSkipsAnEndedOne, TestEveryReaderSkipsEndedRows. The predicates are **unit (mock) only**. |
| `two_factor/no-reconfirm` | ConfirmTotp MUST refuse an already-confirmed authenticator, so that new recovery codes cannot be minted without the step-up. | Security | TestConfirmTotp_RefusesAConfirmedAuthenticator |
| `two_factor/unsignable-account-no-session` | A disabled or machine account MUST NOT get a session from VerifyMfa, even with a correct code. | Security | TestVerifyMfa_ADisabledAccountGetsNoSession |
| `two_factor/replace-is-atomic` | Re-enrolment (revoke then create the secret) MUST run in one transaction. A failed create MUST leave the previous authenticator in place. | Consistency & concurrency | TestEnrollTotp_AFailedCreateDoesNotLeaveTheUserWithoutAFactor, TestEnrollTotp_BothWritesGoThroughOneSeam (seam only; actual rollback is **unit (mock) only**) |
| `two_factor/confirm-with-codes-atomic` | Confirming an authenticator and storing its recovery-code set MUST commit together, as must a full set replacement (revoke plus 10 inserts). | Consistency & concurrency | none for rollback. TestConfirmTotp_HandsOutTenRecoveryCodesStoredHashed covers only the success path. |
| `two_factor/emit-after-commit` | `MfaChallengeRequested` MUST be emitted only after the challenge row commits. A rolled-back issuance MUST NOT deliver a code. | Consistency & concurrency | none. Depends on the transaction-scoped emitter. A live test must force a commit failure and observe no event. |
| `two_factor/one-authenticator-per-user` | A user MUST have at most one `UserTotpSecret` row (unique `user_id`). | Consistency & concurrency | none. Schema property; needs a live insert-twice test. |
| `two_factor/indexed-lookups` | Lookups MUST be indexed: `UserTotpSecret.user_id` (indexed; unique with `ended_key`), `UserRecoveryCode(user_id, code_hash)` (unique), `MfaChallenge.user_id` (supersede, count). Challenge lookup is by primary key. | Performance | none (schema) |
| `two_factor/recovery-shape-check-before-lookup` | A code that does not have recovery-code shape MUST be refused without a storage lookup. | Performance | `TestAcceptRecoveryCode_AWrongShapeIsRefusedWithoutALookup` |

The trusted signal is the (user, device) pair's, never a default of "trusted". | Failure & configuration | `TestValidateConfig_NewDevicesNeedsNoFeature`, `TestSignIn_TrustIsPerUserAndDeviceOnASharedDevice` |
| `two_factor/unknown-scope-fails-boot` | An unrecognised `two_factor_scope` value MUST fail boot. | Failure & configuration | TestTwoFactorScope_AnUnknownValueRefusesTheBoot |
| `two_factor/non-positive-knobs-default` | `mfa_code_ttl_seconds`, `mfa_max_challenges_per_window` and `mfa_challenge_window_seconds` ≤ 0 MUST fall back to their defaults and MUST NOT disable the guard. | Failure & configuration | TestMfaIssuance_CountsOverTheConfiguredWindow (configured value only); fallback: none |
| `two_factor/fail-closed-on-unaccounted-guess` | If the attempt counter, the step claim or the issuance count cannot be written or read, the request MUST be refused. | Failure & configuration | TestTotp_AnUnansweredClaimFailsClosed, TestMfaIssuance_CapFailsClosedWhenTheCountIsUnreadable |
| `two_factor/budget-refusal-is-distinct` | A spent issuance budget MUST answer ResourceExhausted with a retry message, not "invalid credentials". | Failure & configuration | TestMfaIssuance_CapRefusalIsNotInvalidCredentials |
| `two_factor/enrol-refused-when-totp-off` | With `two_factor_totp=false`, EnrollTotp and ConfirmTotp MUST be refused (FailedPrecondition). | Failure & configuration | TestEnrollTotp_RefusedWhenTheAuthenticatorIsOff |
| `two_factor/policy-decides-who` | `required` (or unset) MUST challenge every account; `optional` MUST challenge only an account with a confirmed authenticator the built-in verifier checks, and sign the others in with the password; `disabled` MUST challenge nobody. `two_factor_scope` applies only to an account the policy challenges. | Security | TestTwoFactorPolicy_TheDefaultDecidesWhoIsChallenged, TestTwoFactorPolicy_OptionalWithoutTheAuthenticatorChallengesNobody |
| `two_factor/disabled-refuses-enrolment` | Under `disabled`, EnrollTotp and ConfirmTotp MUST be refused (`TWO_FACTOR_DISABLED`): an authenticator nothing asks for would protect nothing. One already confirmed MUST stay stored and count again when the policy changes. | Security | TestTwoFactorPolicy_DisabledRefusesEnrolment |
| `two_factor/unknown-policy-fails-boot` | A `two_factor_policy` that is none of the three MUST fail boot. | Failure & configuration | TestTwoFactorPolicy_TheBootRefusesAnUnknownValue |

**Gaps / suspected defects**

1. `mfa.go:346–363`: **The first enrolment needs only a session.** `EnrollTotp` asks for a step-up only when a *confirmed* authenticator already exists. A stolen bearer can enrol the attacker's authenticator on an account without 2FA, confirm it, and keep the recovery codes. From then on the owner cannot sign in. Password reset does not remove the authenticator, and `reset.go` never touches `UserTotpSecret`.
2. Fixed: the expiry check skipped a challenge without `created_at` (fail-open); it refuses one now. Still: neither `RecordMfaAttempt` nor `ConsumeMfaChallenge` has an age predicate in its `WHERE`, so the handler's earlier read is the only expiry enforcement.
3. `MfaChallengeRequested` (events proto, the `code` field) puts the plaintext fallback code on the `events` channel. The event is internal (`public: false`), so the gateway never streams it to a client; a hand-written consumer that republishes it, or a durable channel's retention, still holds live codes (as `sign_up_confirmation` gap 1).
4. Fixed: a recovery code was spent before `ConsumeMfaChallenge` and burned when a concurrent consume won, and the consume (with `MfaVerified`) ran before the account check. The code check, the account check and the consume are one transaction now (`two_factor/verify-is-one-transaction`).
5. Fixed: `two_factor_scope` is validated at boot; a typo used to mean `all_logins`.
6. No retention: `MfaChallenge` rows are never purged, and the table grows with every challenged sign-in.
7. `required` with an account that has no confirmed authenticator challenges it by message (`MfaChallengeRequested`): a project without its own SMS/e-mail channel locks such an account out. There is no enrolment-only session that would make a required policy enrol the person at their next sign-in instead; until there is, such a project uses `optional`.

---

### devices

**Purpose.** Self-service device management: a user lists their devices and revokes one, every other one, or all. Device identity itself (the Device table, binding, trust) is base tier — see [device identity](#device-identity-base-tier).

**Adds.**
- Business RPCs: `ListDevices`, `RevokeDevice`, `RevokeOtherDevices`, `RevokeAllDevices`.
- REST (`/auth/devices`, all authenticated): `GET ""`, `DELETE /{device_id}`, `POST /revoke-others`, `POST /revoke-all`.
- Storage RPCs: `ListUserDevices` (live, keyed devices only; revoked and legacy rows stay in the table as history); `MarkDeviceRevoked` and `RevokeDeviceTokens` (base tier since the admin Devices page uses them too; this feature passes the device's owner as `revoked_by`), `RevokeUndevicedSessions`, `RevokeAllUserTokens` (declared, not called). No statement deletes a Device row (`DeleteAllUserDevices` was removed: a deleted row would let a revoked key register again) or a token.
- Events: none. Env knobs: none.

**Requires:** none. **Conflicts:** none. **Combined files:** none.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| `devices/revoke-is-for-good` | Revoking a device MUST revoke its tokens (reason DEVICE_REVOKED, the caller as `revoked_by`) and stamp `Device.revoked_at` (never delete a row), so its key can never sign in to the account again; its holder hears `DEVICE_REVOKED`. | Security | `TestRevokeDevice_RevokesTokensThenMarksTheDeviceRevoked`, `TestRevokeDevice_RevokesTheDevicesTokens`, `TestMarkDeviceRevoked_IsAnUpdateNeverADelete`, `TestMintDevice_ARevokedDeviceIsRefusedForGood` |
| `devices/owner-only-revoke` | Every revoke MUST be scoped by the caller's `user_id`. | Security | `TestRevokeDevice_RevokesTokensThenMarksTheDeviceRevoked` (the guard argument; enforcement by `WHERE … AND user_id` is **unit (mock) only**) |
| `devices/revoke-all-spares-api-tokens` | RevokeAllDevices and RevokeOtherDevices MUST end every session, including sessions bound to no device (minted before binding). They MUST NOT revoke API tokens: an API token is bound to a program's key (`Device.api_key`), which `ListUserDevices` — the list ListDevices shows and both calls revoke from — leaves out, and the devices they revoke do not take the API tokens they created with them (the "approved" op of `RevokeDeviceTokens` matches session tokens only, and `RevokeApprovedApiTokens` runs only for ONE device revoked on purpose: `RevokeDevice`, the admin `RevokeDevices`). Sessions bound to no device are revoked with reason UNBOUND_SESSION. | Security | `TestRevokeAllDevices_PerDeviceSparesApiTokens`, `TestRevokeDevices_SweepSessionsNoDeviceHolds`, `TestRevokeOtherDevices_RevokesUnboundSessions`, `TestDeviceManagement_LeavesAPITokensAlone`, `TestRevokeOtherAndAllDevices_SpareTheAPITokensTheDevicesCreated`, `TestSignIn_AProgramKeyDoesNotSignIn` (no session lives on an unlisted key) |
| `devices/revoke-others-keeps-caller` | RevokeOtherDevices MUST keep the device the caller's token is bound to (the envelope's `device_id`, which Authenticate checked against the request's proof) and the caller's session. If the caller's device cannot be pinned, it MUST revoke rather than keep. | Security | `TestRevokeOtherDevices_KeepsTheCallersDevice`, `TestRevokeDevices_SweepSessionsNoDeviceHolds` |
| `devices/current-is-the-bound-device` | ListDevices MUST mark as current only the device the caller's token is bound to, and MUST NOT return the key thumbprint. | Security | `TestListDevices_MarksTrustedAndCurrent` |
| `devices/authenticated-only` | Every device-management RPC MUST refuse a call with no principal. | Security | `TestListDevices_NoCaller_Unauthenticated` |
| `devices/revoke-idempotent` | Revoking a device that is already revoked or gone MUST succeed. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestRevokeDevice_IdempotentOnMissing` |
| `devices/tokens-before-row` | Revocation MUST revoke the tokens before stamping the row, so a partial failure leaves a live, listed device to retry on. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestRevokeDevice_RevokesTokensThenMarksTheDeviceRevoked` |

**Gaps / suspected defects**

1. **Fixed: revoking deleted the device's tokens.** They are revoked now (`revoked_at`, reason DEVICE_REVOKED), and the history stays (`device/revocation-is-a-state`).
2. `ListUserDevices` is unpaged.

---

### oauth

**Purpose.** Generic OAuth2/OIDC sign-in as a *consumer* of external identity providers. Providers are administrator-managed rows rather than code. The callback finds or creates the local user, links the external subject, and issues a session.

**Adds.**
- Tables: `OAuthProvider` (unique `name`; `secret` CRYPTED_SECRET client secret — or, with `team_id` + `key_id`, the PKCS#8 P-256 key a client secret is minted from; legacy plaintext `client_secret` migrated on first use; URLs; `scopes`; `subject_path`/`email_path`; `response_mode` (empty or `form_post`); `team_id`, `key_id`; `enabled`; `tenant_id` gated on tenant_scope) and `OAuthIdentity` (unique `(provider_id, external_id)`, indexed `user_id`).
- Table `OAuthLoginCode` (`code_hash` unique SHA-256, `code_challenge` = SHA-256 hex of the browser's challenge, `expires_at` = now + 2 min, `consumed_at`).
- Business RPCs: `OAuthAuthorizeURL` (requires `code_challenge`), `OAuthCallback`, `OAuthCallbackForm` (the callback of a `form_post` provider; its route is `oauth_form_post`'s), `OAuthExchange`.
- REST (`/auth/oauth`): `GET /{provider}/authorize` (**public**, browser 302 to the provider), `GET /{provider}/callback` (**public, failed_only**, browser 302 to the site-relative `redirect_after` with a single-use login code in the fragment) and `POST /exchange` (**public, failed_only**: code + verifier → session).
- Storage RPCs: `GetProviderByName`, `GetOAuthIdentity`; `CreateOAuthIdentity`, `UpsertOAuthProvider`, `MoveOAuthProviderSecret`, `ClearOAuthProviderLegacySecret`, `CreateOAuthLoginCode`, `ConsumeOAuthLoginCode`.
- Sandbox: a deterministic fake OIDC provider (`sandboxes/fakeidp`).
- Events: none of its own; account creation emits the base `UserCreated`. Env knob: `oauth_redirect_base`.

**Requires:** `email_verification`, needed so the local account's verification can be checked before linking. **Conflicts:** `tenant_scope` (declared on tenant_scope): the create path writes no `tenant_id`. **Combined files:** none.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| `oauth/state-signed-and-provider-bound` | `state` MUST be HMAC-signed with the provider's client secret and bound to the provider name. A tampered state, a wrong key or a state for another provider MUST be refused. | Security | TestOAuthState_SignVerifyRoundTrip, TestOAuthCallback_BadState_Unauthenticated |
| `oauth/state-single-use-and-expiring` | A state MUST expire within minutes and MUST be bound to the PKCE challenge of the browser that started the flow (by its digest, see `oauth/state-hides-the-challenge`). A sign-in MUST complete only in that browser: the callback's login code is single-use, short-lived, and redeemable only with that challenge's verifier (login-CSRF defence). | Security | TestOAuthState_Expires, TestOAuthState_WithoutAChallengeIsRefused, TestOAuthAuthorizeURL_RequiresAPKCEChallenge, TestOAuthExchange_OnlyTheStartingBrowsersVerifierRedeems, TestOAuthExchange_ACodeRedeemsOnce (single use is **unit (mock) only**: a live test races two exchanges of one code and asserts exactly one session) |
| `oauth/state-hides-the-challenge` | The state and the stored login code MUST carry only a digest of the browser's challenge, never the challenge itself, so it cannot be lifted from a URL to start a sign-in bound to it. | Security | TestOAuthAuthorizeURL_TheStateDoesNotRevealTheBrowsersChallenge, TestOAuthCallback_HandsOutACodeNotASession, TestOAuth_TheRawChallengeIsAcceptedNowhere |
| `oauth/callback-carries-no-session` | The callback MUST NOT issue a session or put one in its redirect. It MUST hand out only a login code, stored as its hash. | Security | TestOAuthCallback_HandsOutACodeNotASession |
| `oauth/pkce-towards-provider` | The authorize URL MUST carry the plugin's own S256 challenge, distinct from the browser's, and the token request the matching verifier. | Security | TestOAuthCallback_SendsPKCEToTheProvider |
| `oauth/no-empty-hmac-key` | A provider with no client secret MUST be refused at both authorize and callback. It MUST NOT sign a state with an empty key. | Security | TestOAuthAuthorizeURL_EmptyClientSecret_Rejected, TestOAuthCallback_EmptyClientSecret_Rejected |
| `oauth/disabled-provider-refused` | An unknown or disabled provider MUST be refused. | Security | TestOAuthAuthorizeURL_DisabledProvider |
| `oauth/redirect-after-site-relative` | `redirect_after` MUST be a site-relative path (no scheme, no host, no `//`, no backslash or NUL). It MUST be refused at authorize, and an unsafe value inside a validly signed state MUST be dropped at callback. | Security | TestOAuthAuthorizeURL_RejectsUnsafeRedirect, TestOAuthCallback_DropsUnsafeRedirect |
| `oauth/link-needs-idp-verified-email` | Auto-linking to an existing local account MUST require the provider to assert `email_verified=true`. | Security | TestOAuthCallback_UnverifiedEmail_RefusesLinkToExisting, TestOAuthCallback_VerifiedEmail_LinksToExisting |
| `oauth/link-needs-locally-verified-email` | Auto-linking MUST also require the existing local account to have verified the address (`email_verified_at`). An unverified local account MUST NOT receive a provider identity. | Security | TestOAuthAutoLink_RefusesAnUnverifiedLocalAccount, TestOAuthAutoLink_LinksIntoAVerifiedLocalAccount |
| `oauth/existing-identity-wins` | A known `(provider, subject)` MUST resolve to its linked user regardless of the email currently asserted. | Security | TestOAuthCallback_ExistingIdentity_ReusesUser |
| `oauth/creation-gated` | Creating an account through OAuth MUST pass the same `sign_up_allowlist` and `invite_only` gates as password sign-up, with the invitation token the exchange carries (an open invitation works by token, and its link is spent by that registration). The account MUST be created in the exchange, never in the callback, so the token never travels through a URL or the provider. | Security | TestOAuthCreate_ConsultsTheInviteGate, TestOAuthCallback_CreatesNoAccount, TestOAuthExchange_SignsUpWithTheInvitationItCarries, TestOAuthExchange_RefusesASignUpWithoutAnInvitation, TestOAuthExchange_AnExistingAccountIgnoresTheInvitation; allowlist: none |
| `oauth/refusals-coded` | Every OAuth refusal MUST carry an `ErrorDetail` code: `OAUTH_ACCOUNT_UNVERIFIED`, `OAUTH_EMAIL_UNVERIFIED`, `ACCOUNT_DISABLED`, the sign-up gate's own code, `AUTH_UNAVAILABLE` for an outage, or `OAUTH_FAILED`. A password sign-in to a disabled account MUST still answer `INVALID_CREDENTIALS`. | Security | TestOAuthRefusal_Codes, TestOAuth_ADisabledAccountSaysAccountDisabled, TestOAuthExchange_ABadCodeSaysOAuthFailed, TestSignupInviteGate_ABoundInvitationForAnotherAddress |
| `oauth/unsignable-account-no-session` | A disabled or machine account MUST NOT get a session through the callback or the exchange. | Security | TestOAuth_ADisabledAccountSaysAccountDisabled (disabled; machine: none) |
| `oauth/client-secret-encrypted` | The client secret MUST be stored encrypted (CRYPTED_SECRET). A legacy plaintext value MUST be moved on first read and cleared once `secret` is set, and MUST NOT override a newer `secret`. | Security | TestOAuthProvider_SecretFromEncryptedColumnOrMovedFromLegacy (handler logic); at-rest encryption: none (needs a raw-column read in a live test) |
