| `oauth/id-token-validated` | Claims taken from an `id_token` MUST be checked for `aud` (= client_id), `azp` (= client_id when present), `exp`, and the nonce of this sign-in. | Security | TestCheckIDTokenClaims, TestOAuthCallback_IDTokenPath. `iss` and the signature are not checked (see gaps). |
| `oauth/second-factor-policy` | **Decided.** An OAuth sign-in MUST NOT ask for this plugin's second factor, also for an account with one confirmed: the provider already verified the identity, and a second factor at the provider and again here is not wanted. It MUST NOT make the device trusted either, so a later password sign-in under `new_devices` still asks for the factor. | Security | TestOAuthExchange_DoesNotTrustTheDevice, TestIssueSession_BindsAndTrustsOnlyWithACutoff (the absence of a challenge is the exchange's design: no test asserts it). |
| `oauth/opaque-failures` | Every callback failure except the unsignable-account refusal MUST be an opaque Unauthenticated. | Security | TestOAuthCallback_BadState_Unauthenticated, TestOAuthCallback_UnverifiedEmail_RefusesLinkToExisting |
| `oauth/created-account-is-complete` | An OAuth-created account MUST get its default/first-user roles, and `email_verified` when the provider asserted it, in the same transaction as the user insert. | Consistency & concurrency | TestOAuthCallback_UserinfoPath_CreatesUser, TestOAuthCallback_AProviderVerifiedAddressIsRecorded (transaction rollback is **unit (mock) only**) |
| `oauth/user-and-identity-atomic` | The created user and its `OAuthIdentity` MUST commit together. | Consistency & concurrency | TestOAuthExchange_SignsUpWithTheInvitationItCarries (both written in the exchange's unit of work; rollback is **unit (mock) only**) |
| `oauth/one-link-per-subject` | `(provider_id, external_id)` MUST be unique. Concurrent first sign-ups for one subject (two login codes redeemed by two exchanges) MUST yield one account and one link. The exchange resolves the identity again before creating, so a code redeemed after another one made the account signs in to it. | Consistency & concurrency | TestOAuthExchange_AnAccountMadeSinceTheCallbackIsUsed (sequential). Unique index for a true race; a live test needs parallel exchanges. |
| `oauth/email-canonical` | The asserted email MUST be trimmed and lower-cased before lookup and create. | Consistency & concurrency | TestOAuthCallback_UserinfoPath_CreatesUser (partial) |
| `oauth/provider-mapping` | Subject and email MUST be read via `subject_path`/`email_path` (defaults `sub`/`email`). Numeric ids MUST be coerced to strings. A missing subject or email MUST be refused. | Failure & configuration | TestOAuthCallback_UserinfoPath_CreatesUser, TestOAuthCallback_IDTokenPath, TestOAuthCallback_GitHubNumericId |
| `oauth/outbound-bounded` | Token and userinfo calls MUST time out (15 s) and MUST read at most 1 MiB. | Performance | `TestOAuthHTTPClient_TimesOutAfterFifteenSeconds`, `TestOAuthOutbound_ReadsAtMostOneMiB` (token and userinfo endpoints; exactly 1 MiB parses, 1 MiB + 1 is cut and refused) |
| `oauth/indexed-lookups` | `OAuthProvider.name` (unique), `OAuthIdentity(provider_id, external_id)` (unique) and `OAuthIdentity.user_id` MUST be indexed. | Performance | none (schema) |
| `oauth/redirect-base-configured` | The callback URL sent to the provider MUST come from configuration, not from a request header. | Failure & configuration | TestOAuthAuthorizeURL_BuildsRedirect (configured path only); not enforced when `oauth_redirect_base` is empty |
| `oauth/minted-client-secret` | A provider with `team_id` and `key_id` (Sign in with Apple) MUST send the token endpoint a client secret minted per exchange: an ES256 JWT (`r‖s` signature) with header `kid` = key_id, claims `iss` = team_id, `sub` = client_id, `aud` = the token endpoint's origin, `iat` now and `exp` five minutes later. The stored key MUST NOT be sent. Only one of the two ids, a `secret` that is not a PKCS#8 P-256 PEM, or a token URL without an origin MUST be refused before anything is sent. A provider with neither id MUST send `secret` as it is. | Security | `TestOAuthCallbackForm_SignInWithApple` (verifies the secret with the key's public half), `TestOAuthProvider_AppleMisconfigurations`, `TestOAuthProvider_PlainSecretUnchanged` |
| `oauth/state-key-survives-minting` | The state, the PKCE verifier towards the provider and the OIDC nonce MUST stay keyed by `secret` (for a minting provider the key, which does not change between mints), never by a minted client secret. | Security | `TestOAuthCallbackForm_SignInWithApple` (a state signed with the key completes) |
| `oauth/response-mode` | `response_mode` MUST be empty (the query string) or `form_post`, which the authorize URL then asks for. A `form_post` provider MUST be refused at authorize where `oauth_form_post` is off, and only a `form_post` provider MUST be accepted on `OAuthCallbackForm`, before its code is exchanged. | Failure & configuration | `TestOAuthAuthorizeURL_FormPostNeedsItsRoute`, `TestOAuthCallbackForm_RefusesAQueryProvider`, `TestOAuthProvider_AppleMisconfigurations`, `TestOAuthProvider_PlainSecretUnchanged` |
| `oauth/provider-secret-encrypted` | A provider configured through `UpsertOAuthProvider` MUST store its secret only in the encrypted `secret` column, on creation and on every rewrite, and MUST leave no plaintext `client_secret`. The same request twice MUST leave one row. | Security | `TestUpsertOAuthProvider_WritesTheSecretEncryptedOnBothBranches` (the declaration). **Unit (mock) only:** a live test would upsert twice and read the row back: one row, ciphertext in `secret`, `client_secret` NULL, the secret decrypting to the second value. |

**Gaps / suspected defects**

1. **Fixed: login CSRF.** See verified finding 1 and the `oauth/state-*`, `oauth/callback-carries-no-session` and `oauth/pkce-towards-provider` invariants.
2. The `id_token` signature and `iss` are not checked. `aud`, `exp` and the nonce are. The token comes straight from the provider's token endpoint over TLS, and the nonce ties it to this sign-in. Checking the signature needs the provider's JWKS, and checking `iss` needs an issuer column on `OAuthProvider`.
3. **Decided: the callback skips the second factor** (`oauth/second-factor-policy`, also the README). Combined with auto-linking, an account that was password + TOTP becomes reachable with the provider login alone once an identity is linked; the provider's own sign-in, and its own second factor, is what protects it. There is no per-account or per-provider switch to require the plugin's factor as well. The exchange does not trust the device, so this cannot spill over into password sign-ins.
4. Fixed: the user (with roles) used to be committed in its own transaction, with `CreateOAuthIdentity` afterwards outside it. A failed link insert then left an account for the address with no identity, and a retry with `email_verified=false` hit `errOAuthEmailUnverified`, locking the person out of the account just created for them. The exchange now writes both, the spent invitation included, in one unit of work, so a failed link insert leaves no account.
5. **Fixed: a deleted account's identity blocked its provider subject for good.** `DeleteUser` is gone; erasure (`EraseUsers`) replaces every identity's `external_id` with the row's own id and blanks its email (`EraseOAuthData`), so the subject resolves to nobody and the person can sign up again through the provider. An identity whose user row is missing (from before) is still refused (`TestOAuthCallback_AnIdentityWhoseAccountWasDeletedCannotSignIn`).
6. **Decided: `redirect_uri` from the request host when `oauth_redirect_base` is empty.** A deployment that tells its tenants apart by host needs the callback on each tenant's host, so the boot does not refuse it. The defence is the provider's exact match against its registered redirect URIs: a forged `Host` yields a `redirect_uri` the provider refuses. A deployment on one public host sets the knob.
7. Fixed: the manifest text described the client secret as PASSWORD-typed. It is CRYPTED_SECRET (`secret`).
8. **Fixed: linking an identity is an event.** `CreateOAuthIdentity` emits `OAuthIdentityLinked` (identity, account, provider, the exchanging device), at an account's creation through the provider and when an identity is linked to an existing account — linking grants a new way in.

---

### cli_login

**Purpose.** Makes the deployment act as an OAuth authorization server for its own clients: the authorization-code flow with mandatory PKCE (S256). Loopback redirects (RFC 8252) need no registration. Any other redirect must be an exact `(client_id, redirect_uri)` row in the `AuthClient` registry, whose clients are confidential (a secret is required at the token endpoint).

**Adds.**
- Tables: `AuthClient` (unique `(client_id, redirect_uri)`, revoked rows included — a partial index is refused on MySQL, indexed `client_id`, `client_secret` PASSWORD = argon2 hash, per-row `enabled`, `revoked_at`) and `CliAuthCode` (`code_hash` unique SHA-256, `code_challenge`, `redirect_uri`, `client_id`, `expires_at` = now + 60 s, `consumed_at`, `session_expires_at` = the approving session's expiry, NULL when it never expires; `approved_by_device_id` = the Device row the approving session is bound to, which CliToken records on the CLI session as `UserToken.approved_by_device_id` — `device/cli-own-device`).
- Business RPCs: `CliAuthorize`, `CliToken`, `RegisterClient`, `ListClients`, `SetClientEnabled`, `DeleteClient` (revokes: stamps `revoked_at`; `DELETE /auth/clients/{id}`), `RotateClientSecret` (published as `POST /auth/clients/{id}/rotate-secret` only with the opt-in feature `client_secret_rotation`).
- REST: `POST /auth/cli/authorize` (bearer), `POST /auth/cli/token` (**public, failed_only**), and `POST|GET /auth/clients`, `PATCH|DELETE /auth/clients/{id}` (bearer; authorization is left to the project's endpoint ACL).
- Storage RPCs: `GetRegisteredRedirect`, `ListAuthClients`, `GetAuthClientSecret`, `GetLiveSessionExpiry`, `GetCliApproverRevocation`; `GetAuthClientRevocation`; `RegisterAuthClient`, `SetAuthClientEnabled`, `RevokeAuthClient`, `RotateAuthClientSecret`, `CreateCliAuthCode`, `ConsumeCliAuthCode`.
- Events: none. Env knobs: none (code TTL fixed at 60 s; the issued token uses `session_token_ttl_seconds`, capped at the approving session's expiry).

**Requires:** none declared (it uses the bearer principal from the gateway envelope). **Conflicts:** none. **Combined files:** none.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cli_login/authorize-needs-principal` | CliAuthorize MUST refuse a call with no resolved principal, or one that did not authenticate with a live session of its own, and MUST NOT mint a code. | Security | TestCliAuthorize_Rejections, TestCliAuthorize_AnAPITokenCallerIsRefused, TestCliAuthorize_RefusesAnApproverThatIsNotALiveSessionOfTheCaller, TestCliAuthorize_RefusesWhenTheSessionReadFails, TestCliAuthorize_AnEmptySessionAnswerIsNotANonExpiringSession |
| `cli_login/pkce-mandatory` | CliAuthorize MUST refuse an empty `code_challenge`. CliToken MUST refuse a verifier shorter than 43 characters without consuming the code, and MUST issue only when BASE64URL(SHA256(verifier)) equals the stored challenge (constant-time). | Security | TestCliAuthorize_Rejections, TestCliToken_Rejections, TestVerifyPKCES256 |
| `cli_login/redirect-allowlist` | A code MUST be minted only for a loopback `http://127.0.0.1|[::1]|localhost` redirect, or for an `https` redirect registered *exactly* (whole string) and enabled under the presented `client_id`. Prefix, host-only and plaintext matches MUST be refused. | Security | TestIsLoopbackRedirect, TestCliAuthorize_LoopbackStillNeedsNoRegistration, TestCliAuthorize_ARegisteredCallbackIsAccepted, TestCliAuthorize_RefusesEverythingNotRegistered, TestCliAuthorize_RefusesAPlaintextRegisteredCallback, TestCliAuthorize_RefusesARowThatIsNotTheOneItAskedFor, TestGetRegisteredRedirect_MatchesTheWholeStringAndNothingLess |
| `cli_login/registry-lookup-fails-closed` | A registry read error MUST refuse the redirect. | Security | TestCliAuthorize_AFailedRegistryLookupRefuses |
| `cli_login/code-hashed` | Only the SHA-256 of the code MUST be stored. | Security | TestCliLogin_EndToEnd |
| `cli_login/code-single-use-short-lived` | A code MUST be redeemable once, within 60 s, by one conditional UPDATE (`consumed_at IS NULL AND expires_at > NOW()`). A wrong verifier MUST still burn the code. | Security | TestCliLogin_EndToEnd (replay), TestCliToken_Rejections (expired, wrong verifier burns); the concurrent double redemption is **unit (mock) only**. A live test must race two CliToken calls with one code and show one token. |
| `cli_login/redirect-bound-at-exchange` | For a registered (non-loopback) code, CliToken MUST require the same `redirect_uri` the code was issued for. | Security | TestCliToken_RefusesAnExchangeNamingADifferentRedirect, TestCliToken_AcceptsTheRedirectTheCodeWasIssuedFor |
| `cli_login/confidential-client-auth` | A code minted for a registered client MUST be redeemed only with that client's secret. The client MUST still be enabled at exchange time. Unknown client, disabled client and wrong or absent secret MUST be indistinguishable. | Security | TestCliToken_RegisteredClientMustPresentItsSecret, TestCliToken_ClientDisabledAfterAuthorizeCannotRedeem, TestCliToken_ClientAuthFailuresAreIndistinguishable |
| `cli_login/loopback-public-client` | A loopback code MUST NOT be bound to a client or require a secret, even if a `client_id` is sent. | Security | TestCliToken_LoopbackStaysAPublicClient |
| `cli_login/client-secret-hashed-once` | A client secret MUST be random, MUST be stored only as a password hash, and MUST be returned exactly once at registration. | Security | TestRegisterClient_IssuesASecretAndStoresOnlyItsHash |
| `cli_login/registered-redirect-shape` | Registration MUST refuse a non-absolute, non-`https` or fragment-bearing redirect. | Security | TestRegisterClient_RefusesARedirectThatCannotWork |
| `cli_login/state-echoed` | `state` MUST be returned unchanged. | Security | TestCliAuthorize_EchoesStateUnchanged |
| `cli_login/registry-admin-only` | Registering, listing, disabling and revoking clients MUST be restricted to operators. | Security | none. The plugin checks only authentication; the gate is the project's ACL. |
| `cli_login/client-secret-rotation` | A live registration's secret MUST be replaceable without changing its client_id (`RotateClientSecret`, `POST /auth/clients/{id}/rotate-secret`): a new random secret, stored only hashed, returned once; the old one stops verifying. A revoked or unknown registration MUST be refused (NotFound) and keep its secret. Same gating as `RegisterClient` (a session caller; the project's ACL). | Security | `TestRotateClientSecret_KeepsTheClientIDAndRefusesARevokedOne`, `TestAuthClientStatements` |
| `cli_login/client-revocation-is-a-state` | Revoking a registration MUST stamp `AuthClient.revoked_at` and keep the row. A revoked registration MUST be refused by every resolver exactly as an unregistered one (`GetRegisteredRedirect` and `GetAuthClientSecret` read live rows only), MUST NOT be restorable by `SetAuthClientEnabled`, and MUST NOT be revived. Its `(client_id, redirect_uri)` stays taken (client_id is the operator's choice and the unique index is full): registering the pair again MUST be refused with `FailedPrecondition` / `CLIENT_REVOKED` naming the remedy (a new client_id), while a live duplicate keeps `AlreadyExists`. Revoking it again is done, not an error. | Security | `TestRevokeClient_KeepsTheRowAndRefusesItLikeAnUnregisteredOne`, `TestRegisterClient_ARevokedPairIsRefusedClearly`, `TestAuthClientStatements` (the full index). |
| `cli_login/revoked-approver-mints-nothing` | CliToken MUST refuse a code whose approving device (`approved_by_device_id`) was revoked after it approved the code (`GetCliApproverRevocation`), with the opaque refusal of every other code failure, recorded as `REFUSED` (not `DEVICE_REVOKED`: that outcome names the key signing the request, and the CLI's own key is not revoked). A failed read refuses too (an outage as one); a code with no approver has nothing to check. | Security | `TestCliToken_RefusesACodeWhoseApproverWasRevoked` (revoked, outage, no row), `TestCliLogin_RecordsTheApproversDeviceAndBindsTheCLIsOwn` (a live approver mints), `TestPublicComputerAndApproverStatements` |
| `cli_login/unsignable-account-no-token` | A disabled or machine account MUST NOT redeem a code. | Security | `TestCliToken_AnUnsignableAccountGetsNoSession` (disabled and machine account, changed between CliAuthorize and CliToken) |
| `cli_login/no-lifetime-extension` | Redeeming a code MUST NOT produce a credential that outlives, or is broader than, the session that approved it. The minted session expires at min(now + `session_token_ttl_seconds`, the approving session's `expires_at`), with no floor; an approving session that has expired by the exchange mints nothing. Only a session may approve (an API token is refused). | Security | TestCliToken_InheritsTheApprovingSessionsEarlierExpiry, TestCliToken_NeverOutlivesTheNormalTTL, TestCliToken_ANonExpiringApproverGivesTheNormalTTL, TestCliToken_AChainOfLoginsCannotExtendTheFirstSession, TestCliToken_RefusesWhenTheApprovingSessionExpiredMeanwhile, TestCliSessionExpiry, TestCliAuthorize_AnAPITokenCallerIsRefused. **Unit (mock) only:** the liveness and ownership filters of `GetLiveSessionExpiry` are in its SQL. |
| `cli_login/opaque-token-errors` | Refusals MUST be opaque Unauthenticated. Storage failures MUST surface as errors, not as a bad code. | Failure & configuration | TestCliToken_Rejections, TestCliLogin_StorageErrorsPropagate |
| `cli_login/one-registration-per-pair` | `(client_id, redirect_uri)` MUST be unique, and the secret lookup MUST read exactly that row. | Consistency & concurrency | none (schema) |
| `cli_login/indexed-lookups` | `CliAuthCode.code_hash` (unique), `CliAuthCode.user_id`, `AuthClient(client_id, redirect_uri)` (unique) and `AuthClient.client_id` MUST be indexed. | Performance | none (schema) |
| `cli_login/expired-codes-purged` | Expired or consumed codes MUST be purged. | Performance | none. Not enforced. |

**Gaps / suspected defects**

1. **Fixed: a credential could renew itself.** CliAuthorize accepted any resolved principal, and CliToken minted a fresh session with the full `session_token_ttl_seconds`. Since rc.25 only a session may approve; the minted session now inherits the approving session's expiry (`cli_login/no-lifetime-extension`).
2. `cli_login.go:351–425`: **The registry endpoints only check authentication.** Any signed-in account can register a callback (and receive its secret), list every registration, or disable or delete other clients, unless the project's ACL forbids it. A project with the endpoint ACL axis off has no gate at all. The README documents this, but the plugin enforces nothing.
3. **Fixed: `code_challenge` was checked only for being non-empty.** `CliAuthorize` (and the OAuth server's authorization) accepts exactly an S256 challenge — 43 characters of canonical unpadded base64url decoding to a SHA-256 digest (`isPKCEChallenge`) — and refuses anything else up front instead of minting a code that can never be redeemed. S256 stays implied: the request has no `code_challenge_method`.
4. Mutation proto, `RegisterAuthClient`: the comment says a repeat registration is a no-op, but the unique index makes it an AlreadyExists error. Fixed: a lost secret is replaced with `RotateClientSecret`, keeping the client_id; revoking is for good.
5. No retention: `CliAuthCode` rows are never deleted. No events: there is no audit trail for client registration or CLI token issuance.

---

### oauth_server

**Purpose.** An OAuth 2.1 authorization server (the MCP authorization specification, 2025-11-25) for MCP clients — Claude, Cursor, ChatGPT — and for w17ctl's browser login. The OAuth wire (RFC 8414 metadata, `/oauth/authorize`, `/oauth/token`, `/oauth/register`, `/oauth/revoke`) is the SDK's `lib/oauthas`, mounted by the generated REST gateway at the root of the issuer; this feature is every decision behind it.

**Adds.**
- Tables: `OAuthClient` (public clients: `client_id` and its unique hash, kind DCR / CIMD, the name and links the client states, `redirect_uris` one per line, `metadata_fetched_at`, `disabled_at`), `OAuthAuthorization` (one request from the authorization endpoint to the decision and the code: the hashed request id, client, redirect, state, PKCE challenge, scope, resource, issuer, `expires_at` = now + 10 min, the decision with who / which org / which device / the approving session's expiry, the hashed code with `code_expires_at` = 60 s and `code_consumed_at`), `OAuthGrant` (a connected app: user, org, client, resource, approving device, `expires_at` = oauth_grant_max_days, `last_used_at`, `revoked_at` / `revoked_by` / `revoke_reason`), `OAuthRefreshToken` (hashed, per grant, `expires_at`, `used_at`). `UserToken` gains `oauth_grant_id`, `resource`, `oauth_org_id` (field-gated). Enum values: `TokenType.MCP`, `RevokeReason.REFRESH_REUSED` / `GRANT_REVOKED` / `CODE_REPLAYED`, `SignInMethod.AUTHORIZED_APP`. `OAuthAuthorization.session_token_hash` links a w17ctl login's code to the session it minted.
- Business RPCs: `GetOAuthServerSettings`, `RegisterOAuthClient`, `BeginOAuthAuthorization`, `ExchangeOAuthToken`, `RevokeOAuthToken` (the wire's, unauthenticated; a refusal travels as `oauth_error` in the response, a failure as a gRPC error); `GetOAuthAuthorization`, `ApproveOAuthAuthorization`, `DenyOAuthAuthorization` (the consent page's), `ListMyOAuthGrants`, `RevokeMyOAuthGrant` (connected apps) — the last five need a session.
- REST: `GET /auth/oauth/authorizations/{request_id}`, `POST …/approve`, `POST …/deny`, `GET /auth/oauth/connected-apps`, `DELETE /auth/oauth/connected-apps/{grant_id}`.
- Events: `OAuthGrantApproved`. Settings: `oauth_access_ttl_seconds` (3600), `oauth_refresh_ttl_days` (30), `oauth_grant_max_days` (365), `oauth_dynamic_registration` (true), `oauth_redirect_hosts` (empty = any https host), `oauth_resources` (empty = any https resource), `oauth_code_redirect` (empty = no sign-in with a code; set, the gateway's code page `<issuer>/oauth/code`, where w17ctl on another machine than the browser has the code shown to type in).
- Eraser: the user's grants end. Revocations: `RevokeDeviceTokens` ends the access tokens a device approved; every revocation that reaches a grant's newest access token ends the grant at its next refresh.

**Requires:** `api_token` (the realm-aware resolver and `token_type`), `authenticate_turnkey`. **Combined files:** `oauth_server_org.go` with `org_membership`: the consent page offers the user's organizations, the grant and its tokens carry the chosen one, and `Authenticate` scopes an access token into it (a `W17-Org` naming another is refused).

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| `oauth_server/client-and-redirect-first` | The authorization endpoint MUST check the client and its redirect before anything else, and a refusal about either MUST be shown, never redirected (RFC 6749 §4.1.2.1). | Security | `TestBeginOAuthAuthorization_Refusals` |
| `oauth_server/exact-redirect` | A redirect MUST equal one the client registered as a whole string, except a loopback redirect's port (RFC 8252 §7.3). Registration MUST accept https (on `oauth_redirect_hosts` when set), loopback http and a native app's private-use scheme (RFC 8252 §7.1, e.g. `cursor://…`, named on the consent page), and MUST refuse plain http elsewhere, a fragment, credentials and the schemes a browser handles itself (`javascript:`, `data:`, `file:` …). | Security | `TestRedirectRegistered`, `TestRegisterOAuthClient_Rules` |
| `oauth_server/pkce-s256` | PKCE S256 with a 43-character challenge MUST be required; plain MUST be refused. | Security | `TestBeginOAuthAuthorization_Refusals` |
| `oauth_server/one-resource` | An MCP grant MUST name exactly one https resource (http only on loopback, no fragment), within `oauth_resources` when set, compared normalised (scheme and host case, default port, trailing slash) here and by `Authenticate`. No resource is a session grant, allowed only to a client registered as w17ctl (`software_id`) on a loopback redirect; any other client is `invalid_target`. | Security | `TestBeginOAuthAuthorization_Refusals` |
| `oauth_server/consent-is-a-session` | Reading, approving and denying a request MUST need a session (an API or MCP token MUST NOT approve), and a request MUST be decided once, while live. | Security | `TestConsent_ShowsTheRequestAndNeedsASession`, `TestConsent_DecidedOnce` |
| `oauth_server/code-single-use` | A code MUST be spent before it is checked (a wrong verifier burns it), redeemable once within 60 s (5 min for the code page), by its client, at its redirect, for its resource; presented again after it was redeemed, it MUST end what it bought — the MCP grant with its tokens, or the w17ctl session (`CODE_REPLAYED`) (OAuth 2.1 §4.1.3). The spend and the issue are ONE transaction (a refusal commits it, a store failure rolls the spend back), so a replay waits for the redemption and finds what it issued. | Security | `TestOAuthServer_WrongVerifierBurnsTheCode`, `TestOAuthServer_CodeIsBoundToClientRedirectAndResource`, `TestOAuthServer_MCPFlow`, `TestOAuthServer_ReplayedSessionCodeEndsTheSession`; the race itself **live only** (the row lock is the database's) |
| `oauth_server/mcp-token-shape` | An MCP grant's access token MUST be `mcp_only`, of type MCP, for the grant's resource and organization, bound to no device, with the approving device recorded; its permissions are the user's realm-`mcp` roles. | Security | `TestOAuthServer_MCPFlow` |
| `oauth_server/audience` | `Authenticate` MUST accept an MCP token only on the MCP surface naming its resource (`x-w17-mcp-resource`), refuse it on another resource and on a surface naming none, and refuse an `mcp_only` token issued for no resource on a surface that names one. | Security | `TestCheckMCPToken_Resource` |
| `oauth_server/refresh-rotation` | A refresh MUST spend the refresh token and issue a new pair in ONE transaction that holds the live grant row first: a store failure spends nothing (the retry is no reuse), and a revocation racing it either waits and ends what it minted or wins and it mints nothing. A spent token presented again MUST end the grant and its access tokens (`REFRESH_REUSED`); only a refusal ends a grant, never a failed read. | Security | `TestOAuthServer_RefreshRotatesAndReuseEndsTheGrant`, `TestOAuthServer_RefreshByAnotherClient`, `TestOAuthServer_RefreshFailureIsNotAReuse`, `TestOAuthServer_RefreshOfARevokedGrantMintsNothing`; the race itself **live only** (the row lock is the database's) |
| `oauth_server/revocations-reach-grants` | Revoking the approving device, every token of the user, or disabling the account MUST end the grant at its next refresh; erasure MUST end every grant. | Security | `TestOAuthServer_RevocationsReachTheGrant`, `TestEraseUsers_PseudonymisesAndKeepsEveryRow` |
| `oauth_server/session-grant-bound` | A session grant MUST be issued only to a token request with a DPoP proof, bound to its key, no later than the approving session. A valid proof whose key cannot sign in for the account (a revoked device, a program's key) is refused with the extension error `w17_device_key_refused` — the one answer on which w17ctl discards its key; the gateway's own proof checks stay `invalid_dpop_proof`, and its per-address mint limit is `slow_down` (429). | Security | **live only** (w17ctl login, platform `w17ctl/cmd/login` tests on a fake server) |
| `oauth_server/cimd-safe-fetch` | A metadata document MUST be fetched over https from a public address checked at the dial (no redirects, 5 s, 16 KiB), MUST name itself, and is read again after a day; a known client keeps working on its last document for a week while its host is down. | Security | `TestResolveOAuthClient_CIMD`, `lib/safefetch` tests |
| `oauth_server/revocation-endpoint` | RFC 7009: an access or refresh token MUST end its grant; an unknown token, or another client's, MUST answer the same success. | Security | `TestRevokeOAuthToken` |
| `oauth_server/code-page-session` | A session grant MUST also be possible with `oauth_code_redirect` as the redirect, for w17ctl only, and only when it is set (unset, w17ctl MUST be told the console does not offer it); its code MUST live 5 min, and the consent page MUST say the answer is a code to type into w17ctl. The page MUST be accepted at registration whatever `oauth_redirect_hosts` lists. | Security | `TestOAuthServer_SessionThroughTheCodePage`, `TestOAuthServer_CodePageRefused` |
| `oauth_server/boot-checks` | `oauth_redirect_hosts` and `oauth_resources` entries that are not a host / an absolute URL, and an `oauth_code_redirect` that is not an https URL without query or fragment, MUST fail the boot. | Failure & configuration | `TestCheckOAuthServerConfig`, `TestCheckOAuthCodeRedirect` |

A user with organizations names one for an MCP grant; a user with none grants with their realm-wide roles. The wire caps authorization requests per address (30/min), registrations (20/h) and token requests (60/min).

**Gaps.** No admin pages for clients and grants yet (an MCP access token is listed on the Sessions page under its client's name). DPoP-bound MCP tokens are not issued (no MCP client signs proofs).

---

### api_token

**Purpose.** Adds API/MCP credentials that sit alongside browser session bearers. Each token belongs to a user. An API token resolves permissions only from the holder's API-realm roles, and an optional per-token permission subset can narrow that further.

**Adds**
- Tables and columns: `UserToken.token_type` (SESSION=1 default, API=2), `UserToken.name`, `UserToken.last_used_at`, `Role.token_type` (realm), table `TokenPermission` (unique `(token_id, permission_id)`).
- Queries: `GetTokenWithType`, `GetUserRolePermissionsByRealm`, `GetTokenPermissionSubset`, `ListApiTokensByUser` (with each token's `device_thumbprint` and `device_revoked_at`), `GetUserDeviceByThumbprint` (the creating caller's Device row).
- Mutations: `IssueApiToken`, `AddTokenPermission`, `MarkApiTokenRevoked` (was `DeleteApiToken`), `RevokeApprovedApiTokens` (the API tokens one revoked device created; installed as `revokeApprovedAPITokens` for `RevokeDevice` and the admin `RevokeDevices`). `DeleteTokenPermissions` is gone: a revoked token's subset rows stay.
- Business RPCs: `CreateApiToken` (`public_key` required: the program's public JWK; the response's `device_id` is its thumbprint), `ListApiTokens`, `RevokeApiToken`. `IssueApiTokenReq.device_id` (NULL only for a machine account's bearer token, `device/bot-bearer`; the handler refuses any other mint without it) and `approved_by_device_id`.
- REST: `POST /auth/tokens`, `GET /auth/tokens`, `DELETE /auth/tokens/{token_id}`. All three need a bearer token. None is `public` or `failed_only`.
- The resolver is replaced: `resolvePrincipal` becomes realm-aware (installed by `init()` in `apitoken.go`).
- Events: `ApiTokenCreated` (emitted by `IssueApiToken`: token, owner, `created_at`, name, `device_id` = the creating request's key thumbprint, `key_thumbprint` = the program's key, `created_by`; never a secret). `ApiTokenRevoked` (emitted by `MarkApiTokenRevoked` when a person revokes one: token, owner, `device_id` = the revoking device).
- Env: `api_token_default_ttl_hours` (default 2160 = 90 days; 0 = never expires; above 100 years the plugin refuses to boot). `api_token_max_ttl_hours` (default 0 = no cap): the longest a person's token may live — a later requested expiry is refused (`TOKEN_EXPIRY_TOO_LATE`), and a token minted without one takes the default, never more than the cap and never "never". Machine accounts' tokens are not affected. `api_token_step_up_seconds` (default 0): see `step_up/api-token-asks-for-it`.

**Requires / conflicts / combined files.** Requires `rbac` (`GetUserRolePermissionsByRealm` reads its tables) and declares no `conflicts_with` (an earlier conflict with `org_membership` was lifted once both narrowing axes began intersecting on role IDs). Go file: `handlers/apitoken.go`. `service_account` requires this feature and reuses its minting path.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| api_token/realm-separation | A SESSION token MUST resolve only roles whose `token_type` is SESSION, and an API token only roles whose `token_type` is API. | Security | `TestResolvePrincipalRealmAware_Session_NoSubsetLookup`. Unit (mock) only: the realm filter is in the SQL of `GetUserRolePermissionsByRealm`. A live test must show that a user holding only an API-realm role gets an empty permission set with a session token, and the reverse. |
| api_token/subset-narrow-only | An API token's effective permissions MUST be the intersection of its holder's API-realm grants with its subset. A subset entry the holder does not hold MUST NOT grant anything. | Security | `TestResolvePrincipalRealmAware_API_Subset_Intersects`, `TestIntersectInt32` |
| api_token/wildcard-narrowed-by-subset | A wildcard (`all_permissions`) API-realm role MUST become exactly the subset ∩ the deployed lock for a narrowed token, and MUST NOT expand again after the organization axis runs. | Security | `TestResolvePrincipalRealmAware_API_Wildcard_NarrowedBySubset`, `TestResolvePrincipalRealmAware_Wildcard_GrantsAll` |
| api_token/empty-subset-is-holder-ceiling | An API token with no subset rows MUST resolve to exactly its holder's API-realm grants, never more. | Security | `TestResolvePrincipalRealmAware_API_EmptySubset_NoNarrowing` |
| api_token/derived-token-not-wider | A token minted by a caller who authenticated with an API token MUST NOT carry permissions that the calling token does not carry. | Security | `TestCreateApiToken_AnAPITokenCallerIsRefused`, `TestCliAuthorize_AnAPITokenCallerIsRefused` (an API token mints nothing). |
| api_token/not-a-session | An API token MUST NOT appear as `AuthResp.session_token_id`, so `Logout` cannot revoke it and refuses an API-token bearer. | Security | `TestResolvePrincipalRealmAware_ApiTokenHasNoSessionID`, `TestResolvePrincipalRealmAware_ReturnsTokenID`, `TestLogout_NoSessionToken_Refused` |
| api_token/secret-once-hash-only | The plaintext token MUST be returned exactly once, at mint. Only its sha256 MUST be stored, and no list MUST ever return it. | Security | `TestCreateApiToken_StampsAPIRealm_AndSubset`, `TestListApiTokens_ReturnsCallersTokens` (the list projection lacking the secret is a schema property: `ListApiTokensByUser` selects no secret column) |
| api_token/expiry-cap | With `api_token_max_ttl_hours` > 0, every token a person creates MUST expire no later than the cap: a later requested expiry MUST be refused, and a token minted without one MUST take the default capped (a default of "never" included). 0 MUST keep the behaviour before the setting. A negative cap MUST fail the boot. | Security | `TestPersonTokenExpiry_Cap`, `TestCheckAPITokenConfig` |
| api_token/owner-only-management | `ListApiTokens` and `RevokeApiToken` MUST act only on the caller's own API-type tokens. A revoke MUST NOT touch another user's token or any session token; it revokes (reason API_TOKEN_REVOKED, the owner as `revoked_by`) and deletes nothing, the subset rows included. The list shows revoked tokens as revoked. | Security | `TestRevokeApiToken_RevokesTheCallersApiToken`, `TestRevokeApiToken_NothingMatchedIsDone`, `TestRevokeApiToken_RevokesAndKeepsTheRow`. Unit (mock) only: ownership sits in the `MarkApiTokenRevoked` WHERE clause. A live test must revoke user B's token ID as user A and show B's token still authenticates. |
| api_token/expired-token-refused | A token whose `expires_at` has passed MUST fail authentication — with Unauthenticated `SESSION_EXPIRED` for the holder of its key (`base/expired-token-says-so`); the API-token sentence names creating a new token. | Security | `TestAuthenticate_ExpiredApiTokenSaysSo`. The `expires_at IS NULL OR expires_at > NOW()` predicate in `GetTokenWithType` is never run by a test. |
| api_token/last-used-throttled | A token that authenticates MUST have `last_used_at` written at most once per minute: skipped in Go when the lookup's value is inside the window, and guarded in `TouchTokenLastUsed`'s WHERE (`last_used_at IS NULL OR last_used_at < :cutoff`) so concurrent requests write once. The stamp (`:used_at`) and the cutoff both come from the application clock, never mixed with database `NOW()`, so clock skew between app and database cannot shrink the window to nothing; skew between app instances shifts it by at most that skew. A refused request MUST write nothing, and a failed write MUST NOT fail the authentication. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestAuthenticate_TouchesLastUsedAtThrottled`, `TestAuthenticate_FailedTouchDoesNotFailAuth`, `TestAuthenticate_RefusedTokenIsNotTouched`, `TestSessionExpiryStatements`. **Unit (mock) only:** the concurrent guard is SQL. |
| api_token/expiry-future-and-valid | A caller-supplied `expires_at` MUST be a valid timestamp in the future, or the call MUST fail with InvalidArgument (`TOKEN_EXPIRY_IN_PAST` / `TOKEN_EXPIRY_INVALID`). | Security | `TestAPITokenExpiry`, `TestAPITokenExpiry_OutOfRange` |
| api_token/default-expiry | A token minted without `expires_at` MUST expire after `api_token_default_ttl_hours`. 0 MUST mean "never expires". | Security | `TestCreateApiToken_DefaultExpiryReachesTheTokenAndTheCaller`, `TestAPITokenExpiry` |
| api_token/mint-atomic | A token and its subset rows MUST commit together. A failed subset write MUST NOT leave a live token behind, because an empty subset reads as "no narrowing". | Consistency & concurrency | `TestCreateApiToken_NarrowingFails_RollsBackTheLiveToken`, `TestCreateApiToken_BeginRefused_MintsNothing`, `TestCreateApiToken_Success_CommitsOnTheHandlersConnection`. Unit (fake distx) only, and only when `DistTx` is wired (gap 3). |
| api_token/revoke-idempotent | Revoking an absent or already-revoked token MUST succeed; any other failure MUST fail the call. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestRevokeApiToken_NothingMatchedIsDone` (an absent or revoked token matches no row and is done; an outage still fails) |
| api_token/revoke-immediate | Once `RevokeApiToken` returns, the next `Authenticate` with that token MUST fail. | Consistency & concurrency | none (needs a live test: revoke, then authenticate) |
| api_token/indexed-lookups | Token lookup MUST use the unique `token_hash` index, the subset lookup the `token_id` index, and the list the `user_id` index. | Performance | none (declared in `proto/types/models.proto`; no test asserts a query plan) |
| api_token/bounded-auth-cost | Authenticating an API token MUST cost a fixed number of queries (token, realm grants, subset, plus the organization axis), however many tokens or roles exist. | Performance | none |
| api_token/resolver-fails-closed | Any query error while resolving a token MUST produce the opaque Unauthenticated, never a partial principal. | Failure & configuration | none for the realm-aware resolver (only the organization axis has `TestNarrowGrants_QueryErrorFailsClosed`) |
| api_token/ttl-bounded-at-boot | `api_token_default_ttl_hours` above 100 years MUST fail plugin bootstrap. | Failure & configuration | `TestAPITokenExpiry_OutOfRange` |
| api_token/bound-to-its-key | Every API token except a machine account's bearer token (`device/bot-bearer`) MUST be bound to the program key its request names, and only a proof by that key MUST authenticate it (`device/api-token-own-key`, `device/private-key-never-leaves`). An API token minted before device binding MUST be refused by Authenticate (unbound). The device, the token, its subset and the history row MUST commit together. | Security | `TestCreateApiToken_BindsTheTokenToTheGivenKey`, `TestAuthenticate_AnAPITokenNeedsAProofByItsKey`, `TestAuthenticate_AThiefLearnsNothingAboutTheToken` (unbound), `TestCreateApiToken_IsRecordedInTheHistory` (one transaction) |

**Gaps / suspected defects**
1. **Fixed in rc.25: a narrowed token could mint an unnarrowed one.** `CreateApiToken` read only `callerUserID`, so an API token narrowed to `{view}` could mint a token carrying every API-realm permission its holder has. It now refuses any caller that did not authenticate with a session. Fixed later: listing and revoking take a session too (`ListApiTokens`, `RevokeApiToken`), so a leaked token can neither read nor revoke the holder's other tokens (`TestApiTokenManagement_AnAPITokenCallerIsRefused`).
2. **Fixed: a caller-chosen expiry can be bounded.** `api_token_max_ttl_hours` caps it (`TOKEN_EXPIRY_TOO_LATE`; 0, the default, is no cap — a deployment that wants one sets it); a bearer machine-account token never lasts more than 366 days (`TOKEN_EXPIRY_TOO_FAR`).
3. **Not a defect in a wired bundle.** The token and its subset rows are one distributed transaction: `RegisterPlugin` always wires `DistTx`. Only a hand-built test handler (`h.DistTx` nil) writes them separately.
4. **Fixed: `last_used_at` was never written.** Authenticate now writes it through `TouchTokenLastUsed`, at most once a minute per token (`api_token/last-used-throttled`). Best effort: a failed write is not logged (the package has no logger) and is retried by the next request in a new window.
5. **Fixed: creating and revoking an API token are events.** `IssueApiToken` emits `ApiTokenCreated` and `MarkApiTokenRevoked` emits `ApiTokenRevoked`.
6. **Fixed: revoke was two unguarded statements.** The subset cleanup is gone (the rows stay); the revoke is one guarded UPDATE, idempotent.
7. **Fixed: the manifest under-declared dependencies.** `GetUserRolePermissionsByRealm` reads `UserRole`, `Role` and `RolePermission`, which `rbac` owns, so `api_token` without `rbac` could not generate. `api_token` now `requires: [rbac]`, and `make check-stage` reports a statement that names another feature's table.
8. **A personal token is not bound to an organization** (documented in the README). With `org_membership` it selects any organization its holder belongs to, as a session does. Binding a personal token to an organization would be new work.

---

### bot_token_guard

- **Purpose.** A machine account's bearer token is the whole credential: whoever holds it acts. For a bot on a server with a fixed address, an allowlist makes a copied token worthless anywhere else. The use summary shows where and for what a bot's tokens are used, so an unexpected address or method is visible.
- **Adds.** Column `User.allowed_cidrs` (comma-separated canonical CIDRs, empty = anywhere). Table `BotTokenUse` (token, client address, surface, method, first and latest use; unique per combination). Queries `GetBotAllowedCidrs`, `ListBotTokenUses`; mutations `SetBotAllowedCidrs` (emits `BotAllowlistChanged` with the operator and their device), `RecordBotTokenUse` (an upsert). Service methods `SetBotAllowlist`, `GetBotGuard`. REST endpoints `PUT /auth/bots/{user_id}/allowlist` and `GET /auth/bots/{user_id}/guard` (bearer). `GetTokenWithType` projects `allowed_cidrs`.
- **Reads from the gateway.** `x-w17-client-addr` (the client address resolved through the surface's trusted proxies), `x-w17-surface` and `x-w17-method`. The gateway strips client-sent `x-w17-*` headers, so none of them is the client's word.
- **Requires / conflicts / combined files.** Requires `service_account`. Default off.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| `bot_token_guard/allowlist` | With an allowlist set, a bot's bearer token MUST be accepted only from a client address inside it (an IPv4-mapped address read as IPv4), and refused from any other with the opaque refusal, recording nothing. | Security | `TestAuthenticate_ABotBearerTokenIsHeldToItsAllowlist` |
| `bot_token_guard/fail-closed` | With an allowlist set, a request whose gateway resolved no client address MUST be refused, and a stored list that no longer parses MUST match nothing rather than anywhere. | Security | `TestAuthenticate_ABotBearerTokenIsHeldToItsAllowlist` |
| `bot_token_guard/bearer-only` | The allowlist MUST apply to bearer tokens only: a bound token proves its key and a person is never limited by address. | Security | `checkBotAllowlist` runs only on the bot-bearer branch of `authenticate` (after `checkBotBearer`). |
| `bot_token_guard/summary` | An accepted bearer request MUST be recorded with its token, address, surface and method, at most once a minute per combination and process; a refused one MUST NOT. A failed write MUST NOT fail the request. | Audit | `TestAuthenticate_ABotBearerUseIsSummarised`, `TestAuthenticate_ABotBearerTokenIsHeldToItsAllowlist` |
| `bot_token_guard/canonical` | An allowlist entry MUST be a CIDR or an address, stored masked (an address as its /32 or /128, an IPv4-mapped network as the IPv4 one), duplicates dropped, at most 32 entries; anything else, a zoned address included, MUST be refused on the field. | Validation | `TestCanonicalAllowlist` |
| `bot_token_guard/operator` | Setting and reading a bot's guard MUST follow IssueBotToken's operator rules: a signed-in person (never an API token), a bot of the caller's organization, holding no role above the caller's. A change MUST be announced (`BotAllowlistChanged`) with the operator who made it. | Security | `TestSetBotAllowlistAndGetBotGuard`, `TestBotGuard_AnAPITokenCallerIsRefused` |

### org_roles

- **Purpose.** An organization composes roles of its own from the deployed lock's permissions, the way GitHub offers custom roles, without a role of one company reaching another's pickers.
- **Adds.** Column `Role.org_id` (the owning organization; NULL for every seeded role). Queries `ListOrgRoleRows`, `CountRoleGrants`; mutations `ClaimRoleForOrg`, `UpdateOrgRoleLabel` (emits `OrgRoleUpdated`). Service methods `ListOrgRoles`, `CreateOrgRole`, `UpdateOrgRole`, `DeleteOrgRole`. REST endpoints `GET` / `POST /auth/orgs/{org}/org-roles`, `PUT` / `DELETE /auth/orgs/{org}/org-roles/{role_id}` (bearer, the org in the path). Creating reuses `CreateRole` (`RoleCreated`); permissions go through `AddPermissionToRole` / `RemovePermissionFromRole`, each logged in `RolePermissionChange`; deleting reuses `DeleteRole`.
- **Changes.** The org-role catalogue every grant reads (`assignableRoles`: invitation, acceptance, membership role change, `ListAssignableRoles`) becomes `ListOrgRoleRows` for the organization: shared org roles plus its own.
- **Requires / conflicts / combined files.** Requires `org_membership`. Default off.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| `org_roles/own-org` | A custom role MUST be offered, accepted and editable only in the organization that owns it; a shared (seeded) role MUST NOT be edited or deleted here. | Isolation | `TestOrgRoleCatalogue_IsTheOrganizationsAndHoldsNoBotRole`, `TestUpdateOrgRole`, `TestDeleteOrgRole` |
| `org_roles/ceiling` | A role MUST carry only permissions its author holds and the deployed lock allocates, at least one; an existing role MUST be changed or deleted only by a caller who covers what it carries now. | Security | `TestCreateOrgRole`, `TestUpdateOrgRole` |
| `org_roles/in-use` | A role still granted to anyone MUST NOT be deleted. | Integrity | `TestDeleteOrgRole` |
| `org_roles/no-deletes` | Nothing is deleted: a permission is removed by `removed_at`, a role by `deleted_at`. | Audit | `no_deletes_test.go` |

### org_packages

**Purpose.** What an organization's members can hold is bounded by the packages it has been given (w17/packages.proto in the SDK): sets of the project's methods and audiences, named in the domain's protos. The backoffice gives and takes whole packages per organization; nobody in an organization holds more than its packages, a wildcard role included, and a method no package grants is held by nobody there.

**Adds**
- Table `OrgPackage` (org_id, package, enabled_by/at, ended_by/at, ended_key): switching off ends the row; nothing is deleted.
- Business: `AddOrgPackage`, `RemoveOrgPackage`, `ListPackagesOfOrg` (realm-wide only; `/auth/admin/orgs/{org_id}/packages[/{package}]`), `ListMyOrgPackages` (`/auth/orgs/{org}/packages`).
- Env `w17_packages`, generated by codegen and baked into the binary: each package's methods and audiences, and the governed methods (the project's), named as the ACL lock keys them. Parsed at boot.
- Errors `PACKAGE_AUDIENCE_OFF` (403), `PACKAGES_REALM_ONLY` (403), `PACKAGE_UNKNOWN` (400).

**Requires / conflicts / combined files.** Requires `org_membership`. Go file: `handlers/org_packages.go`.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| org_packages/governed-within-packages | In an organization, a governed permission of a role held there MUST survive Authenticate only when one of the organization's live packages grants it; a wildcard is bounded the same way. A role held realm-wide (the backoffice, also a member) MUST NOT be bounded, and a realm-wide wildcard MUST reach any audience there, the endpoint ACL deciding what it calls — as plans do not bound realm-wide product roles. | Security | `TestNarrowGrantsToPackages`, `TestPackagesBoundTheOrganizationsRolesOnly`; live: `examples/grant-proof` |
| org_packages/default-deny | A governed method no live package grants MUST be held by nobody in the organization. | Security | `TestNarrowGrantsToPackages` |
| org_packages/plugin-and-realm-untouched | A plugin's permission (not governed) and a realm-wide request MUST NOT be narrowed. | Security | `TestNarrowGrantsToPackages` |
| org_packages/audience-opened | An audience some package names MUST admit an organization's member only when one of its packages names it (403 `PACKAGE_AUDIENCE_OFF`); an audience no package names is open. | Security | `TestRefuseAudienceOutsidePackages` |
| org_packages/default-floor | A `default` package MUST be held by every organization without a row, so adopting packages leaves no organization with nothing; it MUST NOT be given or taken (`PACKAGE_DEFAULT`), and listing an organization's packages MUST show it. | Correctness | `TestNarrowGrantsToPackages`, `TestDefaultPackageIsNotGivenOrTaken` |
| org_packages/no-lock-refuses | Without an ACL lock, or with the empty one a project without the ACL embeds, an organization MUST be refused, never left unbounded. | Security | `TestNarrowGrantsToPackages` |
| org_packages/realm-only | Packages MUST be given and taken realm-wide only (a call acting inside an organization is refused — the backoffice acts from accounts in no organization), and only where the endpoint ACL is on. | Security | `TestAddOrgPackage`, `TestAddOrgPackageNeedsTheACL` (the lock keys the methods under any activation name) |
| org_packages/ceiling-follows | Nobody MUST hand out or compose a role above the organization's packages — the role ceiling is what the caller holds after this narrowing. | Security | `role_ceiling` tests over the narrowed envelope |

**Gaps / suspected defects**
1. Packages are read once per Authenticate in an organization (one indexed query); there is no cache across requests.
2. **Fixed: a backoffice operator who is a member of exactly one organization was inferred into it and refused the package methods.** A backoffice method the caller holds through a realm-wide role now acts realm-wide wherever the request acts (`org_membership/realm-call-acts-realm-wide`). Another plugin's backoffice methods (the grant plugin's envelope decisions) act on `realm_permissions`, the scope Authenticate sets inside an organization with what the credential holds realm-wide (`org_membership/realm-permissions-scope`).

---

### org_hosts

**Purpose.** A white-label or co-brand host serves one organization: a request arriving on it, through an audience that takes the tenant from the host (w17/audience.proto `TENANT_SOURCE_HOST`), acts in that organization. The binding lives here because `Authenticate` enforces it.

**Adds**
- Table `OrgHost` (host, org_id, bound_by/at, ended_by/at, ended_key): one live binding per host; unbinding ends the row; nothing is deleted.
- Queries `GetOrgOfHost`, `ListOrgHostRows` (live organizations only); mutations `BindOrgHostRow`, `EndOrgHostRow`.
- Business: `BindOrgHost`, `UnbindOrgHost`, `ListOrgHosts` (realm-wide only; `PUT` / `DELETE /auth/admin/hosts/{host}`, `GET /auth/admin/hosts`), `GetHostOrg` (anyone; `GET /auth/hosts/{host}`, limited per address).
- Errors `HOSTS_REALM_ONLY` (403), `HOST_TAKEN` (400), `HOST_INVALID` (400); through an audience, `ORG_HOST_CONFLICT` (403) and `ORG_SELECTOR_NOT_ACCEPTED` (403) — see `org_membership/tenant-from`.

**Requires / conflicts / combined files.** Requires `org_membership`. Go file: `handlers/org_hosts.go`.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| org_hosts/realm-only | Hosts MUST be bound, unbound and listed realm-wide only (a call acting inside an organization is refused), and only where the endpoint ACL is on. | Security | `TestBindOrgHost`, `TestBindOrgHostNeedsTheACL` |
| org_hosts/one-organization | A host bound to one organization MUST NOT be bound to another without unbinding; binding it again to its own changes nothing; a racing second binding lands on the unique index and is told the host is taken. | Security | `TestBindOrgHost`, the `OrgHost` index |
| org_hosts/host-shape | A host MUST be a lower-case DNS name of two labels or more, without a port, scheme or path, stored as the gateway reports it (lower-case, no trailing dot). | Validation | `TestBindOrgHost` |
| org_hosts/public-answer | `GetHostOrg` MUST answer `found = false`, never an error, for an unbound host or no host at all. | Privacy | `TestGetHostOrg` |
| org_hosts/chooses-never-grants | A host MUST only choose among the caller's organizations: the caller still needs a membership there. | Security | `TestResolveOrgScope_TenantFromHost` |

**Gaps / suspected defects**
1. On MCP the organization is the OAuth grant's, chosen on the consent page; a bound host does not yet preselect it there.
2. A host is read on every `Authenticate` through an audience that takes the tenant from it (one indexed query); there is no cache across requests beyond the gateway's auth cache, which keys on the host.

### org_two_factor_policy

**Purpose.** An organization's own second-factor policy — `required`, `optional` or `disabled` — per kind of membership, over the activation's `two_factor_policy`. One deployment can then require a second factor of an organization's staff, leave it to its clients, and switch it off for another organization altogether.

**Adds**
- Table `OrgTwoFactorPolicy` (org_id, kind, policy, set_by/at, ended_by/at, ended_key): one live policy per (organization, kind); kind `''` is the organization's own; replacing or clearing ends the row; nothing is deleted.
- Business: `SetOrgTwoFactorPolicy`, `ClearOrgTwoFactorPolicy`, `ListOrgTwoFactorPolicies` (the backoffice's, realm-wide; `PUT` / `GET /auth/admin/orgs/{org_id}/two-factor-policies`, `POST …/clear`) and `SetMyOrgTwoFactorPolicy`, `ClearMyOrgTwoFactorPolicy`, `ListMyOrgTwoFactorPolicies` (the organization's, from inside it; `PUT` / `GET /auth/orgs/{org}/two-factor-policies`, `POST …/clear`). A list answers the activation's default beside the rows.
- Errors `TWO_FACTOR_POLICIES_REALM_ONLY` (403), `TWO_FACTOR_POLICY_INVALID` (400); `TWO_FACTOR_DISABLED` (412) on an enrolment where it is disabled.

**Which policy applies.** A password sign-in reads the organization it arrives for, decided as Authenticate decides the active organization: the host bound to it (through an audience that takes the tenant from the host), the W17-Org selector, or the person's only organization. The kind of the person's membership there picks the row; without one of its own, the organization's own row; without that, the activation's default. A sign-in for an organization the person is not a member of, or for none, reads the default. An enrolment reads the organization the caller acts in. A selector conflicting with the host refuses the sign-in, as it refuses every request.

**Who sets it.** The backoffice realm-wide and the organization from inside itself — both only where the project's endpoint ACL gates the method: a method nobody gates is refused, because otherwise any member could switch the organization's second factor off.

**Requires / conflicts / combined files.** Requires `two_factor` and `org_membership`. Go file: `handlers/org_two_factor_policy.go`.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| org_two_factor_policy/per-kind-then-org-then-default | The policy MUST be the row of the person's membership kind in the organization the request is for, else the organization's own (kind `''`), else the activation's default; no membership there (or no organization) MUST read the default. | Security | `TestTwoFactorPolicy_TheOrganizationOverridesPerKind`, `TestTwoFactorPolicy_NoMembershipThereIsTheDefault` |
| org_two_factor_policy/org-as-authenticate | The organization of a sign-in MUST be the one Authenticate would choose (host, selector, sole membership); a selector conflicting with the host MUST refuse. | Security | `TestTwoFactorPolicy_TheOrganizationOverridesPerKind`, `TestTwoFactorPolicy_AConflictingSelectorRefuses` |
| org_two_factor_policy/acl-gated | Every policy method MUST be refused unless the endpoint ACL gates it; the backoffice's MUST be refused from inside an organization; the organization's MUST act in the active organization only. | Security | `TestSetOrgTwoFactorPolicy`, `TestSetMyOrgTwoFactorPolicy` |
| org_two_factor_policy/values | A policy MUST be one of required, optional, disabled; a kind MUST be `''` where memberships have no kinds. | Correctness | `TestSetOrgTwoFactorPolicy` |

### org_signup

**Purpose.** Registration on an organization's own host. An organization takes people who register without an invitation per kind of membership — clients openly, staff by invitation, say — with a role the backoffice chose, so a white-label host is a sign-up page of its own.

**Adds**
- Table `OrgSignUpPolicy` (org_id, kind, open, role, set_by/at, ended_by/at, ended_key): one live policy per (organization, kind); replacing or clearing ends the row; nothing is deleted.
- `PendingSignUp.join_org_id / join_kind / join_role` (with `sign_up_confirmation`, `handlers/signup_confirm_org.go`): the join is decided when a registration is requested and written when it is confirmed.
- Business: `SetOrgSignUpPolicy`, `ClearOrgSignUpPolicy`, `ListOrgSignUpPolicies` (realm-wide only; `PUT` / `GET /auth/admin/orgs/{org_id}/signup-policies`, `POST …/signup-policies/clear`). `GetHostOrg` answers `open_kinds`.
- `SignUpReq.signup_host` and `OAuthExchangeReq.signup_host` (field-gated `org_signup`): the host a registration page on the identity's own host names for the person registering.
- Errors `SIGNUP_POLICIES_REALM_ONLY` (403), `SIGNUP_POLICY_INVALID` (400), `SIGNUP_KIND_AMBIGUOUS` (412), `SIGNUP_HOST_CONFLICT` (400, a body host naming another than the gateway's); a closed policy refuses with `SIGNUP_NOT_INVITED`; a body host that is no host name `HOST_INVALID` on `signup_host`.

**How a registration decides** (SignUp, ConfirmSignUp, an OAuth sign-in that creates the account). With an invitation, the invitation decides its organization and `invite_only` applies as before. Without one, through an audience gateway that takes the tenant from the host (its context arrives as metadata, SDK `restgw.AudienceContextMiddleware`), on a host bound to an organization: the policies of the kinds the audience admits are read — one open: the account joins with that kind and role in its own transaction, whatever `invite_only` says; none open but some closed: refused; none: `invite_only` decides and the account joins nothing; two open: refused as a configuration error.

**A registration page on the identity's own host.** A host that serves several organizations' sign-ups — the identity's own, bound to none of them — has no host of its own to decide by. Its page names the host the person comes from in the request (`signup_host`): with no gateway host, that host decides exactly as a registration arriving on it would — the organization bound to it (`org_hosts`) and that organization's policies, filtered by the audience's kinds when it names some and by none otherwise. The page shows the organization the same way: `GET /auth/hosts/{host}` (GetHostOrg, with `open_kinds`), then the grant plugin's approved envelope. Nothing is verified beyond the binding, and nothing needs to be: an open policy admits anyone who reaches that host, so naming it gains nothing more, and a closed or absent policy admits nothing either way. A gateway host bound to an organization is the request's own and wins; a body host naming another one is refused (`SIGNUP_HOST_CONFLICT`), never followed. A gateway host bound to none — the identity's own, behind an audience that reads the host — leaves the body host to decide.

**Requires / conflicts / combined files.** Requires `org_hosts` and `rbac`. Go file: `handlers/org_signup.go`; with `sign_up_confirmation`, `handlers/signup_confirm_org.go`.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| org_signup/policy-decides | Without an invitation, on a bound host, an open policy for the audience's kind MUST join the account to the host's organization with that kind and role; a closed one MUST refuse it; no policy MUST leave invite_only to decide; two open kinds MUST be refused, never guessed. | Security | `TestDecideSignUpJoin`, `TestSignUpGates_OpenPolicyAndInvitation` |
| org_signup/invitation-first | A registration carrying an invitation MUST NOT consult the host. | Correctness | `TestSignUpGates_OpenPolicyAndInvitation` |
| org_signup/body-host | Without a gateway host, the host a registration names (`signup_host`, normalised like a gateway host) MUST decide exactly as a registration arriving on it: its bound organization and policies, the audience's kinds when named; an unbound host MUST join nothing. With a gateway host bound to an organization, a body host naming another MUST be refused (`SIGNUP_HOST_CONFLICT`) and the gateway host MUST decide; a gateway host bound to none (the identity's own) MUST leave the body host to decide. An invitation still decides first. | Security | `TestDecideSignUpJoin_ABodyHost`, `TestSignUpGates_ABodyHostFromEachRequest` |
| org_signup/same-transaction | The membership and its org-scoped grant MUST be written in the account's own transaction, the role resolved against the organization's catalogue then. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestWriteSignUpJoin`; live: `examples/grant-proof` |
| org_signup/decided-at-request | With sign_up_confirmation, the join MUST be decided when the registration is requested and carried on the pending row. | Correctness | `TestPendingSignUpCarriesTheJoin` |
| org_signup/realm-only | Policies MUST be set, cleared and listed realm-wide only, where the endpoint ACL is on; a policy MUST name a kind exactly when memberships have kinds, and an open one a role the organization can hand out. | Security | `TestSetOrgSignUpPolicy` |

**Gaps / suspected defects**
1. A policy is per (organization, kind) and set by the backoffice; an organization does not set its own.
2. The host names the organization a registration joins: the gateway's, or on the identity's own host the one the request names (`signup_host`); an organization in the path does not.

### product_roles

**Purpose.** One identity for the project's products. A product is a domain whose `(w17.domain).identity` names this one: it keeps no users and authenticates every caller here. The roles are the identity's: the products' presets (each product's `(w17.acl_roles)`, baked into the catalogue with the permission codes they grant) and roles the identity composes itself from every product's permissions — the backoffice's, realm-wide, and an organization's own (`ProductRoleDef`, named as product `*`; one may span products). Asked by a product, Authenticate answers the product's permission CODES the caller holds there; the product resolves them against its own lock, so this plugin still holds no permission id of another domain.

**Adds**
- Table `ProductRoleGrant` (user_id, org_id — NULL = realm-wide, product, role_key, granted_by/at, ended_by/at, ended_key): one live grant per (user, organization or realm, product, role); revoking ends the row; nothing is deleted.
- `AuthResp.product_roles` (field 9): set when a product's gateway asks (`x-w17-product`, which only a gateway writes) through the identity's private surface.
- `presets.rpc`: `Authenticate`, `AuthenticateAdmin`, `SignIn`, `OAuthExchange`, `GetHostOrg`, offered to other domains' services. The identity's `(w17.private_api)` confirms the ones its products call; products reach them there, over mutual TLS, never the identity's tiers.
- Generated setting `w17_product_roles`: the catalogue of the products' roles (product, key, name, org_scoped, and `covers` — the product's roles each one carries every permission of), baked in at codegen; never in the env files.
- Business: `ListProductRoles` (`GET /auth/product-roles`); in the organization `ListOrgProductRoleGrants`, `AssignOrgProductRole`, `RevokeOrgProductRole` (`GET` / `PUT /auth/orgs/{org}/product-role-grants`, `POST …/product-role-grants/revoke`); the backoffice's `ListProductRoleGrants`, `AssignProductRole`, `RevokeProductRole` (`GET` / `PUT /auth/admin/product-role-grants`, `POST …/revoke`).
- Errors `PRODUCT_UNKNOWN`, `PRODUCT_ROLE_UNKNOWN`, `PRODUCT_ROLE_REALM_HELD` (400), `PRODUCT_ROLE_ABOVE_CALLER`, `PRODUCT_ROLES_REALM_ONLY` (403), `PRODUCT_ROLE_NOT_MEMBER` (412).
- Table `ProductRoleDef` (org_id — NULL = the backoffice's, key, name, org_scoped, permissions as `<product>:<code>` pairs, created_by/at, ended_by/at, ended_key): the identity's own roles; query `ListProductRoleDefs`, mutations `InsertProductRoleDef`, `UpdateProductRoleDef`, `EndProductRoleDef`. Business: `ListProductPermissions` (`GET /auth/product-permissions`), the backoffice's `DefineProductRole` / `RetireProductRole` (`PUT /auth/admin/product-roles`, `POST …/retire`; realm-only, endpoint ACL), the organization's `DefineOrgProductRole` / `RetireOrgProductRole` (`PUT /auth/orgs/{org}/product-roles`, `POST …/retire`; under the ceiling). `ProductRoleInfo` carries `permissions`, `preset`, `org_id`. Error `PRODUCT_PERMISSION_UNKNOWN` (400).
- With `api_token`: table `TokenProductRole` (token_id, product, role_key; unique per token, product and role), query `ListTokenProductRoles`, mutation `AddTokenProductRole`; `CreateApiTokenReq.product_roles` (5) and `IssueBotTokenReq.product_roles` (6).

**How Authenticate answers a product.** Everything up to the scopes runs as for the identity's own surfaces — the token, the device binding, the active organization (`tenant_from`, `W17-Org`, the host). Then, with `x-w17-product` set — and only when the identity's private gateway reports the call comes from that product (`x-w17-caller-domain`), `PERMISSION_DENIED` otherwise: the caller's live grants are read; the realm-wide ones count, and those of the active organization whose role is held in an organization, each product's permissions of it only where the plan (`product_plans`) carries the product or the role. A role the catalogue or the identity no longer has counts for nothing. The union of the codes the counted roles grant in that product goes to `product_roles` (the keys the product's gateway translates; its table maps every permission code, and a preset's key, to its ids), and `permission_ids` is emptied. A product the catalogue does not name is refused (`FailedPrecondition`): the identity was generated without it.

**The identity's own roles.** The backoffice defines a role from any products' permissions (`DefineProductRole`; a permission no product has is `PRODUCT_PERMISSION_UNKNOWN`), usable realm-wide or in organizations (`org_scoped`); redefining a live key changes what every grant of it holds on the next request; retiring ends it, and its grants hold nothing. An organization defines its own (`DefineOrgProductRole`), held there, from permissions its definer holds there. A preset is not edited — a copy is defined. The catalogue (`ListProductRoles`) lists the presets with their codes and the identity's own (the backoffice's, and the active organization's).

**A token's own share.** With `api_token`, a token names the products' roles it may use when it is minted (`CreateApiToken`, `IssueBotToken` — each one of the catalogue, `PRODUCT_UNKNOWN` / `PRODUCT_ROLE_UNKNOWN` otherwise; written in the mint's transaction, beside its permission subset). Asked by a product, Authenticate answers an API token the roles it names that a role its holder holds there covers (the catalogue's `covers`, the ceiling of handing a role out): naming a role gives nothing the holder could not hand out themselves. A token minted narrow stays narrow on this axis too — one with a permission subset and no product role reaches the products with none. Only a token narrowed on neither axis carries every role its holder holds, as a session does. A revoked token's rows stay, as its permission subset does.

**Invitations and open registration.** With `org_invite`, `InviteToOrgReq.product_roles` names the products' roles accepting gives in the organization (column `OrgInvite.product_roles`, `<product>:<role key>` pairs): each must be one the catalogue holds in an organization, carried by the plan, and covered by a role the inviter holds there. Acceptance holds them to the catalogue and the plan again before it writes the membership, then grants them in its transaction with the inviter as the giver; a role that left either refuses the acceptance. The invitation lists (`ListOrgInvites`, `ListMyInvites`), `InviteToOrgResp` and `AcceptOrgInviteResp` show them. With `org_signup`, `SetOrgSignUpPolicyReq.product_roles` names those an open registration is given (column `OrgSignUpPolicy.product_roles`), held to the catalogue and the plan when the backoffice sets the policy; a registration gives those of the policy live when it joins that the catalogue and the plan still carry, and passes over the rest — the person registering cannot replace the policy.

**Requires / conflicts / combined files.** Requires `org_membership`. Go file: `handlers/product_roles.go`; with `org_invite` also `handlers/product_roles_invite.go` (`inviteProductRoles`), with `org_signup` also `handlers/product_roles_signup.go` (`signupPolicyProductRoles`), with `api_token` also `handlers/product_roles_token.go` (a `tokenMintChecks` and a `productRoleNarrowers` entry). Seams: `authRespEnrichers`, `membershipRemovalCascades`, `orgRemovalCascades`, `configChecks`; it offers `productRoleBounds` and `productRoleNarrowers`.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| product_roles/keys-not-ids | Asked by a product, Authenticate MUST answer the product's permission codes the caller holds there through their roles — realm-wide and in the active organization only — and MUST NOT answer any of this domain's permission ids. | Isolation | `TestAnswerProductRoles`, `TestAnswerProductRolesIsInstalled`; live: `examples/product-roles-proof` |
| product_roles/only-itself | A product MUST be answered only through the identity's private surface and only for itself: a call the private gateway did not report as coming from the product asked about MUST be refused before anything is read. | Isolation | `TestAnswerProductRoles_OnlyThroughThePrivateSurfaceAsItself`; live: `examples/product-roles-proof` |
| product_roles/ceiling | Inside an organization a caller MUST hand out, take and define only roles every permission of which they hold there (or realm-wide) — only to members, and only roles held in an organization. | Security | `TestAssignOrgProductRole`, `TestIdentityRoles_AnOrganizationsOwn` |
| product_roles/identity-roles | The identity's own roles MUST grant exactly the products' permissions they name (each one of the catalogue), in every product they name, from the next request after a definition, a redefinition or a retirement; an organization's own MUST be seen in that organization only and retired only by it. | Security | `TestIdentityRoles_TheBackofficeComposesThem`, `TestIdentityRoles_AnOrganizationsOwn`; live: `examples/product-roles-proof` |
| product_roles/backoffice | The backoffice's grants MUST be made realm-wide, where the endpoint ACL gates the method; a grant in an organization MUST respect its plan. | Security | `TestBackofficeProductRoles` |
| product_roles/leaving | A member leaving an organization MUST lose the product roles held there, and only those; a deleted organization's MUST end with it. | Lifecycle | `TestProductRolesEndWithTheMembership` |
| product_roles/invitation | An invitation MUST carry only product roles the inviter could hand out in the organization at that moment, and accepting it MUST give them only while the catalogue and the plan still carry them — refusing otherwise, before the membership is written. | Security | `TestInviteProductRoles`; live: `examples/product-roles-proof` |
| product_roles/registration | An open registration policy MUST name only roles the catalogue holds in an organization and the plan carries; a registration MUST give only those still carried when it joins. | Security | `TestSignUpPolicyProductRoles`; live: `examples/product-roles-proof` |
| product_roles/token-subset | Asked by a product for an API token, Authenticate MUST answer only roles the token names that a role the holder holds there covers; a token carrying a permission subset and naming no product role MUST be answered none; a token narrowed on neither axis MUST be answered every role its holder holds. A mint MUST name only roles of the catalogue and MUST write them in its transaction. | Security | `TestTokenProductRoles_AProductIsAnsweredTheTokensShare`, `TestTokenProductRoles_TheMintHoldsThemToTheCatalogue`; live: `examples/product-roles-proof` |
| product_roles/catalogue | The catalogue MUST be the products' own declarations, generated — never edited — and a table that does not parse MUST refuse to boot. | Correctness | `parseProductRoleCatalogue`; codegen `TestWithProductRoleTables` |

**Gaps / suspected defects**
1. **Fixed: a token reached a product with every role of its user.** An API token or a machine account's token now names the products' roles it may use (`product_roles/token-subset`).

### product_plans

**Purpose.** An organization's plan across the products: which roles of which products its members may hold — so a firm that uses one product holds nothing in the others, and the backoffice decides it in one place.

**Adds**
- Table `OrgProductAllowance` (org_id, product, role_key — `''` = the whole product, allowed_by/at, ended_by/at, ended_key).
- Business: `ListOrgProductPlan`, `AllowOrgProduct`, `DisallowOrgProduct` (realm-wide only; `GET` / `PUT /auth/admin/orgs/{org_id}/product-plan`, `POST …/product-plan/remove`); `ListMyOrgProductPlan` (`GET /auth/orgs/{org}/product-plan`).
- Errors `PRODUCT_ROLE_NOT_IN_PLAN` (412), `PRODUCT_PLANS_REALM_ONLY` (403).

**Requires / conflicts / combined files.** Requires `product_roles`. Go file: `handlers/product_plans.go` (a `productRoleBounds` entry).

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| product_plans/bounds | A product role held in an organization MUST count only while the organization's plan carries it (the role, or the whole product), on every request; an organization with no line for a product MUST hold nothing there; realm-wide grants MUST NOT be bounded. | Security | `TestAnswerProductRoles`, `TestProductPlanLines`; live: `examples/product-roles-proof` |
| product_plans/no-grant-outside | Nobody MUST be able to hand out a role the plan does not carry. | Security | `TestAssignOrgProductRole`, `TestBackofficeProductRoles` |
| product_plans/realm-only | Lines MUST be given and taken realm-wide only, where the endpoint ACL gates the method. | Security | `TestProductPlanLines` |

### teams

- **Purpose.** An organization groups its members into teams, and a team holds roles every member holds while they are in it — so roles are given to a group once instead of to each person, and a resource of the project (the console's project access) can be given to a whole team.
- **Adds.** Tables `Team`, `TeamMember`, `TeamRole`. Queries `ListTeamsByOrg`, `GetTeam`, `ListTeamMembersByOrg`, `ListTeamRolesByOrg`, `ListTeamRoleIds`, `GetUserTeamRoleGrants`, `GetOrgMemberAccount`, `CountTeamRoleGrants`, `ListOrgTeamSuperadmins` (the last-superadmin rule's team half, which `RemoveOrgTeamMember`, `SetOrgTeamRoles` and `DeleteOrgTeam` are held to as well); mutations `CreateTeam`, `UpdateTeam`, `DeleteTeam`, `AddTeamMember`, `RemoveTeamMember`, `GrantTeamRole`, `RevokeTeamRole`, `EndUserTeamMemberships`, `EndTeamsOfOrg`. Service methods `ListOrgTeams`, `CreateOrgTeam`, `UpdateOrgTeam`, `DeleteOrgTeam`, `AddOrgTeamMember`, `RemoveOrgTeamMember`, `SetOrgTeamRoles`. REST endpoints `GET` / `POST /auth/orgs/{org}/teams`, `PUT` / `DELETE /auth/orgs/{org}/teams/{team_id}`, `POST /auth/orgs/{org}/teams/{team_id}/members`, `DELETE /auth/orgs/{org}/teams/{team_id}/members/{user_id}`, `PUT /auth/orgs/{org}/teams/{team_id}/roles` (bearer, the org in the path).
- **Changes.** Authenticate adds a member's team roles for a session acting in the team's organization (`orgGrantSources`, after the organization narrower's intersection). Removing someone from an organization asks the caller to cover their team roles (`memberHeldRoleSources`) and ends their team memberships in the same transaction (`membershipRemovalCascades`); deleting an organization ends its teams (`orgRemovalCascades`); `DeleteOrgRole` counts a role a team holds as in use (`roleHolderCounters`).
- **Requires / conflicts / combined files.** Requires `rbac`, `org_membership`. Default off.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| `teams/where` | A team's roles MUST count only for a session acting in the team's organization — never realm-wide, in another organization, without an organization or for an API token — and only while the person is a live member of the team and of the organization, through a membership no older than the team membership. | Isolation | `TestTeamGrants_CountForASessionInTheTeamsOrganization`, `TestTeamGrants_CountNowhereElse`, `GetUserTeamRoleGrants` DQL (`m.created_at <= tm.created_at`) |
| `teams/fail-closed` | A team grant query that fails MUST fail the request. | Security | `TestTeamGrants_QueryErrorFailsClosed` |
| `teams/own-org` | Every call MUST act on a live team of the caller's active organization; another organization's team MUST get the same answer as none. | Isolation | `TestTeams_AnotherOrganizationsTeamIsNotFound` |
| `teams/ceiling` | Wherever someone gains or loses a role through a team — giving or taking a team's role, adding or removing a member, deleting the team, removing the person from the organization — the caller MUST cover every role involved; a kept role is not asked about. | Security | `TestSetOrgTeamRoles`, `TestAddOrgTeamMember`, `TestRemovingFromATeamNeedsTheCeiling`, `TestRemoveOrgMembership_TeamRoles` |
| `teams/roles` | A team MUST hold only org-scoped session-realm roles of its organization's catalogue. | Security | `TestSetOrgTeamRoles` |
| `teams/members` | Only a live member of the organization who is a person (not a machine account) MUST join a team. | Integrity | `TestAddOrgTeamMember` |
| `teams/cascade` | Removing a member from the organization MUST end their team memberships there; deleting the organization MUST end its teams; a role a team holds MUST count as in use. | Integrity | `TestRemoveOrgMembership_TeamRoles`, `TestDeleteOrgRole_ARoleATeamHoldsIsInUse` |
