| `teams/no-deletes` | Nothing is deleted: a team ends by `deleted_at`, a membership by `removed_at`, a team's role by `revoked_at`, each moving `ended_key`. | Audit | `no_deletes_test.go` |

### bot_roles

- **Purpose.** A pipeline that pushes and deploys holds both bot roles, instead of a combined role declared for every pairing.
- **Adds.** Field `BotSummary.role_ids`. Service method `SetBotRoles`. REST endpoint `PUT /auth/bots/{user_id}/roles` (bearer).
- **Requires / conflicts / combined files.** Requires `service_account` and `org_membership`. Default off.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| `bot_roles/replace` | `SetBotRoles` MUST leave the bot holding exactly the named roles in the caller's organization, in one transaction, at least one. | Integrity | `TestSetBotRoles_ReplacesTheSetInTheCallersOrganization` |
| `bot_roles/operator` | Every role MUST be an API-realm role the caller could grant, and the bot MUST pass IssueBotToken's operator rules; a refusal MUST write nothing. | Security | `TestSetBotRoles_Refusals` |

### service_account

**Purpose.** Adds machine accounts (`User.kind = BOT`). A machine account has no usable password and acts only through API tokens that an operator mints for it. Creating one and minting a token for one are both held to the operator's role ceiling.

**Adds**
- Column `User.kind` (enum `AccountKind`, HUMAN=0 stored as a value, CHECK-constrained).
- Queries: `ListBotAccounts`, `ListRealmMachineAccounts`, `ListApiRealmRoles`, `LockMachineAccount` (SELECT … FOR UPDATE on the account) and `ListMachineKeys` (its live keyed devices) for `device/bot-one-key`. Mutation: `CreateBotUser` (password hash set to a non-verifiable sentinel). `IssueBotTokenReq.public_key` (optional: the machine account's public JWK; absent mints a bearer token, `device/bot-bearer`). `IssueApiTokenReq.bearer` (`service_account`) and the column `UserToken.bearer` (`device/bot-bearer`).
- Business RPCs: `CreateBot`, `ListBots`, `ListBotRoles`, `IssueBotToken`.
- REST: `POST /auth/bots`, `GET /auth/bots`, `GET /auth/bots/roles`, `POST /auth/bots/{user_id}/token`. All need a bearer token. None is `public` or `failed_only`.
- Admin page `ServiceAccounts` (list, detail, bulk disable/enable, erase).
- A `SignIn` gate (`signInBotGate`) and the `account_kind=bot` principal label.
- Events: none of its own (`CreateBotUser` emits no `UserCreated`).
- Env: `api_token_default_ttl_hours` (shared with `api_token`).

**Requires / conflicts / combined files.** Requires `api_token`, `user_admin`, `rbac` and `authenticate_turnkey`. Go files: `handlers/service_account.go` and `handlers/role_ceiling.go`. Combined files: `[service_account, tenant_scope]` → `handlers/service_account_tenant.go` (the bot is created in the operator's tenant and only that tenant's bots are visible). `[service_account, org_membership]` → `handlers/service_account_org.go` (the bot joins the operator's active organization, its role is granted org-scoped, and listing and minting are restricted to that organization). If organizations are staged without the org scoping file, every machine-account call refuses with Internal.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| service_account/no-password-sign-in | Password `SignIn` MUST refuse a BOT account with the same opaque Unauthenticated used for a wrong password, even if a password hash was later set on it. | Security | `TestSignInBotGate`, `TestSignInBotGate_ReasonStaysInternal`, `TestSignIn_RefusesAMachineAccountThatHasAPassword`, `TestBotPasswordSentinel_IsNonEmptyAndCannotVerify` |
| service_account/token-only-for-bots | `IssueBotToken` MUST refuse a human account or a missing account with the same PermissionDenied, and MUST write nothing on refusal. | Security | `TestIssueBotToken_RefusesAPerson`, `TestIssueBotToken_WithoutOrgs_RefusesAPersonAndAMissingAccountAlike` |
| service_account/create-role-ceiling | `CreateBot` MUST refuse a role carrying any permission the operator does not hold. | Security | `TestCreateBot_RefusesARoleAboveTheOperator`, `TestCreateBot_WithoutOrgs_TheRoleCeilingHolds` |
| service_account/mint-ceiling | `IssueBotToken` MUST refuse a bot that holds any role the operator could not grant. | Security | `TestIssueBotToken_RefusesABotAboveTheOperator` |
| service_account/api-realm-role-only | `CreateBot` MUST refuse a role that is not in the API realm. | Security | `TestCreateBot_RefusesARoleFromTheSessionRealm`, `TestCreateBot_AcceptsAnApiRealmRoleAndGrantsIt` |
| service_account/role-required | `CreateBot` MUST refuse a request without `role_id`. | Security | `TestCreateBot_RoleIsRequired` |
| service_account/org-isolation | With organizations, a bot MUST be created in the operator's active organization with an org-scoped grant. Listing and minting MUST see only that organization's bots, and having no active organization MUST be refused. | Security | `TestCreateBot_JoinsTheOrgAndScopesTheGrantToIt`, `TestCreateBot_RefusesWithNoActiveOrg`, `TestListBots_AsksOnlyForTheActiveOrganization`, `TestListBots_RefusesWithNoActiveOrg`, `TestIssueBotToken_RefusesABotFromAnotherOrganization`, `TestListBots_InAnOrg_KeepsOnlyTheMachines` |
| service_account/tenant-isolation | With `tenant_scope`, a bot MUST be created in the operator's tenant, and bots of other tenants MUST be invisible and unmintable. Having no tenant in scope MUST see none. | Security | `TestCreateBot_PutsTheBotIntoTheOperatorsTenant`, `TestCreateBot_RefusesWithNoTenantInScope`, `TestMachineAccounts_WithoutOrgs_StayInTheOperatorsTenant`, `TestMachineAccounts_NoTenantInScopeSeesNone` |
| service_account/no-unscoped-fallback | A build with organizations but without the org scoping file MUST refuse every machine-account call rather than serve all organizations. | Failure & configuration | `TestMachineAccounts_OrgsWithoutTheirScopingRefuse` |
| service_account/disabled-bot-unmintable | `IssueBotToken` MUST refuse a disabled machine account. | Security | `TestIssueBotToken_RefusesADisabledBot` |
| service_account/create-atomic | The account, its membership and its role grant MUST commit together or not at all. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestCreateBot_GrantFails_RollsBackTheAccountAndMembership`, `TestCreateBot_Success_CommitsOnTheHandlersConnection`, `TestCreateBot_BeginRefused_WritesNothing` (fake distx; only when `DistTx` is wired) |
| service_account/one-key | A machine account MUST hold exactly one key; see `device/bot-one-key`. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestIssueBotToken_OneKeyPerMachineAccount` |
| service_account/mint-atomic | A bot token, its subset, its key's device and its history row MUST commit together. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestIssueBotToken_NarrowingFails_RollsBackTheLiveToken`, `TestIssueBotToken_Success_CommitsOnTheHandlersConnection`, `TestIssueBotToken_BeginRefused_MintsNothing` (fake distx) |
| service_account/ceiling-check-is-current | The ceiling MUST be evaluated against the catalogue as it stands when the grant is written. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestRoleChanges_RunUnderTheOrganizationsLock`: with organizations, `CreateBot` and `IssueBotToken` re-check the ceiling inside their transaction under the organization's lock, and `SetBotRoles` runs under it. **Unit (statement) only**. Without organizations there is no organization to lock and the check and the write stay separate statements. |
| service_account/listing-bounded | Listing machine accounts MUST be bounded (paged or indexed on `kind`). | Performance | none. `ListBotAccounts` and `ListRealmMachineAccounts` scan `User` on unindexed `kind` and return every row. |
| service_account/label-not-authority | The `account_kind=bot` label MUST be informational only. A failure to resolve it MUST NOT fail or widen authentication. | Failure & configuration | none |

**Gaps / suspected defects**
1. **Fixed in rc.25: a disabled machine account could get a fresh, working token.** `IssueBotToken` checked only `kind` and visibility, and `Authenticate` never read `disabled_at`. It now refuses a disabled bot with `FailedPrecondition`, and `Authenticate` refuses a disabled account's token.
2. **Fixed: the email was not normalized.** `CreateBot` stores the address lower-cased and trimmed, as every other account path does.
3. **The membership label does not match the grant.** `src/handlers/service_account_org.go` writes `OrgMembership.role = "member"` whatever role was granted, so `ListOrgMembers` reports a label unrelated to the bot's permissions.
4. **Without organizations, every operator manages every bot.** By design the realm is one organization, so any authenticated caller whose ceiling covers a bot's role can mint tokens for it. Only the endpoint ACL limits this, which is the project's job, and nothing in the plugin enforces that an ACL exists.
5. **Fixed: machine accounts are audited.** `CreateBotUser` emits `BotCreated` (the account, its address, the operator, their device), and a bot's token is `ApiTokenCreated`, emitted by `IssueApiToken` (`TestCreateBot_TheCreationNamesTheOperatorAndTheirDevice`, `TestCreateBotUser_EmitsBotCreated`).

---

### user_admin

**Purpose.** Adds turnkey administration of accounts: a Users page and a Sessions page on the admin surface (the Devices and SignIns pages are the opt-in `admin_devices` and `admin_sign_ins`, below), soft-disable and enable, erasure (GDPR, by pseudonymisation — nothing is deleted), and revocation of single tokens.

**Adds**
- Columns `User.disabled_at`, `User.erased_at`.
- `DisableUsers` also stamps `User.device_trust_revoked_at`, so a re-enabled account's devices pass the second factor again.
- Queries: `ListUsers`, `GetUser` (no `password_hash`), `ListUserTokens` (with each token's device — row, label, key — and `public_computer`), `GetUserToken` (no secret; `UserTokenAdminDetail`: the token, its device, `public_computer`, and for a CLI session the device that approved it), `ListAllDevices` (cursor-paged on the immutable `(created_at, id)`, the newest first, every device of every account — revoked and legacy ones included — with the owner's address and whether its trust stands) and `GetDevice` (the Devices page, `DeviceAdminRow`), `ListSignInEvents` (cursor-paged, newest first, the account's current address or the attempted one) and `GetSignInEvent` (the Sign-ins page).
- Mutations: `UpdateUser` (email only; clears `email_verified_at`; refuses an erased account; emits `UserUpdated`), `BeginUserErasure` and `EraseUser` (replace `DeleteUser`; the first and last steps of an erasure over the base tables, see below; `EraseUser` emits `UserDeleted`, the released event, topic `auth.user.deleted`), `DisableUsers` (sets `disabled_at` and revokes every token of those users, reason USER_DISABLED), `EnableUsers` (leaves an erased account disabled), `RevokeUserToken` (reason ADMIN_REVOKED, the operator as `revoked_by`; emits `UserTokenRevoked`). The feature-owned erasers: `EraseOAuthData` (oauth), `EraseTwoFactorData` (two_factor), `SpendEmailVerificationTokens` (email_verification), `EraseOrgInviteAddress` (org_invite), `ErasePendingSignUpAddress` (sign_up_confirmation), plus password_reset's `SpendPasswordResetTokens`, and `EraseSignInEvents` for the base-tier sign-in history.
- Service methods: `UpdateAccount` (the Users page's detail `update`: `UpdateUser` behind the erased-domain refusal), `EraseAccount` (the Users and ServiceAccounts pages' `delete`, which erases one account; `EraseUsers` is its bulk form), `RevokeSession` (the Sessions page's `delete`, which revokes one token; `RevokeSessions` is its bulk form) and `RevokeDevices` (the Devices page's `revoke` action: the base-tier `RevokeDeviceTokens` + `MarkDeviceRevoked`, one transaction per device). Each records the operator, the bulk forms refuse an empty selection (the admin's "all rows"), and treat an already erased account / revoked token as done.
- A `SignIn` gate (`signInDisabledGate`), also applied by `refuseUnsignableAccount` to MFA completion, the OAuth callback and the CLI exchange.
- REST: none. The surface is the admin preset only (pages `Users` and `Sessions` — with each session's device label, not linked: the Devices page is opt-in), so authorization is the admin surface's ACL. Each page's `delete` keeps its name and permission and deletes nothing: on `Users` (and `ServiceAccounts`) it erases (`EraseAccount`), on `Sessions` it revokes (`RevokeSession`).
- Env: none.

**Requires / conflicts / combined files.** No `requires`, no conflicts. Go file: `handlers/user_admin.go`. `service_account`, `admin_sign_ins` and `admin_devices` require this feature.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| user_admin/disabled-cannot-sign-in | Password `SignIn` MUST refuse a disabled account, even with the correct password, using the opaque Unauthenticated. | Security | `TestSignInDisabledGate_SeamRejectsDisabledAllowsActive`, `TestSignIn_RejectsDisabledAccount`, `TestSignIn_TheFourRefusalsAreIndistinguishable` |
| user_admin/disabled-gets-no-session-elsewhere | Every other path that issues a session to an existing account (second factor, OAuth callback, CLI code exchange) MUST refuse a disabled account. | Security | `TestVerifyMfa_ADisabledAccountGetsNoSession` (OAuth and CLI paths: none) |
| user_admin/disable-revokes-all-tokens | Disabling MUST revoke every session and API token of the account (reason USER_DISABLED, no actor: the action binds the storage mutation) in the same unit of work, keeping the rows. | Security | `TestDisableUsers_RevokesEveryTokenClassWithTheDisable` (the two legs), `TestDisableUsers_RevokesEveryTokenAndEnableGivesNoneBack` (fake store). A live test must disable an account holding a session and an API token and show both fail `Authenticate`. |
| user_admin/disable-ends-device-trust | Disabling MUST end the trust of every device of the account in the same statement as `disabled_at`, so re-enabling does not bring back a device that skips the second factor. | Security | `TestDisableUsers_RevokesDeviceTrustInTheSameStatement` (the statement), `TestMintDevice_TrustRevokedByACredentialChange_NotTrusted` (the read side). A live test would disable, re-enable, and show the same device key is challenged again. |
| user_admin/disabled-token-refused-at-auth | A token belonging to a disabled account MUST fail authentication, including a token minted after or concurrently with the disable. | Security | `TestResolvePrincipal_ADisabledAccountIsRefused`. **Unit (mock) only**: a live test must mint a token for an account after disabling it and show `Authenticate` refuses it. |
| user_admin/enable-restores-nothing | Re-enabling MUST NOT restore any revoked token, and MUST NOT re-enable an erased account. | Security | `TestDisableUsers_RevokesEveryTokenAndEnableGivesNoneBack`, `TestEraseUsers_TheAccountCannotSignInAnyMore`, `TestEraseStatements` (`erased_at IS NULL` on EnableUsers) |
| user_admin/delete-revokes-tokens | **Retired:** accounts are no longer deleted; see `user_admin/erasure-keeps-every-row`. | — | — |
| user_admin/erasure-keeps-every-row | Erasing an account (GDPR) MUST NOT delete any row. Per account — one transaction with a coordinator (`DistTx`); without one each statement commits alone, and a failed erasure is completed by retrying it (`user_admin/erasure-retry-completes`) — in three steps: (1) `BeginUserErasure` ends access and leaves the address alone: the account is disabled (an earlier disable's time stands), `password_hash` → the unusable `!`, `device_trust_revoked_at` stamped, every token revoked (reason USER_ERASED, the operator as `revoked_by`), every Device revoked and its `label` / `ip` blanked; (2) each feature's personal data, and the sign-in history, is handled per the table below; (3) `EraseUser` pseudonymises the row in place (`email` → `erased-<id>@erased.invalid`, `email_verified_at` cleared, `erased_at` stamped). With a coordinator a failing step rolls the account's erasure back. | Security | `TestEraseUsers_PseudonymisesAndKeepsEveryRow`, `TestEraseUsers_OneTransactionPerAccount`, `TestEraseStatements`, `TestEraseUser_TouchesTheBaseTablesOnly`, `TestEraseUsers_APartialErasureLeavesNoUsableAccount`. **Unit (mock) only** for the SQL: a live test would erase an account holding a row in every table below and diff the tables. |
| user_admin/erasure-retry-completes | An erasure stopped part-way MUST leave no usable account, and MUST be completed by running it again, with or without a transaction coordinator. Access ends FIRST (`BeginUserErasure`: disabled, password unusable, email proof cleared so no OAuth link-by-email can attach, tokens and devices revoked), so a run stopped at any later point — and whatever the person does in the meantime, a new reset link or a re-enrolled authenticator — signs nobody in; the erasers run next; `EraseUser` (which stamps `erased_at` and replaces the address the invitation eraser looks for) runs last. Every step is an idempotent UPDATE, and an account already erased skips `EraseUser` alone and still runs every other step. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestEraseUsers_APartialErasureLeavesNoUsableAccount`, `TestEraseUsers_ARetryCompletesAFailedErasureWithoutATransaction`, `TestEraseUsers_AnErasedAccountStillGetsEveryEraser`, `TestEraseUsers_IsIdempotent` |
| user_admin/erased-domain-reserved | No path but an erasure MAY give an account an address in `erased.invalid`: SignUp, ConfirmSignUp (a registration parked before the refusal), the OAuth account creation, the admin's update (`AuthService.UpdateAccount`, which the Users page binds instead of `AuthMutation.UpdateUser`) and CreateBot refuse it with `InvalidArgument` / `EMAIL_INVALID` on `email`. Otherwise an account holding `erased-<id>@erased.invalid` first would block the erasure of account `<id>` for good (the address is unique). | Security | `TestRefuseReservedEmail`, `TestSignUp_RefusesTheErasedDomain`, `TestConfirmSignUp_RefusesTheErasedDomain`, `TestOAuthAccountCreation_RefusesTheErasedDomain`, `TestUpdateAccount_RefusesTheErasedDomain`, `TestCreateBot_RefusesTheErasedDomain`, `TestAdminPagesEraseAndRevokeNeverDelete` (the page binds UpdateAccount). Project code calling `AuthMutation.UpdateUser` / `CreateUser` directly is not covered. |
| user_admin/erased-cannot-sign-in | An erased account MUST be refused at every sign-in path: by its old address (no account has it), by the placeholder (no password verifies against `!`), and on every path that issues a session to an existing account by id (VerifyMfa, OAuthExchange, CliToken: `refuseUnsignableAccount`, the account is disabled), and every token it held MUST be refused as an expired one. `EnableUsers` and `UpdateUser` leave it alone, so it cannot become usable again. | Security | `TestEraseUsers_TheAccountCannotSignInAnyMore`, `TestEraseUsers_PseudonymisesAndKeepsEveryRow` (`assertRefusedLikeExpired`), `TestEraseStatements` |
| user_admin/erased-address-reusable | After an erasure the old address MUST be free: a new sign-up with it succeeds and creates a new account. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestEraseUsers_TheAddressCanSignUpAgain` |
| user_admin/erase-idempotent | Erasing an erased account MUST change nothing and MUST NOT fail: the handler reads `erased_at` first, and the statement's `erased_at IS NULL` guard settles a race (NotFound = done, and no feature eraser runs). | Consistency & concurrency | `TestEraseUsers_IsIdempotent` |
| user_admin/admin-revoke-records-operator | The Sessions page's `delete` (a revocation) MUST record the operator as `revoked_by` (reason ADMIN_REVOKED), keep the row listed as revoked, and be a no-op on a row already revoked. An empty selection MUST be refused. | Security | `TestRevokeSessions_TheOperatorRevokesAndARevokedRowIsANoOp`, `TestRevokeSessionAndEraseAccount_TheDeleteSlots`, `TestAdminPagesEraseAndRevokeNeverDelete` |
| user_admin/devices-page | The Devices page MUST list every Device row of every account — revoked and legacy (keyless) ones included, nothing is filtered — the newest first, cursor-paged on `(created_at, id)` (immutable: every sign-in rewrites `last_seen_at`, and a keyset over it would skip or repeat rows while paging; last seen is a column), with the owner's address, label, key thumbprint (blank on a legacy row), address, first seen (`created_at`), last seen, `trusted_at`, the account's `device_trust_revoked_at` and `trusted` (the sign-in's rule: `trusted_at` later than that revocation, and the device not revoked), and `revoked_at`. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestDevicesAdminStatements`, `TestAdminPages_DevicesAndTheSessionsDeviceColumn` (every column and detail field exists on the projection; no delete, no update) |
| user_admin/admin-device-revoke | The Devices page's revoke MUST end the device and every token bound to it in one transaction per device, record the operator, leave a device already revoked as it is (no transaction opened) except for ending the API tokens it created (`RevokeApprovedApiTokens`, idempotent), treat an unknown id as done, refuse a failed device read instead of revoking blind, and refuse an empty selection. A legacy device is revoked like any other: it can never be used, and revoking it records the operator's decision (`device/revoke-device-ends-everything`). | Security | `TestRevokeDevices_RevokesTheDeviceAndEveryTokenOnIt`, `TestRevokeDevices_ARevokedDeviceIsANoOp`, `TestRevokeDevices_ALegacyDeviceIsRevokedLikeAnyOther`, `TestRevokeDevices_OneTransactionPerDevice`, `TestRevokeDevices_AFailedReadRefuses` |
| user_admin/sessions-show-the-device | The Sessions list MUST show each token's device (its label, as text — the Devices page is opt-in and a column ref cannot be gated; its key thumbprint) and whether it is a public-computer session; the detail MUST also show, for a CLI session, the device of the browser session that approved it. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestSessionsAdminStatements_ShowTheDevice`, `TestAdminPages_DevicesAndTheSessionsDeviceColumn` |
| user_admin/no-secret-projection | Admin reads MUST NOT return `password_hash` or any token secret or hash. | Security | `TestEveryUserProjectionFillsTheFieldsItsGatesRead` covers the projections that are present. Absence of secrets: none (schema/DQL only). |
| user_admin/email-change-drops-proof | An email change MUST clear every proof of address ownership recorded for the previous address. | Security | none. Partly violated, see gap 3. |
| user_admin/email-canonical | An email written through the admin MUST be stored in the same canonical (trimmed, lower-cased) form that sign-in looks up. | Consistency & concurrency | none. **Violated**, see gap 2. |
| user_admin/revoke-token-immediate | `RevokeUserToken` MUST make that token fail the next authentication. | Consistency & concurrency | none (live) |
| user_admin/no-self-lockout | An operator MUST NOT be able to disable or erase their own account (`OWN_ACCOUNT`), nor the last account able to administer the realm (`LAST_ADMIN`), both FailedPrecondition before anything is written. An account able to administer the realm is one with a live grant of a live wildcard role (`all_permissions`, any organization or none) of the session realm, neither disabled nor erased (`ListRealmSuperadmins`; a machine account's API-realm wildcard does not count). The check and the writes are ONE transaction: the administrators are read (`ListRealmSuperadmins`), their User rows locked in id order (`LockUsers`, SELECT … FOR UPDATE — a JOIN-shaped read cannot carry the lock) and read again, so two operators disabling each other at once run one after the other and the second sees what the first disabled. A realm with no such account, or without `rbac`, refuses nothing. The admin's Disable action is `DisableAccounts`; the storage bulk form `DisableUsers` is not held to it. | Failure & configuration | `TestDisableAccounts_RefusesTheOperatorsOwnAccount`, `TestDisableAccounts_KeepsTheLastAdministrator` (a disabled administrator does not count), `TestDisableAccounts_ARealmWithoutAdministratorsRefusesNothing`, `TestEraseUsers_KeepsTheOperatorAndTheLastAdministrator`. **Unit (mock) only** for the race: two parallel disables need a live database to show the lock serialising them. |
| user_admin/listing-bounded | `ListUsers`, `ListUserTokens`, `ListSignInEvents` and `ListAllDevices` MUST be paged. | Performance | Each response declares `w17.Paging` (cursor-paged by the admin list handler); `TestDevicesAdminStatements` (the Devices order the keyset seeks on). No unit test pages them. |

**Erasure, table by table.** Nothing is deleted; what is personal is replaced, blanked or spent.

| table | feature | on erasure | why |
|---|---|---|---|
| `User` | base | `email` → `erased-<id>@erased.invalid`; `password_hash` → `!`; `email_verified_at` → NULL; `erased_at`, `disabled_at`, `device_trust_revoked_at` stamped | The row is the account's history and the target of every reference; the placeholder is unique, non-routable and frees the address. |
| `UserToken` | base | revoked (USER_ERASED, operator) | Tokens hold no personal data beyond the link; they are history. `public_computer` is a flag and `approved_by_device_id` a reference to a Device row, whose own personal columns are blanked below. |
| `Device` | base | revoked; `label` (user agent) and `ip` blanked | The user agent and address are personal; the key thumbprint is not, and keeping it keeps the key refused. |
| `OAuthIdentity` | oauth | `external_id` → the row's own id; `email` blanked | The provider subject and asserted email are personal; replacing the subject unlinks the identity and frees it for a new sign-up. |
| `OAuthLoginCode` | oauth | spent (`consumed_at`); `email`, `external_id` blanked — the account's codes by `user_id`, and the codes of the sign-in that created it (written with `user_id` NULL, never back-filled) by the `(provider_id, external_id)` of one of its identities, before the identities lose their subject | A live code must not redeem; the copied identity is personal. |
| `UserTotpSecret` | two_factor | `seed`, `secret` → NULL; `confirmed_at` → NULL | The seed is a credential of the person; the row stays. |
| `UserRecoveryCode` | two_factor | every unused code spent (`used_at`) | Hashes of credentials; spent so none redeems. |
| `MfaChallenge` | two_factor | pending challenges consumed | No personal data; consumed so none completes a sign-in. |
| `PasswordResetToken` | password_reset | every live link spent | A link would set a password on the erased account. |
| `EmailVerificationToken` | email_verification | every live link spent | A link would stamp the erased account verified. |
| `OrgInvite` | org_invite | `email` = the old address (normalised; skipped when the account already holds its placeholder) → NULL, pending and accepted; a pending one is first ended (`expires_at` → NOW()) | The address is personal. NULL, not a placeholder: the partial unique `(org_id, email) WHERE accepted_at IS NULL AND closed_at IS NULL` would make a placeholder collide when the organization re-invited the address between two attempts, blocking the erasure; NULL never collides. A NULL pending invitation would be OPEN, so it is ended first (binding and accepting require `expires_at > NOW()`); an accepted one keeps its record. |
| `PendingSignUp` | sign_up_confirmation | every row for the old address (normalised; skipped when the account already holds its placeholder): an open one is ended (`superseded_at`), then `email` → the placeholder, `password_hash` and `code_hash` → `!` (`ErasePendingSignUpAddress`) | Pending rows are kept now (`sign_up_confirmation/registration-ends-as-a-state`) — the account's own claimed registration and any superseded, expired or open ones for the address, a stranger's included — and each holds the address and hashes. A row created after the erasure is the person's new registration and is not touched. Proved by `TestEraseUsers_PseudonymisesAndKeepsEveryRow` (the eraser runs with the address and placeholder), `TestEraseUsers_AnErasedAccountStillGetsEveryEraser` (not with the placeholder), `TestEveryFormerDeleteIsAGuardedStateChange` (the statement). |
| `SignInEvent` | base | the account's rows, and the rows that reached no account but named its address (normalised): `address` → the placeholder, `ip` and `user_agent` blanked (`EraseSignInEvents`). Time, method, outcome, `public_computer`, the account link and the device key stay. | The address, the client address and the user agent are personal; the rest is the account's security history, kept pseudonymous. A row that reached no account but named the address is as often the person's own (before the account existed, a typo of the password) as anyone's, and it carries their address, so it is erased too — matched by the address the account held at the erasure. Addresses it held before an `UpdateAccount` change are not stored anywhere, so rows that reached no account under such an earlier address are not matched and keep it. A row written after the erasure about an account-less address is not touched; one that reaches the erased account is written pseudonymised (`device/sign-in-history-after-erasure`). Proved by `TestEraseUsers_PseudonymisesTheSignInHistory`, `TestEraseUsers_PseudonymisesAndKeepsEveryRow` (the eraser runs), `TestSignInEventIsAppendOnly` (the statement). |
| `CliAuthCode` | cli_login | untouched | No personal data (`approved_by_device_id` is a reference to a Device row); CliToken refuses the disabled account. |
| `UserRole`, `OrgMembership`, `BootstrapClaim`, `TokenPermission` | rbac, org_membership, api_token | untouched | No personal data; grants are refused through the disabled account. Their delete paths are a separate change. |

**Gaps / suspected defects**
1. **Fixed in rc.25: authentication did not check `disabled_at`.** "Disabled" was enforced only by deleting tokens, so a token minted after the disable (a racing `SignIn`, `IssueBotToken` for a disabled bot) worked. `GetTokenWithType` and `GetUserByTokenWithPermissions` now project the owner's `disabled_at`, and both resolvers refuse a disabled account.
2. **Fixed: admin email edits were not normalized.** `UpdateAccount` stored the address as typed, so a mixed-case one could never sign in and slipped past the case-sensitive unique index beside its lower-case twin. It is lower-cased and trimmed as at sign-up, and so is a machine account's (`CreateBot`) (`TestUpdateAccount_NormalizesTheAddress`, `TestCreateBot_NormalizesTheAddress`).
3. **Fixed: the proof survived an email change.** `UpdateUser` cleared `email_verified_at` but kept `email_proof`, so a `DERIVED_FROM_INVITE` carried over to the new address and, under `email_proof_required=derived`, let the account accept an OPEN invitation on an address it never proved. Both go now, and the erasure's first step (`BeginUserErasure`) drops the proof the same way (`TestEraseStatements`).
4. **Fixed: deletion left dependent rows pointing at nothing.** Accounts are erased, not deleted, so every reference (`UserRole`, `OrgMembership`, `OrgInvite.invited_by`) still names a row; admin lists show the placeholder address. An erased member keeps its org memberships and role grants (refused through the disabled account); revoking them is the roles/org change.
5. **Fixed: no last-admin or self guard.** The admin's Disable action (`DisableAccounts`), `EraseUsers` and `EraseAccount` refuse the operator's own account and the realm's last administrator (`user_admin/no-self-lockout`).
6. **Fixed: disable and enable are events.** `DisableAccounts` / `EnableAccounts` emit `UserDisabled` / `UserEnabled` per account they change, with the operator and their device (`TestDisableAndEnableAccounts_EmitPerAccountChanged`). The storage bulk forms `DisableUsers` / `EnableUsers` emit nothing.
7. **Fixed in rc.25: a stale comment contradicted the behaviour.** `User.disabled_at` in `proto/types/models.proto` said existing tokens keep working after a disable.

---

### admin_sign_ins, admin_devices

**Purpose.** The two admin pages that arrived with device identity: `SignIns` (`admin_sign_ins`: the sign-in history of every account, read-only, every outcome, cursor-paged) and `Devices` (`admin_devices`: every device of every account, with the `Revoke` action). They are features of their own, off by default, because an admin that declares `nav` groups must list every page, the pages a plugin contributes included: a page added to `user_admin` would break every such adopter on upgrade. To get one, enable the feature and add the page to the admin nav if it declares groups.

**Adds.** The page only (the preset's `feature:`). The queries and the action they bind — `ListSignInEvents`, `GetSignInEvent`, `ListAllDevices`, `GetDevice`, `RevokeDevices` — are `user_admin`'s, and their invariants are listed there (`user_admin/devices-page`, `user_admin/admin-device-revoke`, `user_admin/listing-bounded`). No Go file, table, column, REST route or env.

**Requires / conflicts / combined files.** Both require `user_admin`. No conflicts.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| admin_pages/opt-in | An activation that enables neither feature MUST get no `SignIns` or `Devices` page, and no page any activation gets MUST link to a page its own gate does not guarantee (a column ref cannot be gated, and a ref to an absent page fails the admin build). | Failure & configuration | `TestAdminPages_NewPagesAreOptIn` |

### admin_mfa

**Purpose.** The way back for a person who lost both the phone and the recovery codes. With every sign-in challenged, the authenticator is the only proof such an account accepts. The `Authenticators` admin page lists every enrolment (never the seed), and its `Reset` action ends the selected live authenticators and the accounts' unused recovery codes, as `DisableTotp` does — `TotpDisabled` carries the operator's device. The next sign-in falls back to the mailed code, and the person sets up a new authenticator.

**Adds.** Queries `ListAuthenticators` and `GetAuthenticator` (`AuthenticatorAdminRow`). Service method `ResetAuthenticators`. The page. No table, column, REST route or env. Go file: `handlers/admin_mfa.go`.

**Requires / conflicts / combined files.** Requires `user_admin` and `two_factor`. No conflicts.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| admin_mfa/reset-ends-the-factor | `ResetAuthenticators` MUST end the live authenticator AND the unused recovery codes of each selected enrolment's account, in one transaction per account. An ended enrolment, or an id naming nothing, MUST be left alone; an empty selection MUST be refused. Nothing MUST be deleted. | Security | `TestResetAuthenticators` |

### my_sign_ins, client_secret_rotation

**Purpose.** The two REST endpoints that arrived with device identity: `GET /auth/sign-ins` (`my_sign_ins`: `ListMySignInEvents`, the caller's own sign-in history) and `POST /auth/clients/{id}/rotate-secret` (`client_secret_rotation`: `RotateClientSecret`). They are features of their own, off by default, because an endpoint a feature with adopters starts publishing makes every adopter's code generation scaffold an e2e test for it, a deliberately failing placeholder until someone writes it.

**Adds.** The endpoint only (the preset's `feature:`). The methods stay with `authenticate_turnkey` and `cli_login`, whose invariants cover them (`device/sign-in-history-own-rows`, `cli_login/client-secret-rotation`). No Go file, table, column or env.

**Requires / conflicts / combined files.** `my_sign_ins` requires `authenticate_turnkey`, `client_secret_rotation` requires `cli_login`. No conflicts.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| surface/upgrade-adds-nothing | Against the last release (rc.23), no preset page, page slot, page action or REST/MCP endpoint MAY disappear, and every one added MUST be gated by a feature that release did not have and that is off by default — except an addition the README's upgrade guide names as required (`POST /auth/oauth/exchange`). A slot MAY be rebound to another method of the same request key and response shape. | Failure & configuration | `TestPresetSurface_AnUpgradeRemovesNothingAndAddsOnlyOptIn` |

### oauth_form_post

**Purpose.** The OAuth callback for a provider that comes back by an HTML form POST (`OAuthProvider.response_mode` `form_post` — Sign in with Apple, which requires it whenever the scopes ask for the address): `POST /auth/oauth/{provider}/callback` (`OAuthCallbackForm`), the same callback as the GET with `code` and `state` in an `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` body, answered with the same 302 (a login code in the fragment, never a session).

**Adds.** The endpoint only (the preset's `feature:`). The method stays with `oauth`, whose invariants cover it (`oauth/response-mode`, `oauth/callback-carries-no-session`, `oauth/state-*`). Apple's `user` form field (the name, sent on a first sign-in only) is not read.

**Requires / conflicts / combined files.** Requires `oauth`. No conflicts, no Go files. The gateway must decode a form body into the request (platform: REST body negotiation).

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| oauth_form_post/opt-in | The route MUST be published only where `oauth_form_post` is on (`surface/upgrade-adds-nothing`). | Compatibility | `TestPresetSurface_AnUpgradeRemovesNothingAndAddsOnlyOptIn` |

### org_members_rest

**Purpose.** A member's management over the REST preset, beside the member list, for a UI: `PUT /auth/orgs/{org}/members/{id}/role` (`UpdateOrgMembershipRole`) and `DELETE /auth/orgs/{org}/members/{id}` (`RemoveOrgMembership`).

**Adds.** The two endpoints only (the preset's `feature:`). The methods stay with `org_membership`, whose invariants cover them (`org_membership/role-change-ceiling`, `org_membership/removal-ceiling`, `org_membership/last-superadmin`, `org_membership/path-header-agree`). `OrgMember.membership_id`, which a UI addresses them by, is `org_membership`'s.

**Requires / conflicts / combined files.** Requires `org_membership` and `org_invite` (the role vocabulary a role picker needs, `ListAssignableRoles`). No conflicts, no Go files. Changing one's own role or removing oneself is allowed under the ceiling, except for the organization's last superadmin (`LAST_SUPERADMIN`). Every membership is managed the same way, its creator's included.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| org_members_rest/opt-in | The two routes MUST be published only where `org_members_rest` is on (`surface/upgrade-adds-nothing`). | Compatibility | `TestPresetSurface_AnUpgradeRemovesNothingAndAddsOnlyOptIn` |

### org_membership

**Purpose.** Adds multi-organization membership. One identity belongs to many `Organization`s through `OrgMembership`. Each request acts in one validated active organization, and the caller's grants are narrowed to that organization. An organization has no owner: what anyone may do in it, its creator included, comes from the roles they hold there.

**Adds**
- Tables: `Organization` (unique `(slug, ended_key)`; `deleted_at`; field 6, the former `owner_id`, reserved and its column dropped), `OrgMembership` (unique `(user_id, org_id, ended_key)`; `removed_at`, `removed_by`). Nothing in them is deleted (`org_membership/removal-is-a-state`).
- Columns: `UserRole.org_id` (indexed; partial unique indexes, with `ended_key`, for realm-wide and org-scoped grants), `Role.org_scoped`.
- Queries: `ListUserOrgs`, `ListUserOrgGrants`, `GetUserOrgPermissions`, `GetUserRealmWidePermissions`, `ListOrgSuperadmins`, `LockOrganization`, `GetUserOrgBySlug`, `ListOrgScopedRoles`, `ListOrgMemberAccounts`, `GetOrgMember`, `ListOrganizations`, `GetOrganization`, `ListOrgMemberships`, `GetOrgMembership`, `ListOrgMembersByOrg`.
- Mutations: `CreateOrganization`, `UpdateOrganization` (live organizations only), `DeleteOrganization` (stamps `deleted_at`, removes the memberships and revokes the org grants; the admin page reaches it through `AuthService.RemoveOrganization`, which also closes the organization's invitations under `org_invite`), `AddOrgMembership`, `UpdateOrgMembership` (row-locked; revokes the member's org grants, the caller as `revoked_by`, and writes the new one), `MarkOrgMembershipRemoved` (was `DeleteOrgMembership`; row-locked; stamps `removed_at` / `removed_by` and revokes the org grants).
- Business RPCs: `ListMyOrgs`, `ListOrgMembers` (each member with its `membership_id`; a page at a time — `page_size`, `page_token`, `next_page_token`), `UpdateOrgMembershipRole`, `RemoveOrgMembership` (these two are held to the role ceiling and the last-superadmin rule, and take the path's `org`), `RemoveOrganization` (the Organizations page's delete).
- REST: `GET /auth/orgs`, `GET /auth/orgs/{org}/members` (and, with `org_members_rest`, the member's role change and removal). Both need a bearer token, and neither is `public`. The `{org}` group uses `org_from_path`, so the path slug becomes the `W17-Org` selector before authentication.
- Auth header `W17-Org`. Scope keys `org_id` and `org_slug`. `WhoAmI` gains `orgs` and `active_org_id`.
- Admin pages `Organizations` and `OrgMemberships` (update and delete go through the ceiling-checked service methods).
- Events: `OrganizationCreated`, `OrganizationUpdated`, `OrganizationDeleted`, `OrgMembershipAdded`, `OrgMembershipUpdated`, `OrgMembershipRemoved`.
- Env: none.

**Requires / conflicts / combined files.** Requires `authenticate_turnkey` and `rbac`. Go files: `handlers/org_membership.go`, `handlers/role_ceiling.go`, `handlers/org_membership_role.go`. Combined with `service_account`: `handlers/service_account_org.go`. It can coexist with `tenant_scope`, but a project is expected to pick one model.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| org_membership/selector-validated | A client-sent organization selector (header or path) MUST resolve to an organization only if the caller holds a live membership in it — no other claim selects one, whoever created the organization. Otherwise authentication MUST fail with a distinct "not a member" refusal. | Security | `TestResolveOrgScope`, `TestResolveOrgScope_WithoutAMembershipNobodySelectsTheOrg`, `TestOrgNotMember_SaysWhatIsWrong`, `TestResolveOrgScope_HeaderLookupIsCaseInsensitive`, `TestResolveOrgScope_SlugFromHeadersWithoutMetadata` |
| org_membership/path-header-agree | A request whose `org` field names a different organization from the one authentication resolved MUST be refused. An empty field MUST defer to authentication. | Security | `TestRefuseOtherOrg`, `TestManageMembership_RefusesAnotherOrgInTheRequest`, `TestRefuseOtherOrgDefersWhenNoOrgWasResolved` |
| org_membership/sole-org-inference-only | With no selector, the active organization MUST be inferred only when the caller belongs to exactly one. With zero or several it MUST stay unset. | Security | `TestResolveOrgScope_SoleOrgInference`, `TestResolveOrgScope_MultipleOrgsStayAmbiguous`, `TestResolveOrgScope_HeaderWinsOverInference`, `TestResolveOrgScope_NoSelectorFallsBackToInference`, `TestResolveOrgScope_SoleOrgInferenceAlsoStampsTheSlug` |
| org_membership/realm-call-acts-realm-wide | A backoffice method that refuses inside an organization (packages, hosts, sign-up policies, the products' roles and plans — every `refuse…InsideOrg`) MUST act realm-wide for a caller who holds it through a realm-wide role (its permission, or a wildcard), whatever organization the request acts in — named or the caller's only one inferred; a caller who holds it only inside an organization MUST be refused as before. Authenticate MUST NOT decide it (its answer is cached per credential, not per method): the method asks the store (`heldRealmWide`). | Security | `TestBackofficeCallFromInsideAnOrg_HeldRealmWide`, `TestRealmOnlyRefusals_AskWhetherTheCallIsHeldRealmWide`; live: `examples/product-roles-proof` |
| org_membership/realm-permissions-scope | A request acting inside an organization whose credential holds roles realm-wide (granted with no organization, carried by this credential) MUST carry the scope `realm_permissions`: `*` when one is a wildcard, otherwise the permission ids they grant, sorted and comma-separated; no other request MAY carry it, and it adds no permission. It depends on the credential and the organization only, never on the method called, so the gateway's auth cache keeps it right. | Security | `TestNarrowGrants_MarksTheRealmPermissions`; live: `examples/grant-proof` |
| org_membership/sole-org-inference-switch | With `org_from_sole_membership` false, a request naming no organization MUST act in none (and the memberships MUST NOT be read for it); through an audience, only `TENANT_SOURCE_DEFAULT` MAY infer. | Security | `TestResolveOrgScope_TenantFromHost` (the switched-off cases), `TestResolveOrgScope_SoleInferenceOffReadsNothing` |
| org_membership/tenant-from | Through an audience naming `tenant_from`, the sources MUST be tried in order and the first that names an organization decides; a bound host MUST decide over the selector, and a selector naming another organization MUST be refused (`ORG_HOST_CONFLICT`); a selector on an audience whose sources omit `DEFAULT` MUST be refused (`ORG_SELECTOR_NOT_ACCEPTED`); the caller MUST still hold a membership (of an admitted kind) in the organization named. Naming the host without `org_hosts` MUST fail the request. | Security | `TestResolveOrgScope_TenantFromHost` (with and without kinds), `TestResolveOrgScope_HostWithoutOrgHosts` |
| org_membership/grants-narrowed-to-active-org | With an active organization, grants MUST be limited to realm-wide non-org-scoped roles plus roles assigned in that organization. With none, they MUST be realm-wide roles only. | Security | `TestNarrowGrants_ActiveOrg`, `TestNarrowGrants_NoActiveOrg`. Unit (mock) only for the role selection itself: the `GetUserOrgPermissions` predicate is SQL. A live test must give a user an org-scoped admin role in A and show that acting in B grants nothing from it. |
| org_membership/intersect-on-roles | A permission MUST survive only if one single role grants it and that role passes both the realm axis and the organization axis. | Security | `TestNarrowGrants_APermissionTwoDifferentRolesGrantDoesNotSurvive`, `TestNarrowGrants_WildcardRoleMustPassTheOrgAxis` |
| org_membership/admin-plane-realm-only | `AuthenticateAdmin` (the admin `user_lookup`) MUST select no organization, from a header, the context or sole-org inference. Only realm-wide roles survive. `Authenticate` MUST keep selecting one. | Security | `TestResolveOrgScope_TheAdminPlaneSelectsNoOrganization`, `TestAuthenticateAdmin_MarksTheAdminPlaneAndAuthenticateDoesNot`, `TestNarrowGrants_NoActiveOrg` |
| org_membership/no-ownership | Nothing MUST grant authority for having created an organization. `Organization` declares no owner (field 6, `owner_id`, reserved), no statement reads one, and a principal holding no role in the active organization MUST hold nothing there, for a session and an API token alike. `UserOrg.owner` and `OrgMember.owner` stay on the wire, deprecated, and MUST always be false. | Security | `TestNarrowGrants_NoRoleMeansNothingWhoeverCreatedTheOrg`, `TestOrganizationHasNoOwner`, `TestOwnerFlagsAreAlwaysFalse` |
| org_membership/role-change-ceiling | A role change MUST be refused unless the caller holds every permission of the new role and covers every role the member currently holds in that organization. | Security | `TestUpdateOrgMembershipRole_RefusesARoleAboveTheCaller`, `TestMembership_TheMemberAboveTheCallerIsNotTheirsToChange` |
| org_membership/removal-ceiling | A removal MUST be refused unless the caller covers every role the member holds in that organization. | Security | `TestMembership_TheMemberAboveTheCallerIsNotTheirsToChange` |
| org_membership/no-bot-role-for-a-person | A membership MUST NOT carry an API-realm role: the org-role catalogue MUST NOT offer one, and naming one MUST be refused (`ROLE_IS_FOR_BOTS`). | Security | `TestOrgRoleCatalogue_IsTheOrganizationsAndHoldsNoBotRole` |
| org_membership/last-superadmin | No change MUST leave an organization that has a superadmin without one (`LAST_SUPERADMIN`, FailedPrecondition, before anything is written): a member's role change or removal (`UpdateOrgMembershipRole`, `RemoveOrgMembership` — the member themselves included, the organization's creator included) and, with `teams`, a team member's removal, a change of a team's roles and a team's deletion (`RemoveOrgTeamMember`, `SetOrgTeamRoles`, `DeleteOrgTeam`). A superadmin is a member holding a wildcard role there, directly or through a team, and there is ONE definition of who counts (`orgSuperadmins`): a live grant (direct, or through a live team, team membership and team role grant) of a live wildcard role, on a live membership, of an account that is neither disabled nor erased, of a session-realm role (`ListOrgSuperadmins`, `ListOrgTeamSuperadmins`; the account's stamps are projected behind `?feature(user_admin)`, a direct grant's realm behind `?feature(api_token)`, and dropped in the handler — a bot's API-realm wildcard administers nothing through a session, and `SetBotRoles` revokes it unasked). Each change asks it with the grants it ends: a role change ends the member's direct grants (a wildcard new role keeps them one; a team's wildcard stays), a removal ends their team-held ones too, a team member's removal that member's grants through the team, a team's deletion — or a role change that leaves it no wildcard — every grant through the team. The check and the write are ONE transaction that first locks the organization's row (`LockOrganization`, SELECT … FOR UPDATE; `withOrgLock`, as every change to who holds what there), so two such changes in one organization run one after the other and the second one's check sees what the first ended — checked before the write's transaction, two superadmins demoting each other at once both passed and left none. `SetOrgTeamRoles` reads the team's current roles under the lock as well, so two of them on one team diff one after the other. `RemoveOrganization` takes the same lock first, so it and such a change take their row locks in one order and cannot deadlock. Without a coordinator (no `DistTx`) every statement commits on its own and the lock holds for none of them. An organization with no superadmin already refuses nothing. An operator's `DisableAccounts` / `EraseUsers` is not held to it, by design: they are realm-level actions, and an operator may disable an organization's last superadmin deliberately; they have the realm's own rule instead (`user_admin/no-self-lockout`). Every other membership, the creator's too, is changed or removed under the role ceiling alone. | Integrity | `TestRefuseLastSuperadmin`, `TestLastSuperadmin_CannotDemoteOrRemoveThemselves`, `TestRefuseLastSuperadmin_TeamHeldWildcards`, `TestOrgSuperadmins_DropsDisabledAndErasedAccounts`, `TestTeamMethods_KeepTheLastSuperadmin`, `TestSuperadminQueries_ReadOnlyLiveGrantsAndProjectTheAccountsStamps`, `TestManageMembership_TheCreatorsMembershipIsManagedLikeAnyOther`, `TestSuperadminChanges_CheckAndWriteUnderTheOrganizationsLock` (the lock, the read and the write in one transaction, a refusal rolled back). **Unit (statement) only** for the queries' liveness; **unit (mock) only** for the race: two parallel demotions need a live database to show the lock serialising them. |
| org_membership/cross-org-management-refused | A caller acting in organization A MUST NOT change or remove a membership in organization B. | Security | `TestMembership_AnotherOrganizationsMemberIsRefused` |
| org_membership/org-scoped-roles-only | A membership MUST carry only an org-scoped role, with distinct refusals for an unknown name (`UNKNOWN_ORG_ROLE`) and a realm-wide role (`ROLE_NOT_ORG_SCOPED`). The role MUST be applied by the ID that was checked. | Security | `TestRoleIDByName_RefusesARealmRole`, `TestRoleIDByName_AnUnknownNameIsATypoNotAScopingProblem`, `TestRoleIDByName_AcceptsAnOrgScopedRole`, `TestAssignableRolesAndValidatorCannotDisagree`, `TestUpdateOrgMembershipRole_GrantsTheCheckedRoleByID`, `TestUpdateOrgMembershipRole_OtherRefusals` |
| org_membership/ceiling-fails-closed | A grant decision with no resolved permission envelope (a direct gRPC call) MUST be refused. | Failure & configuration | `TestCheckMayGrantRole_NoEnvelopeFailsClosed`, `TestUpdateOrgMembershipRole_OtherRefusals` |
| org_membership/label-and-grant-move-together | A role change MUST replace the member's org-scoped grants and the membership label in one transaction. A removal MUST revoke the org-scoped grants with the membership. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestEveryFormerDeleteIsAGuardedStateChange` (the legs of both mutations), `TestRemoveOrgMembership_KeepsTheRowsAndTheMemberCanComeBack`, `TestUpdateOrgMembershipRole_KeepsTheReplacedGrantsAndCanGoBack` (through a fake that applies the legs). **Unit (mock) only:** a live test must demote a member and show the old role's permissions are gone. |
| org_membership/removal-is-a-state | Removing a member, changing a member's role and deleting an organization MUST NOT delete a row. The membership is stamped `removed_at` / `removed_by` (the caller through `RemoveOrgMembership`), a replaced or removed grant `revoked_at` / `revoked_by` (the caller through `UpdateOrgMembershipRole` and `RemoveOrgMembership`; NULL from `DeleteOrganization`, which the admin page binds directly), an organization `deleted_at`. Each ending releases the row's unique key (`ended_key` = id), so a removed member MAY be invited back and accept, the same role MAY be granted again, and a new organization MAY take a deleted one's slug — each as a new row beside the ended one. Every reader (`ListUserOrgs`, `GetUserOrgBySlug`, `ListUserOrgGrants`, the permission queries, the member and admin lists) reads live rows only: a removed membership and a deleted organization are invisible, cannot be selected, and grant nothing. A second removal or deletion matches nothing (NotFound), as before. | Security | `TestRemoveOrgMembership_KeepsTheRowsAndTheMemberCanComeBack`, `TestUpdateOrgMembershipRole_KeepsTheReplacedGrantsAndCanGoBack`, `TestEveryFormerDeleteIsAGuardedStateChange`, `TestEveryReaderSkipsEndedRows`, `TestUniqueIndexesLetAnEndedKeyBeReused`. **Unit (mock) only** for the predicates and the indexes. |
| org_membership/concurrent-role-changes-serialize | Two concurrent changes to one membership MUST leave the member holding exactly one org role, matching the label. | Consistency & concurrency | none. The `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` lock is declared in the mutation, but no test runs two transactions. |
| org_membership/ceiling-and-write-consistent | The ceiling check and the grant MUST see the same catalogue and the same member grants: every change to who holds what in an organization runs, from its first read, in one transaction holding the organization's row lock (`withOrgLock`; gap 4). | Consistency & concurrency | `TestWithOrgLock`, `TestUnderOrgLock`, `TestRoleChanges_RunUnderTheOrganizationsLock`, `TestCeiling_UnderTheLockJudgesTheCallersGrantsNow`, `TestCeiling_UnderTheLockCountsTeamsForASessionOnly`, `TestBotOrgLock_MarksTheTransactionAsUnderTheLock` (every such method, read off the source), `TestSuperadminChanges_CheckAndWriteUnderTheOrganizationsLock`. **Unit (mock) only** for the race: two parallel changes need a live database to show the lock serialising them. |
| org_membership/no-orphan-grants | Deleting an organization MUST end its memberships and org-scoped grants atomically (one statement list). | Consistency & concurrency | `TestEveryFormerDeleteIsAGuardedStateChange` (the three legs). **Unit (mock) only.** |
| org_membership/narrowing-fails-closed | A query error in the organization narrower MUST fail authentication. | Failure & configuration | `TestNarrowGrants_QueryErrorFailsClosed` |
| org_membership/whoami-degrades | A failure to list the caller's organizations MUST NOT fail `WhoAmI`. It MUST report no organizations instead. | Failure & configuration | `TestWhoAmI_SurvivesAFailingOrgQuery`, `TestWhoAmI_CarriesOrgsAndTheActiveOne`, `TestWhoAmI_NoActiveOrgIsReportedAsEmpty` |
| org_membership/members-paged | `ListOrgMembers` MUST answer at most one page (100 by default, 500 at most), ordered by address, with a `next_page_token` while members remain; walking the tokens MUST visit every member once, and a token the list did not issue MUST be refused. | Performance | `TestListOrgMembers_PagesVisitEveryMemberOnce`, `TestListOrgMembers_AFullLastPageHasNoNextToken`, `TestListOrgMembers_PageSizeIsBounded`, `TestListOrgMembers_AForeignTokenIsRefused` |
| org_membership/indexed-auth-path | The per-request lookups (membership by `(user_id, org_id)`, organization by `slug`, grants by `user_id` and `org_id`) MUST be served by indexes. | Performance | none (schema declares them; untested) |
| org_membership/bounded-auth-cost | Organization resolution MUST add a constant number of queries per request. | Performance | none. The current cost is up to 2 extra round trips (slug, then grants) plus one per organization grant source (teams), uncached. |

**Gaps / suspected defects**
1. **Fixed: ownership widened to the entire deployed permission lock.** An organization's owner (`Organization.owner_id`) was handed a synthetic `RoleGrant{AllPermissions: true}` in the active organization, expanded from the whole lock rather than from what applies inside the organization, so any endpoint not row-scoped by `org_id` passed (realm-wide administration through an app surface, every role ceiling, realm-level API roles through `CreateBot`). The admin surface was already closed (`AuthenticateAdmin` selects no organization). Ownership is gone: nothing grants anything for owning an organization (`org_membership/no-ownership`); whoever administers one holds a role there.
2. **Fixed: no in-plugin ownership lifecycle.** No plugin mutation ever wrote `Organization.owner_id`, so the authority it carried (gap 1, the selection of an organization without a membership, a membership nobody could change, the last-superadmin exemption) applied only to rows written by hand. The field is reserved and its column dropped; the last superadmin of an organization can never demote or remove themselves (`org_membership/last-superadmin`). With ownership gone that rule is what keeps an organization administered, and two holes in it are closed with it: a disabled or erased account's wildcard grant counted as "another superadmin", so the last ACTIVE one could leave; a wildcard held through a team was seen by neither side of the rule; and the team's own methods (`RemoveOrgTeamMember`, `SetOrgTeamRoles`, `DeleteOrgTeam`) could take the last one away. An operator's `DisableUsers` / `EraseUsers` stays outside the rule by design (`org_membership/last-superadmin`).
3. **Fixed (the products' roles); Decided (the rest).** A member holding the products' roles in the organization (`product_roles`) is managed only by a caller holding every permission of those roles there (`checkMemberProductRolesWithinCaller`), since a removal ends them. A member whose authority is realm-wide keeps it whatever happens to the membership: realm-wide roles are not the organization's. The last-superadmin rule counts wildcard roles; an organization administered only through non-wildcard roles is protected by the ceiling (whoever removes an administrator must cover their role), not against the last one leaving.
4. **Fixed: time-of-check versus time-of-use in role changes.** The ceiling and last-superadmin checks read the member's grants, the team's roles and the role catalogue outside the transaction that then wrote the change, so a concurrent promotion of the member, a widening of the target role, or a second superadmin's demotion between check and write was applied unchecked. Every change to who holds what in an organization now runs, from its first read, in one transaction that first locks the organization's row (`withOrgLock`, `LockOrganization`): the membership's role change and removal, the team methods, the organization's own roles (create, update, delete), an invitation and its acceptance, an open registration's join, the products' roles (in the organization and from the backoffice) and the organization's product plan (`AllowOrgProduct`, `DisallowOrgProduct`), and a machine account's roles, creation and token minting (the ceiling re-checked inside the mint's transaction). Two such changes in one organization run one after the other. Without a coordinator (no `DistTx`) the statements still commit one by one and the window stays; a realm-wide role (not the organization's) edited by an operator is not under any organization's lock. The caller's own permissions are re-read under the lock (`heldUnderOrgLock`): every ceiling compares against what Authenticate resolved for the request, less each permission whose grants the caller has all lost since (the active organization's and realm-wide roles, or realm-wide alone, and for a session a team's roles), so a caller demoted while their request waited for the lock no longer hands out what the demotion took — in a machine account's transaction as in `withOrgLock`'s. The re-read only removes; what Authenticate narrowed away stays away. It reads on role ids within the caller's realm, as Authenticate narrows (`callerRealmGrants`): a session's roles for a session, the token realms' (API and MCP) for any other caller — the envelope tells a session from a token, not an API token from an MCP one, so for either a role of the other token realm still keeps a permission (`TestCeiling_UnderTheLockCountsTheCallersRealm`).
5. **Fixed: sole-organization inference swallowed errors.** The inference still answers "no organization" when listing the caller's organizations fails (a login must not fail on it), and now logs why, so the scoped model's "missing required scope" that follows has a cause in the log.
6. **Fixed: unpaged lists.** The admin lists `ListOrganizations` and `ListOrgMemberships` are cursor-paged (their `w17.Paging`); an organization's own member list (`ListOrgMembers`) was the one left whole, and is now paged by address (`org_membership/members-paged`).
7. **Fixed: a slug change was announced without the slug.** `UpdateOrganization` can change `slug`, the selector clients send in `W17-Org` and in paths. `OrganizationUpdated` now carries the organization's `slug`, `name` and `kind` as they are after the change, so a subscriber that knows the organization by `org_id` learns its new handle; the old slug selects nothing (or, once another organization takes it, only what a membership there admits).

---

### membership_kinds

**Purpose.** Memberships of several kinds — the auth half of audience surfaces (a domain publishing one REST / MCP / gRPC surface per audience, each on its own gateway — `w17/audience.proto` in the SDK). A membership has ONE kind: a person is an organization's staff or its client, and may be staff of one organization and a client of another. An audience gateway names the kinds it admits in every `Authenticate`, and the plugin admits the caller only through a membership of one of them.

**Adds**
- Columns: `OrgMembership.kind`, `OrgInvite.kind` (CHAR 32, '' = no kind). The unique indexes do not change: one live membership per (user, organization), one pending invitation per (organization, address).
- `ListUserOrgs` reports each membership's `membership_kind`.
- `InviteToOrg.kind` (with `org_invite`): the kind of membership accepting creates. The kind must be one some audience admits (or '', a membership no kind-filtering gateway admits); a member may hand out the kinds the audiences admitting their own kind may invite (`w17_audiences`, from `MembershipPolicy.invites`, default the audience's own kinds); an inviter from outside the organization, any known kind (ownership widens nothing). Checked before anything is written. Refused: `INVITE_KIND_NOT_ALLOWED` (PermissionDenied).
- Env `w17_audiences`, generated by codegen from the domain's audiences and parsed at boot: each audience's admitted kinds, the variable carrying its MCP public URL, and the kinds it may invite. A malformed table, an unset URL variable or two audiences sharing a URL refuse to boot.
- With `oauth_server`: the consent page offers an MCP client only the organizations where the user holds a kind its resource's audience admits — none refuses at once, one is the only choice.
- Error code `MEMBERSHIP_NOT_ADMITTED` (PermissionDenied).
- Auth header `x-w17-membership-kinds` (gateway-owned, comma-separated).
- `AuthResp.membership_kind` (8): the kind of the caller's membership in the organization the request acts in (`scopes[org_id]`), the project's account type. The gateway copies it into every request record (`RequestEventMeta.membership_kind`).

**Requires / conflicts / combined files.** Requires `org_membership`. Go file: `handlers/membership_kinds.go`. Combined with `org_invite`: `handlers/membership_kinds_invite.go`; with `oauth_server`: `handlers/oauth_server_kinds.go`.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| membership_kinds/org-among-admitted | Through a gateway that names kinds, the active organization MUST be resolved among the caller's memberships of an admitted kind only: a selector, an OAuth token's organization and sole-organization inference all read the admitted set. | Security | `TestResolveOrgScopeByKind` |
| membership_kinds/not-admitted-is-403 | A caller with no admitted membership — none at all, or one of another kind in the organization named — MUST be refused PermissionDenied (`MEMBERSHIP_NOT_ADMITTED`) before any handler, distinct from "not a member". | Security | `TestResolveOrgScopeByKind`, `TestNotAdmittedSurvivesTheDecoratorFold` |
| membership_kinds/invite-within-kind | An invitation's kind MUST be one some audience admits, and one the inviter's audiences may invite unless the inviter is not a member of the organization; a refused invitation MUST write nothing. | Security | `TestCheckInviteKind` |
| membership_kinds/consent-by-kind | The consent page MUST offer, and an approval MUST name, only organizations where the user holds a kind the resource's audience admits. | Security | `TestOAuthConsent_OffersOnlyTheOrganizationsTheResourcesAudienceAdmits` |
| membership_kinds/table-fails-at-boot | A table that would turn the consent filter off silently — malformed, an unset MCP URL, one URL for two audiences — MUST refuse to boot. | Security | `TestParseAudienceTable` |
| membership_kinds/no-kinds-no-change | A gateway that names no kinds MUST resolve and narrow exactly as `org_membership` does. | Security | `TestResolveOrgScopeByKind` |
| membership_kinds/kind-of-the-request | `AuthResp.membership_kind` MUST be the kind of the caller's membership in the organization this request resolved, whichever way it was resolved; empty when the request acts in no organization. It MUST NOT be a property of the user (no last or default kind). | Consistency & concurrency | `TestAnswerMembershipKind` |
| membership_kinds/ungated-kinds | When no audience names a kind (no audience, or only audiences that take the tenant without gating on kind), an invitation, a kind change and a registration policy MUST accept any kind: the kinds are the project's account types, and nothing is gated on them. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestCheckInviteKind_NoAudienceNamesAKind`; live: `product-roles-proof` |
| membership_kinds/kind-change-within-caller | `UpdateOrgMembershipKind` MUST refuse a member the caller may not manage (the role ceiling, and the member's products' roles in the organization) and a kind the caller may not hand out there (as an invitation of it); a removed membership MUST NOT change. | Security | `TestCheckInviteKind` (the kind rule, shared); live: `audience-proof` |
| membership_kinds/header-gateway-owned | The admitted kinds MUST come only from the gateway: `x-w17-*` keys a client sends never reach `Authenticate`. | Security | none here; the platform gateway's reserved-key strip |

**Gaps / suspected defects**
1. **Fixed: no tenant from the host.** `org_hosts` binds a host to an organization, and an audience's `tenant_from: [TENANT_SOURCE_HOST, …]` takes the tenant from it; codegen accepts `tenant_from` since rc.35.
2. **Fixed: a member's kind was changed by removing and re-inviting them.** `UpdateOrgMembershipKind` changes it in place, under the role ceiling and the kinds the caller may hand out (`membership_kinds/kind-change-within-caller`).
