### org_invite

**Purpose.** Adds invitations to an organization with a named org-scoped role, delivered as a single-use secret link. An invitation is either bound to one address or open, meaning whoever holds the link picks the address. Accepting writes the membership and the org-scoped role grant together. The same table can gate sign-up (`invite_only`).

**Adds**
- Table `OrgInvite`: `token_hash` unique; nullable `email` (NULL = open); `role` (a Role name); `role_snapshot`; opaque `metadata` (JSON); `expires_at`; `accepted_at`; `closed_at`, `closed_by`. There is a partial unique index on `(org_id, email) WHERE accepted_at IS NULL AND closed_at IS NULL`. No invitation is deleted.
- Queries: `GetPendingOrgInviteByToken`, `ListOrgInvitesByOrg`, `ListPendingInvitesForEmail`.
- Mutations: `CreateOrgInvite`, `BindOpenOrgInvite`, `ConsumeOrgInviteByToken` (matches an open invitation or one bound to the accepting address), `CloseExpiredOrgInvite` (was `ClearExpiredOrgInvite`), `CloseOrgInvite` (was `DeleteOrgInvite`; scoped by `org_id`), `CloseOrgInvitesOfOrg` (an organization's deletion).
- Business RPCs: `ListAssignableRoles`, `InviteToOrg`, `ListOrgInvites`, `RevokeOrgInvite`, `ListMyInvites`, `AcceptOrgInvite`.
- REST: `GET /auth/orgs/{org}/roles`, `GET|POST /auth/orgs/{org}/invites`, `DELETE /auth/orgs/{org}/invites/{invite_id}`, `GET /auth/invites`, `POST /auth/invites/accept` (the token travels in the body, never in the path). All need a bearer token. None is `public` or `failed_only`.
- Sign-up seams: `signupInviteGate` (also consulted by OAuth account creation) and `signupClaimInvite`.
- Events: `OrgInviteCreated`, `OrgInviteAccepted` (emitted after commit), `OrgInviteRevoked`.
- Env: `invite_only` (default true), `email_proof_required` (`none` default, then `derived`, then `full`), `org_invite_ttl_hours` (default 168).

**Requires / conflicts / combined files.** Requires `org_membership` and `rbac`. Go file: `handlers/org_invite.go`. It interacts with `sign_up_turnkey`, `sign_up_confirmation` and `oauth` through the sign-up gate, and with `email_verification`, which provides the real `inviteVerifiedEmailGate` and `markEmailDerived`. Without `email_verification` both are no-ops.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| org_invite/invite-role-ceiling | `InviteToOrg` MUST refuse a role carrying any permission the inviter does not hold, and the refusal MUST name the rule. | Security | `TestInviteToOrg_RefusesARoleAboveTheInviter`, `TestInviteToOrg_ARoleAboveTheInviterNamesTheRule`, `TestRoleWithinCeiling`, `TestCheckMayGrantRole_ARoleYouDoNotHoldButCover` |
| org_invite/picker-equals-validator | `ListAssignableRoles` MUST offer exactly the org-scoped roles within the caller's ceiling, the same set `InviteToOrg` accepts. | Security | `TestListAssignableRoles_OffersOnlyRolesWithinTheCeiling`, `TestAssignableRolesAndValidatorCannotDisagree` |
| org_invite/snapshot-holds-at-accept | Acceptance MUST refuse, without spending the invitation, if the role is no longer the one checked at invite time or now carries a permission outside the stored snapshot. A role of `all_permissions` MUST be snapshotted as `<role>:*` and accepted while the role still grants all permissions, however the lock has grown; a role that stopped being one MUST be refused. A snapshot listing ids (any written before the wildcard spelling) MUST be held to its list. A missing snapshot MUST be refused. | Security | `TestCheckMayGrantRole_ReturnsTheSnapshotItChecked`, `TestCheckRoleWithinSnapshot`, `TestAcceptOrgInvite_RefusesARoleWidenedSinceTheInvitation`, `TestRoleSnapshot_AWildcardRoleOutlivesTheLockGrowing`, `TestRoleSnapshot_AnEnumeratedSnapshotOfAWildcardRoleIsHeldToItsList` |
| org_invite/secret-link | The invitation token MUST be returned to the inviter exactly once. Only its sha256 MUST be stored, and acceptance MUST look it up by hash. | Security | `TestInviteToOrg_ReturnsThePlaintextOnceAndStoresTheHash`, `TestAcceptOrgInvite_ConsumesTheHashNotThePlaintext` |
| org_invite/bound-to-address | A bound invitation MUST be accepted only by the account whose canonical email equals the invited address, at sign-up and at acceptance. | Security | `TestAcceptOrgInvite_BoundInvitationRefusesAnotherAddressOpaquely`, `TestSignupInviteGate_ABoundTokenDoesNotAdmitAnotherAddress`, `TestSignupInviteGate_ABoundTokenAdmitsItsOwnAddress` |
| org_invite/opaque-refusal | Unknown, expired, spent and wrong-address acceptances MUST all return the same NotFound refusal. | Security | `TestAcceptInvite_NotFoundBecomesTheOpaqueRefusal`, `TestAcceptInvite_EmptyResponseGivesTheSameRefusal`, `TestAcceptOrgInvite_AnUnknownTokenIsOpaque`, `TestAcceptInvite_OtherErrorsAreNotDisguised` |
| org_invite/grant-is-org-scoped | Acceptance MUST write the role grant with the invitation's `org_id`, never as a realm-wide grant. The role MUST come from the claimed row, never from the request. | Security | `TestAcceptOrgInvite_BoundRecordsDerivedAndOpenDoesNot` (indirect). Unit (mock) only: no test asserts `AssignRoleToUserReq.OrgId`. |
| org_invite/invite-only-gate | With `invite_only=true`, sign-up (password, confirmation and OAuth creation) MUST refuse an address that has neither a pending invitation nor a valid open or own bound link, with an opaque refusal. With `invite_only=false`, the gate MUST admit everyone. A query error MUST refuse. | Security | `TestSignupInviteGate_OverrideIsInstalled`, `TestSignupInviteGate_InvitedAddressPasses`, `TestSignupInviteGate_OffAllowsEveryone`, `TestInviteGate_RefusesAnUninvitedAddress`, `TestInviteGate_AdmitsAnInvitedAddress`, `TestInviteGate_OffAdmitsEveryone`, `TestInviteGate_RefusalIsOpaque`, `TestOAuthCreate_ConsultsTheInviteGate`, `TestSignupInviteGate_AnOpenLinkAdmitsAnyAddress`, `TestSignupInviteGate_AStaleTokenFallsBackToTheAddress` |
| org_invite/open-link-registers-once | Under `invite_only`, an open link MUST admit at most one registration: the first sign-up binds it to its address, and a second one with the same link MUST be refused unless its address has its own invitation. | Security | `TestClaimOpenInvite_TheFirstRegistrationSpendsTheLink`, `TestClaimOpenInvite_AnUncommittedBindIsTheClaimantsOwn`, `TestClaimOpenInvite_TheLoserOfARaceKeepsItsOwnInvitation`, `TestClaimOpenInvite_WithoutInviteOnlyNothingIsRefused`. Unit (mock) only: the race is decided by the row lock of `BindOpenOrgInvite`. A live test must run two concurrent sign-ups on one link and show one account was admitted. |
| org_invite/email-proof-floor | With `email_proof_required=full`, acceptance MUST require a self-confirmed address. With `derived`, it MUST accept a bound invitation or an already derived or confirmed address, and refuse an open invitation from an unproven account. A refused acceptance MUST NOT spend the invitation. | Security | `TestEmailProofGate_FullRefusesAnUnprovenAccount`, `TestEmailProofGate_FullAllowsASelfConfirmedAccount`, `TestEmailProofGate_NoneChangesNothing`, `TestEmailProofGate_DerivedFloorSeparatesBoundFromOpen`, `TestEmailProofGate_AlreadyDerivedSatisfiesDerivedButNotFull`, `TestEmailProofGate_RefusalIsActionableAndLeaksNothing`, `TestAcceptOrgInvite_ARefusedAcceptanceDoesNotConsumeTheInvitation` |
| org_invite/bound-accept-derives-proof | Accepting a bound invitation MUST record `DERIVED_FROM_INVITE` in the acceptance transaction and MUST NOT downgrade a self-confirmed address. An open invitation MUST record nothing. | Security | `TestAcceptOrgInvite_BoundRecordsDerivedAndOpenDoesNot` (no-downgrade is SQL only) |
| org_invite/revoke-own-org-only | `RevokeOrgInvite` MUST close only a pending invitation of the caller's active organization. | Security | none. Unit (mock) only: `org_id` sits in the `CloseOrgInvite` WHERE clause. A live test must revoke organization B's invitation ID while acting in A and show it survives. |
| org_invite/withdrawal-is-a-state | Withdrawing an invitation and releasing a lapsed one MUST NOT delete it. `CloseOrgInvite` stamps `closed_at` and `closed_by` (the withdrawing account); `CloseExpiredOrgInvite` stamps `closed_at` on an expired pending one (`closed_by` NULL). A closed invitation MUST be refused by every path that reads, binds, accepts or lists a pending one (`GetPendingOrgInviteByToken`, `BindOpenOrgInvite`, `ConsumeOrgInviteByToken`, `ListOrgInvitesByOrg`, `ListPendingInvitesForEmail`), with the same opaque refusal as a spent one, and MUST leave the pending index, so the address can be invited again. Deleting an organization MUST leave none of its invitations usable: `AuthService.RemoveOrganization` (the admin page's delete) closes its pending ones (`CloseOrgInvitesOfOrg`, the caller as `closed_by`) in the deletion's transaction, and every statement that reads, binds or accepts an invitation requires its organization to be live (`EXISTS … o.deleted_at IS NULL` or a join), which also covers a direct `AuthMutation.DeleteOrganization` and a race with it. | Security | `TestRemoveOrganization_ClosesItsInvitationsAndTheLinksAreDead`, `TestRevokeOrgInvite_ClosesTheRowAndTheLinkIsDead`, `TestInviteToOrg_ClosesALapsedInvitationAndKeepsIt`, `TestEveryFormerDeleteIsAGuardedStateChange`, `TestEveryWriteThatUsesARowSkipsAnEndedOne`, `TestEveryReaderSkipsEndedRows` |
| org_invite/single-use | An invitation MUST be consumed at most once. Two concurrent acceptances of one token MUST yield exactly one membership. | Consistency & concurrency | none. The guard is `accepted_at IS NULL` in the consuming `UPDATE`. Live only. |
| org_invite/accept-atomic | Consuming the invitation, writing the membership, writing the org-scoped grant and recording the derived proof MUST commit together. Any failure MUST leave the invitation unspent. | Consistency & concurrency | none. No org_invite test wires `DistTx`; `TestAcceptOrgInvite_RefusesARoleWidenedSinceTheInvitation` shows only the snapshot refusal. Only holds when `DistTx` is wired, see gap 2. |
| org_invite/one-pending-per-address | At most one pending bound invitation MUST exist per `(org, email)`. An expired one MUST NOT block a new invitation. Open invitations MUST NOT collide with each other. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestInviteToOrg_ClearsALapsedInviteFirst`, `TestInviteToOrg_ClosesALapsedInvitationAndKeepsIt`, `TestInviteToOrg_NothingExpiredIsNotAFailure`, `TestInviteToOrg_ClearFailureStopsTheWrite`, `TestInviteToOrg_AnOpenInvitationSkipsTheClear`, `TestInviteToOrg_PropagatesTheConstraintDetail` (uniqueness itself: partial index, live only) |
| org_invite/no-invite-for-a-member | `InviteToOrg` MUST refuse a bound invitation whose address (normalised, looked up with `GetUserByEmail`) belongs to an account with a live membership of the organization (`GetOrgMember`), with `AlreadyExists` / `INVITE_ALREADY_MEMBER` on `email`, before anything is written. A removed member (`org_membership/removal-is-a-state`), a member of another organization and an address with no account MUST still be invited. A membership that starts after the check is refused at acceptance by the membership's unique index. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestInviteToOrg_RefusesAnAddressThatIsAlreadyAMember`, `TestInviteToOrg_ARemovedMemberCanBeInvitedBack`, `TestInviteToOrg_AnAddressWithNoMembershipHereIsInvited`, `TestInviteToOrg_AFailedMemberLookupStopsTheWrite` |
| org_invite/expiry-enforced | An invitation past `expires_at` MUST be refused at sign-up and at acceptance. | Consistency & concurrency | none (SQL predicate `expires_at > NOW()` in three statements) |
| org_invite/address-canonical | Bound invitation addresses MUST be stored in canonical form, and an empty address MUST create an open invitation. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestInviteToOrg_TheAddressMayBeEmptyForAnOpenInvitation`, `TestInviteToOrg_AnOpenInvitationStoresNoAddress` |
| org_invite/metadata-opaque | `metadata` MUST be stored as given (an absent value as `{}`) and returned from the same statement that spends the invitation. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestInviteToOrg_AbsentMetadataBecomesTheEmptyObject` |
| org_invite/indexed-lookups | Token lookup MUST use the unique `token_hash` index. Per-org and per-address listings MUST use the `org_id` and `email` indexes. | Performance | none (schema only) |
| org_invite/proof-knob-effective | A non-`none` `email_proof_required` MUST be enforced or MUST fail boot. It MUST NOT be silently ignored. An unknown value MUST fail boot. | Failure & configuration | `TestEmailProofFloor_RefusedAtBootWhenItWouldNotHold` |
| org_invite/claim-names-the-address | The statement that spends an invitation through its link MUST match only an open invitation or one bound to the accepting account's address, so a bind racing the acceptance cannot hand a bound invitation to another account. | Security | `TestAcceptOrgInvite_TheClaimNamesTheCallersAddress` (that the claim carries it); the predicate is the SQL's |
| org_invite/managed-within-ceiling | `ListOrgInvites` MUST list, and `RevokeOrgInvite` MUST withdraw, only invitations whose role the caller could give (`checkMayGrantRole`); withdrawing one above is refused (`INVITE_ABOVE_CALLER`). | Security | `TestOrgInvites_ListedAndWithdrawnWithinTheCeiling` |

**Gaps / suspected defects**
1. **Fixed: the binding check raced with the consume.** The consuming `UPDATE` now carries `(email IS NULL OR email = :email)`: a bind between the advisory read and the consume leaves the invitation to the bound address. Whether accepting derives the address is read from the claimed row.
2. **Not a defect in a wired bundle.** Acceptance runs in one distributed transaction: `RegisterPlugin` always wires `DistTx`. Only a hand-built test handler runs the statements separately.
3. **Fixed: the proof floor was silently ignored without `email_verification`.** A floor above `none` without that feature, or an unknown value, refuses to boot (`checkEmailProofFloor`).
4. **Decided: the address-guessing window is open by default.** With `email_proof_required=none` (default) whoever registers an invited address first may take an invitation bound to it — through `invite_accept_from_list`, or by registering under `invite_only`. `derived` or `full` closes it. The documentation now names `email_proof_required` (it pointed to the removed `invite_requires_verified_email`).
5. **Fixed: `ListMyInvites` returned invitations the caller could not accept from the list.** `invite_accept_from_list` accepts a bound one by its id (`AcceptMyOrgInvite`).
6. **Fixed: invitation management had no ceiling of its own** (`org_invite/managed-within-ceiling`).
7. **Decided: `POST /auth/invites/accept` has no `public_limit`.** The gateway rate-limits per address only an `exclude_auth` endpoint; this one needs an account, and the token is 256-bit random.
8. **Decided: expiry mixes clocks.** `expires_at` is written from the application's clock and compared with the database's `NOW()`, as for every token of this plugin: the DQL has no interval arithmetic. Skew shortens or lengthens the TTL by the skew.

### invite_accept_at_signup

**Purpose.** A registration through an invitation's link joins at once: the person arrives a member with the invited role, without a second call.

**Adds**
- SignUp, an OAuth account's creation and (with `sign_up_confirmation`) ConfirmSignUp accept the invitation in the transaction that creates the account. `ConfirmSignUpReq.invite_token` carries the link again at confirmation.

**Requires.** `org_invite`. Go files: `handlers/org_invite_at_signup.go`; with `sign_up_confirmation`, `handlers/org_invite_at_signup_confirm.go`.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| invite_accept_at_signup/same-acceptance | The acceptance MUST be AcceptOrgInvite's (the claim by the link and the address, the snapshot, the plan, the kind, the products' roles), in the registration's transaction. | Security | `TestAcceptInviteAtSignup`; live: `product-roles-proof` (ivan) |
| invite_accept_at_signup/declines-before-writing | It MUST NOT accept, and MUST NOT fail the registration, when the link names nothing, names another address, or the address does not reach `email_proof_required` without a proof of its own (an open link under `derived`; `full` without a confirmation or a provider's verified email); the invitation then stays for AcceptOrgInvite. | Security | `TestAcceptInviteAtSignup` |
| invite_accept_at_signup/refusal-refuses-the-registration | Once the invitation is claimed, a refusal (the role or the plan moved since the invitation) MUST refuse the registration, which then writes nothing: an invitation spent with no membership MUST NOT commit. | Consistency & concurrency | none (the registration's one transaction) |
| invite_accept_at_signup/policy-join-first | A registration the host's policy joins (org_signup) MUST NOT also accept an invitation. | Consistency & concurrency | none |

### invite_accept_from_list

**Purpose.** Accepting an invitation from the caller's own list, without its link: register first, accept later.

**Adds**
- Query `GetPendingOrgInviteForEmail` (by id AND the caller's address; pending, unexpired, a live organization). The acceptance then spends it through `ConsumeOrgInviteByToken`, so `OrgInviteAccepted` has one emitting site.
- Business RPC `AcceptMyOrgInvite`; REST `POST /auth/invites/{invite_id}/accept`.

**Requires.** `org_invite`. Go file: `handlers/org_invite_from_list.go`.

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| invite_accept_from_list/bound-to-the-caller | `AcceptMyOrgInvite` MUST accept only a pending invitation bound to the caller's address; any other id — another address's, an open one, unknown — MUST get the opaque refusal and spend nothing. | Security | `TestAcceptMyOrgInvite_AnIdOutsideTheCallersListIsOpaque`, `TestAcceptMyOrgInvite_FromTheCallersOwnList` |
| invite_accept_from_list/no-derived-proof | Accepting from the list MUST NOT derive the address, and the floor (`email_proof_required`) MUST be asked of the account: under `derived` an unproven account is refused, under `full` a derived one too. | Security | `TestAcceptMyOrgInvite_TheFloorAsksTheAccountForItsProof` |
| invite_accept_from_list/same-acceptance | Accepting from the list MUST write the same membership, grant, kind and products' roles, under the same lock and snapshot check, as accepting through the link. | Consistency & concurrency | `TestAcceptMyOrgInvite_FromTheCallersOwnList` |

## Across features

Every interaction goes through one of these mechanisms:

| Mechanism | Where | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| **Seam variable** (a single `var` replaced in `init()`) | `auth_service.go`: `resolvePrincipal`, `signInMfaGate`, `signInDisabledGate`, `signInBotGate`, `signupResolveRoleIDs`, `signupAssignRoleIDs`, `signupInviteGate`, `signupClaimInvite`, `inviteVerifiedEmailGate`, `markEmailDerived`, `markEmailConfirmed`, `whoAmIEnrich`; `signup.go`: `signupCreateUser`, `signUpConfirmation`; `signup_confirm.go`: `pendingSignUpTenant`, `confirmCreateUser` | Exactly **one** feature may assign each seam. The default is the behaviour with that feature off. A seam is declared in a file staged for every feature combination that can assign or call it. |
| **Append-only list** | `scopeDecorators` (tenant_scope, org_membership, service_account), `grantNarrowers` (org_membership), `botUserFillers` / `botVisible` (service_account × tenant_scope) | Features add to the list and never replace it. A narrower may only **remove** grants, or add the active organization's own session grants (`orgGrantSources`: a team's roles). |
| **Feature marks** | `orgMembershipStaged` (org_membership) | Read by boot validation and by fail-closed checks. |
| **Field / RPC gates in DQL** | `?feature(x)` in SET / WHERE / projection lists | A column that belongs to feature X may appear only behind `?feature(X)`, or in an RPC gated on X. |
| **Combined files** | `go_files_combined` | `service_account_tenant.go` (service_account + tenant_scope), `service_account_org.go` (service_account + org_membership), `signup_confirm_tenant.go` (sign_up_confirmation + tenant_scope). |

---

### Feature interaction matrix

Abbreviations: **rb** rbac, **at** authenticate_turnkey, **su** sign_up_turnkey, **sc** sign_up_confirmation, **si** sign_in_turnkey, **ts** tenant_scope, **ap** api_token, **dv** devices, **oa** oauth, **tf** two_factor, **pr** password_reset, **ev** email_verification, **cl** cli_login, **om** org_membership, **oi** org_invite, **pc** password_change, **sa** service_account, **ua** user_admin.

Cell codes (row × column, upper triangle; the relation is symmetric unless the code says which side):
**R** one requires the other (plugin.yaml) · **X** conflicts_with · **C** combined go_files · **H** one overrides or extends the other's seam/hook · **T** shared table or column (one writes or deletes what the other reads) · **Q** shared query/RPC or shared helper · **O** ordering constraint inside one request · **G** one feature's gate applies on the other's path · **·** no interaction found.

|    | rb | at | su | sc | si | ts | ap | dv | oa | tf | pr | ev | cl | om | oi | pc | sa | ua |
|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|----|
| **rb** | — | R Q | H O | H | · | Q | T Q | · | H | · | · | · | · | R T Q | R T | · | R T | T |
| **at** | | — | · | · | · | H | H | Q | · | · | · | · | G | R H | · | Q | R H | G |
| **su** | | | — | R H | · | R H | · | T | G | · | · | · | · | · | H G | · | · | · |
| **sc** | | | | — | · | C | · | T | G | · | · | H | · | · | G O | · | · | · |
| **si** | | | | | — | · | · | R H | · | R H O | · | · | · | · | · | · | H O | H O |
| **ts** | | | | | | — | · | · | X | · | · | · | · | H | · | · | C | · |
| **ap** | | | | | | | — | Q T | · | · | T | · | G | H O | · | T | R Q | T |
| **dv** | | | | | | | | — | H | H R* | T | · | T | · | · | T | · | T |
| **oa** | | | | | | | | | — | O | · | R T | · | · | G | · | G | G T |
| **tf** | | | | | | | | | | — | O | · | O | · | · | O | G | G |
| **pr** | | | | | | | | | | | — | · | · | · | · | T | G | G |
| **ev** | | | | | | | | | | | | — | · | · | H G | · | · | T |
| **cl** | | | | | | | | | | | | | — | · | · | · | G | G |
| **om** | | | | | | | | | | | | | | — | R T | · | C | T |
| **oi** | | | | | | | | | | | | | | | — | · | · | T |
| **pc** | | | | | | | | | | | | | | | | — | G | · |
| **sa** | | | | | | | | | | | | | | | | | — | R T |

`admin_sign_ins` and `admin_devices` are left out of the matrix: each gates one admin page and requires `user_admin` (**R** with **ua**), and touches nothing else.

`R*` (dv × tf): retired. `two_factor_scope=new_devices` no longer needs `devices` (device identity is base tier).

Base tier: `sessionTokenExpiry`, `newBearerToken`, `refuseUnsignableAccount`, `callerUserID`, `callerSessionTokenID`, `signUpAllowlistGate`, `notFoundOK`, `intersectInt32` and `normalizeEmail` are always staged, and every feature uses them.

---

### Interaction invariants

Proof column: exact unit-test names in `auth/src/handlers`, or `none`. A unit test that runs against a fake store proves the handler's **sequencing** only. Where the property is enforced by SQL (a conditional update, a constraint, a transaction), that is listed under [How to prove it — live-only coverage](#how-to-prove-it--live-only-coverage).

#### Identity, roles and sign-up

| ID | Features | Mechanism | MUST hold | Proof |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `interaction/rbac-sign_up-role-decision-before-tx` | rb su (also sc, oa) | H O: `signupResolveRoleIDs` reads `CountUsers` from committed state **before** the write transaction opens. `signupAssignRoleIDs` writes inside it. | The first account receives the `assign_first_user` roles and every later account receives `assign_default`. The count is never read inside the transaction, because the new user is not visible to pooled reads. A zero count only makes the sign-up a candidate: the bootstrap decision is settled inside the transaction by the `BootstrapClaim` write (`interaction/rbac-sign_up-first-user-race`). All three account-creating doors (SignUp, ConfirmSignUp, OAuth create) use the same pair. | TestAssignSignupRoles_FirstUserGetsBootstrapRoles, TestAssignSignupRoles_LaterUserGetsDefaultRoles |
| `interaction/rbac-sign_up-first-user-race` | rb su sc oa | Q: `CountUsers == 0` is not serialised. | Two sign-ups that run concurrently on an empty table MUST NOT both receive bootstrap roles. The first_user set goes only to the sign-up that writes the `BootstrapClaim` row (constant key, `UPSERT … DO NOTHING`) in its transaction; a taken claim is never reopened. | TestAssignSignupRoles_ConcurrentFirstSignUpsGetOneBootstrapAdmin, TestAssignSignupRoles_ATakenClaimIsNotReopened (**unit (mock) only**: a live test runs concurrent first sign-ups and asserts one administrator) |
| `interaction/rbac-tenant_scope-first-user-is-global` | rb ts | Q: `CountUsers` is realm-wide, not per tenant. | Only the first account in the whole deployment gets bootstrap roles. The first account of each later tenant gets default roles. | `TestSignUp_OnlyTheDeploymentsFirstAccountIsBootstrapped`, `TestCountUsers_IsRealmWide` |
| `interaction/sign_up-sign_up_confirmation-seam` | su sc | H: `signUpConfirmation` diverts SignUp after the allowlist and invite gates. | With sc on, SignUp creates no User and issues no token. It answers `confirmation_required` and `pending_id`. The allowlist and invite gates run for both paths. | TestSignUpConfirmation_SignUpParksAndConfirmCreates, TestSignUpAllowlist_GatesBothSteps |
| `interaction/sign_up-tenant_scope-create-seam` | su ts | H: `signupCreateUser` is replaced by `createUserWithResolvedTenant`. | Under ts, every self-service account is created with the tenant resolved from `tenant_slug` or the forwarded Host (never from a client-sent tenant id). A request with no resolvable tenant is refused with `TENANT_UNKNOWN` and nothing is written. | TestResolveSignupTenant_ByForwardedHost |
| `interaction/sign_up_confirmation-tenant_scope-pending-carries-tenant` | sc ts | C: `signup_confirm_tenant.go` stamps `PendingSignUp.tenant_id` at SignUp and creates the User in that tenant at ConfirmSignUp. | The tenant is decided when the code is requested and is not re-resolved at confirmation. | `TestConfirmSignUp_CreatesTheAccountInTheTenantSignUpResolved` |
| `interaction/tenant_scope-email-global` | ts su sc | T: `User.email` is unique across the table, not per tenant. | One address can hold at most one account in the deployment, whatever its tenant. A second registration in another tenant answers `EMAIL_TAKEN`, or `AlreadyExists` at confirmation. | live only: register one address in tenant A, then in tenant B (SignUp, and ConfirmSignUp under sc), and assert one `User` row and `EMAIL_TAKEN`. The guarantee is the table-wide unique index on `User.email`; the handlers' mapping of its violation is `TestSignUp_ATakenAddressSaysSignInInstead`, `TestConfirmSignUp_ATakenAddressSaysSignInInstead`. |
| `interaction/tenant_scope-oauth-conflict` | ts oa | X | The activation is refused at codegen. Reason: OAuth creates accounts through `CreateUser` with no tenant, and has no request-derived tenant to resolve. Under ts, `User.tenant_id` is NOT NULL, so an OAuth sign-up would fail at runtime with a constraint violation. | codegen (manifest) |
| `interaction/tenant_scope-org_membership-both-scopes` | ts om | H: both append a scope decorator. | Both `scopes[tenant_id]` and `scopes[org_id]` may be stamped. Neither decorator reads the other's scope, and nothing prevents a project from activating both. | `TestAuthenticate_TenantAndOrgScopesAreStampedIndependently` (both scopes and labels stamped; either decorator order gives the same answer) |
| `interaction/sign_up-org_invite-invite-gate` | su oi (also oa) | H G: `signupInviteGate` = `requirePendingInvite`, read at call time from `invite_only`. | With `invite_only=true` (the default), SignUp **and** the OAuth create branch refuse an address that holds no pending, unexpired invitation and presents no matching invite token. The refusal is `PermissionDenied`. It does not say whether an invitation expired or an account exists. Without oi the gate is permissive and `invite_only` has no effect. | TestInviteGate_RefusesAnUninvitedAddress, TestInviteGate_AdmitsAnInvitedAddress, TestInviteGate_OffAdmitsEveryone, TestInviteGate_RefusalIsOpaque, TestSignupInviteGate_OverrideIsInstalled, TestOAuthCreate_ConsultsTheInviteGate, TestSignupInviteGate_ABoundTokenDoesNotAdmitAnotherAddress |
| `interaction/sign_up-org_invite-open-link-claim` | su oi | H: `signupClaimInvite` binds an open invitation to the registering address **inside** the sign-up transaction. | An open link is spent by exactly one registration. A failed registration leaves the link open. The loser of a race keeps its own invitation. | TestClaimOpenInvite_TheFirstRegistrationSpendsTheLink, TestClaimOpenInvite_AnUncommittedBindIsTheClaimantsOwn, TestClaimOpenInvite_TheLoserOfARaceKeepsItsOwnInvitation, TestClaimOpenInvite_WithoutInviteOnlyNothingIsRefused |
| `interaction/sign_up_confirmation-org_invite-gate-timing` | sc oi | G O: the invite gate and the open-link bind run in `startSignUpConfirmation`; ConfirmSignUp runs the invite gate again (and the allowlist). | An invitation revoked or expired between SignUp and ConfirmSignUp stops the confirmation; no account is created. An open link is spent by a pending registration even if it is never confirmed. A registration an open organization policy took is not asked. | `TestSignUpConfirmation_TheInviteGateRunsAgainAtConfirmation` (the gate runs at SignUp and again at confirmation; a withdrawal refuses, a standing invitation confirms) |
| `interaction/sign_up_confirmation-email_verification-self-confirmed` | sc ev | H: `markEmailConfirmed` → `MarkEmailVerified` inside the confirmation transaction. | An account created by ConfirmSignUp has `email_verified_at` set and `email_proof = SELF_CONFIRMED`. Without ev the call is a no-op. | none (no test reaches `confirmSignUpTx` with ev staged) |
| `interaction/sign_up_confirmation-oauth-bypass` | sc oa | G | The OAuth create branch does not pass through confirmation. It runs the allowlist and invite gates, and marks the address confirmed only if the provider asserts `email_verified=true`. | TestOAuthCallback_AProviderVerifiedAddressIsRecorded |
| `interaction/sign_up-devices-undeviced` | su sc dv | T: SignUp and ConfirmSignUp register the request's device inside their transaction and mint through `issueBoundToken`. | **Retired:** registration sessions are device-bound like every other; `RevokeDevice` ends them. Only sessions minted before binding are undeviced, swept by `RevokeOtherDevices` / `RevokeAllDevices`. | `TestSignUpThenSignIn_TheSamePasswordWorks`, `TestSignUpConfirmation_SignUpParksAndConfirmCreates`, `TestRevokeDevices_SweepSessionsNoDeviceHolds` |

|---|---|---|---|---|
| `interaction/sign_in-user_admin-disabled-after-password` | si ua | H O: `signInDisabledGate` runs after the password check. | A disabled account is refused with the same opaque `INVALID_CREDENTIALS` as a wrong password. The refusal is indistinguishable from a wrong password. | TestSignInBotGate, TestSignInBotGate_ReasonStaysInternal, TestSignIn_RefusesAMachineAccountThatHasAPassword, TestBotPasswordSentinel_IsNonEmptyAndCannotVerify |
| `interaction/user_admin-every-session-door-rechecks` | ua sa × tf oa cl | G: `refuseUnsignableAccount` re-reads the account before VerifyMfa, OAuthExchange and CliToken issue a session (and before OAuthCallback mints a login code). | No path that mints a session for an account decided earlier (a challenge, a linked identity, an authorization code) may mint one for an account that is disabled or BOT at mint time. | TestVerifyMfa_ADisabledAccountGetsNoSession (VerifyMfa only; OAuthCallback, OAuthExchange and CliToken: none) |
| `interaction/user_admin-disable-purges-every-token-class` | ua ap dv sa | T: `DisableUsers` sets `disabled_at` and revokes every `UserToken` of the ids (sessions, API, bot, device-bound) in one unit of work. | After DisableUsers commits **no** token of that account may resolve, and no later mint may produce one. Two enforcements: the revoke ends every live bearer, and both token resolvers refuse a disabled account (since rc.25, Verified finding 4). | `TestDisableUsers_RevokesEveryTokenClassWithTheDisable` (statement text: both legs on one id set, the token revoke unnarrowed), `TestResolvePrincipal_ADisabledAccountIsRefused` (a token minted after the disable). **Unit (statement text) only:** a live test would disable an account holding one token of each class and assert each answers 401. |

| `interaction/user_admin-disable-vs-inflight-mint` | ua × si ap cl | T | A sign-in or token mint that read the account before DisableUsers committed, and writes its token afterwards, leaves a live token for a disabled account. The same unguarded window exists for CreateApiToken from a token resolved before the disable. | none (live only, gap) |
| `interaction/sign_in-two_factor-gate` | si tf | H O: `signInMfaGate` runs after the password and account gates, and after device resolution. | When challenged, SignIn returns `mfa_required` and `challenge_id` and issues **no** token. Only VerifyMfa issues the session, through the same `issueSession` seam. If the enrolment status cannot be read, SignIn refuses rather than skipping the challenge. | TestMfaGate_AllLogins_NoTotp_GeneratesCode, TestMfaGate_TotpEnrolled_NoCode, TestMfaGate_ReadFailure_RefusesRatherThanSkipTheChallenge, TestEnrollConfirmVerify_TotpRoundTrip |
| `interaction/two_factor-devices-new_devices-scope` | tf | H: `mintDevice` returns `trusted` only for a (user, thumbprint) row whose `trusted_at` is later than the account's trust revocation. There is no "trusted" default. | `two_factor_scope=new_devices` works in every activation. A device is trusted only through the pair's own row: the key's holder, for that account. | `TestValidateConfig_NewDevicesNeedsNoFeature`, `TestMfaGate_NewDevices_SkipsTrusted`, `TestMfaGate_NewDevices_ChallengesUntrusted`, `TestSignIn_TrustIsPerUserAndDeviceOnASharedDevice`, `TestMintDevice_TrustRevokedByACredentialChange_NotTrusted` |
| `interaction/devices-issue-session-binds-and-trusts` | si tf oa su sc cl | H: every mint registers the device (`mintDevice`) and mints through `issueBoundToken`; `issueSession` (SignIn, VerifyMfa, OAuthExchange) also writes trust when given a cutoff. | Every session is device-bound. Trust is per (user, device), written only with a cutoff. | `TestIssueSession_BindsAndTrustsOnlyWithACutoff`, `TestEveryMintPath_RefusesWithoutAProofBeforeTouchingStorage` |
| `interaction/oauth-two_factor-bypass` | oa tf | O: OAuthExchange calls `issueSession` without `signInMfaGate`. | An OAuth sign-in is **not** challenged for the plugin's second factor; the identity provider is treated as the second factor. This is documented behaviour, and a project that needs both must layer its own policy. | `TestOAuthExchange_IsNotChallengedForTheSecondFactor` |
| `interaction/oauth-devices-two_factor-trust-laundering` | oa tf | H: OAuthExchange issues its session through `issueSession` with no trust cutoff. | Under `new_devices`, a device MUST become trusted only by a sign-in that passed the plugin's second factor or was not asked for one. | `interaction/two_factor-password_reset-factor-survives` | tf pr | O: ResetPassword sets the hash and revokes tokens. A challenge opened before the reset stays redeemable until its TTL runs out. | `TestResetPassword_TouchesThePasswordAndTokensOnly` (the reset runs exactly its four statements, none naming the authenticator, recovery codes or challenges, and returns no session) |
| `interaction/two_factor-password_change-no-step-up` | tf pc | O | ChangePassword requires the current password only, not a second-factor step-up. A hijacked session that knows the password can rotate it and end every other session. | `TestChangePassword_AsksForNoSecondFactor` |
| `interaction/two_factor-cli_login-inherits` | tf cl | O | CliToken never runs the second factor. It may mint a session only from a code that CliAuthorize created for an already-authenticated principal, so the second factor is inherited from that session. See `interaction/api_token-cli_login-realm-escalation` for the exception. | `TestCliToken_RunsNoSecondFactor`, `TestCliAuthorize_AnAPITokenCallerIsRefused`, `TestCliAuthorize_Rejections` (no principal) |

#### Tokens, realms and organizations

| ID | Features | Mechanism | MUST hold | Proof |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `interaction/api_token-authenticate-realm-resolver` | ap at rb | H: `resolvePrincipal` = `resolvePrincipalRealmAware`. | A SESSION token counts only session-realm roles and an API token counts only API-realm roles. An API token's non-empty permission subset intersects every role, a wildcard included. An empty subset means no narrowing. An API token carries no `session_token_id`. | TestResolvePrincipalRealmAware_Session_NoSubsetLookup, TestResolvePrincipalRealmAware_API_Subset_Intersects, TestResolvePrincipalRealmAware_API_Wildcard_NarrowedBySubset, TestResolvePrincipalRealmAware_API_EmptySubset_NoNarrowing, TestResolvePrincipalRealmAware_ApiTokenHasNoSessionID |
| `interaction/api_token-org_membership-compose-on-roles` | ap om | H O: the realm resolver runs first, then `narrowGrantsToActiveOrg` keeps a grant only if its **role** is eligible in the active org (or realm-wide when no org is active). Permissions are flattened last. | A permission survives only when a **single** role grants it and that role passes both axes. A wildcard role must pass the org axis. Organization ownership grants nothing. A failed narrowing query refuses authentication. | TestNarrowGrants_ActiveOrg, TestNarrowGrants_APermissionTwoDifferentRolesGrantDoesNotSurvive, TestNarrowGrants_WildcardRoleMustPassTheOrgAxis, TestNarrowGrants_NoRoleMeansNothingWhoeverCreatedTheOrg, TestNarrowGrants_QueryErrorFailsClosed, TestNarrowGrants_NoActiveOrg |
| `interaction/org_membership-authenticate-active-org` | om at | H: `resolveOrgScope` decorator + `whoAmIEnrich`. | `scopes[org_id]` is stamped only for an org the caller belongs to, selected by `W17-Org` (header or path); otherwise it is inferred only when the caller has exactly one org. A slug the caller does not belong to (or that names no organization) is refused `PermissionDenied` / `ORG_NOT_MEMBER`. A path org and a header org that disagree are refused (`ORG_MISMATCH`). A request that could have named one (the selector is accepted there) and named none keeps the realm-wide roles only, and answers what it set aside — `scopes[org_permissions]`, the ids of the roles held inside organizations (`*` for a wildcard), and to a product `scopes[org_product_roles]`, its codes held there, never the identity's ids — so the gateway answers `ORGANIZATION_REQUIRED` rather than `MISSING_PERMISSION`; nothing is selected and no organization is named. | TestResolveOrgScope, TestResolveOrgScope_WithoutAMembershipNobodySelectsTheOrg, TestResolveOrgScope_SoleOrgInference, TestResolveOrgScope_MultipleOrgsStayAmbiguous, TestRefuseOtherOrg, TestOrgNotMember_SaysWhatIsWrong, TestOrgNotMember_AnUnknownOrganizationAnswersTheSame, TestWhoAmI_CarriesOrgsAndTheActiveOne, TestResolveOrgScope_MarksTheUnnamedOrganization, TestResolveOrgScope_SelectorInHeadersIsNamed, TestNarrowGrants_SaysWhatAnUnnamedOrganizationSetAside, TestAnswerProductRoles_SaysWhatAnUnnamedOrganizationSetAside |
| `interaction/api_token-cli_login-realm-escalation` | ap cl | G: CliToken mints a **SESSION** token; CliAuthorize requires a session caller (`requireSessionCaller`) and binds its expiry. | An API token (API realm, possibly narrowed by a subset) MUST NOT be exchangeable for a session token, which would carry the user's full session-realm roles. ---

### Cross-cutting invariants

#### Nothing is deleted

No statement of the plugin deletes a row from any auth table, and no Go of the plugin carries a `DELETE` of its own. Every path that used to delete writes a state instead, and the row stays as the history. Two mechanisms, each where the schema allows it:

- **The key is released, the re-add is a new row** (`Role`, `Organization`, `UserRole`, `OrgMembership`, `UserTotpSecret`). The ending UPDATE stamps the state (`deleted_at`, `revoked_at`, `removed_at`) and sets `ended_key` from `''` to the row's own id. `ended_key` is part of the table's unique index, so every live row shares `''` (two live rows still collide) while every ended row has a value of its own: the name, slug, grant, membership or authenticator can be taken again by a plain INSERT, and the ended row is never rewritten. The index stays FULL — a partial `WHERE <state> IS NULL` index is refused on MySQL (the platform IR), and `rbac`, `two_factor` and `sign_up_confirmation` do not otherwise need one — and every existing INSERT is unchanged (the membership role change, the invitation acceptance, the sign-up role links). Chosen over reviving the old row because a revive overwrites the row it brings back (its grant time, its revocation) and so needs a second, append-only history table kept in step; here the rows are the history. It also could not be done for `UserRole`: under `org_membership` its uniques are partial, and an UPSERT cannot target a partial index without its predicate.
- **One row per pair, revived, with an append-only log** (`RolePermission` only). Its unique `(role_id, permission_id)` is the conflict target of the platform's role seed (`upsert_on: [role_id, permission_id]`), so it cannot take an `ended_key` column. `RemovePermissionFromRole` stamps `removed_at` / `removed_by`; `AddPermissionToRole` is an UPSERT that clears them; both append a `RolePermissionChange` row in the same transaction, so what the revive overwrites is kept.
- **Tables whose rows simply end** need neither: `PendingSignUp` (`claimed_at`, `superseded_at`, `expires_at`), `UserRecoveryCode` (`used_at`, `revoked_at`), `OrgInvite` (`accepted_at`, `closed_at`; its index is partial already, since `org_invite` requires `org_membership`), and the token, device, user and client tables (`device/revocation-is-a-state`).
- **Append-only logs** (`RolePermissionChange`, `SignInEvent`) are only ever inserted into; the one exception is an erasure, which pseudonymises `SignInEvent`'s personal columns in place (`device/sign-in-history-append-only`).

Every reader of an endable row filters its state, so what a caller and every ACL check sees is what the delete gave: a revoked grant grants nothing, a removed permission is not granted, a deleted role or organization and a removed membership are invisible and grant nothing, a closed invitation cannot be bound, accepted or listed, a claimed, superseded or expired registration cannot be guessed at or claimed, a revoked authenticator verifies nothing and a revoked recovery code is refused like a used one. Every ending is guarded on its state being NULL, so the first one stands and a second answers NotFound, as a delete of a missing row did. Events keep their names and payloads.

| ID | MUST hold | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| `auth/nothing-is-deleted` | No statement of any auth proto MAY be a `DELETE`, against any table, and no hand-written Go of the plugin MAY carry one. Each former delete is the guarded state change of the table above; the readers skip ended rows; the unique indexes let a released key be taken again (`RolePermission` keeps its seed-pinned index and revives). | `TestNoStatementDeletesAnything` (every statement of every auth proto, comments excluded; every non-test Go file of `handlers/` and `lib/`; its own matchers' cases), `TestEveryFormerDeleteIsAGuardedStateChange`, `TestEveryWriteThatUsesARowSkipsAnEndedOne`, `TestEveryReaderSkipsEndedRows` (every alias of every endable table in every query), `TestUniqueIndexesLetAnEndedKeyBeReused`, and per path the row kept with its state: `TestRemoveOrgMembership_KeepsTheRowsAndTheMemberCanComeBack`, `TestUpdateOrgMembershipRole_KeepsTheReplacedGrantsAndCanGoBack`, `TestRevokeOrgInvite_ClosesTheRowAndTheLinkIsDead`, `TestInviteToOrg_ClosesALapsedInvitationAndKeepsIt`, `TestDisableTotp_KeepsTheRowsRevokedAndNothingVerifies`, `TestEnrollTotp_TheReplacedAuthenticatorStaysRevoked`, `TestGenerateRecoveryCodes_ReplacesTheSetBehindTheStepUp`, `TestSignUpConfirmation_ACodeIsSingleUse`, `TestSignUpConfirmation_AnAbandonedRegistrationBlocksNobody`, `TestSignUpConfirmation_AnExpiredRegistrationStaysAndIsRefused`. **Unit (mock) only** for the SQL predicates and indexes; the statements typecheck against the platform's code generator for PostgreSQL and SQLite. Outside this plugin, a platform role seed older than this release still prunes `RolePermission` with a `DELETE` (`rbac` gap 6). |

#### Bearer tokens

| ID | MUST hold | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| `auth/bearer-hash-only` | Every mint path (SignUp, ConfirmSignUp, `issueSession` used by SignIn, VerifyMfa and OAuthExchange, CliToken, CreateApiToken, IssueBotToken) creates a 64-char random token with `newBearerToken`, stores only `token_hash = sha256 hex`, and returns the plaintext once. No query returns the token, and admin views mark `token_hash` as SECRET. | TestNewBearerToken, TestIssueSession_BindsAndTrustsOnlyWithACutoff, TestCreateApiToken_StampsAPIRealm_AndSubset |
| `auth/bearer-legacy-upgrade` | A row created before hashing (plaintext `token`, NULL `token_hash`) is still found by its value. The first Authenticate that resolves it moves it to `token_hash` and clears `token` with a conditional update (`WHERE token = :legacy AND token_hash IS NULL`). The move is best-effort and never fails the request. `GetUserByToken` called from hand-written code does not upgrade. | TestUpgradeLegacyToken, TestResolvePrincipalSession_LegacyRowIsMovedToTheHash, TestResolvePrincipalRealmAware_LegacyRowIsMovedToTheHash |
| `auth/bearer-expiry-in-sql` | Every live token lookup filters `expires_at IS NULL OR expires_at > NOW()` and `revoked_at IS NULL`; only the miss-path probes (`GetExpiredToken*`) read expired or revoked rows, and only to answer `SESSION_EXPIRED` (or `DEVICE_REVOKED`) to the bound key's holder (`device/revoked-token-refused-as-expired`). A session expires at `session_token_ttl_seconds` (≤0 → 30 days). An API or bot token expires at the caller's future `expires_at`, or else at `api_token_default_ttl_hours` (0 = never; negative → 90 days). An `expires_at` in the past or invalid is refused. | TestSessionTokenExpiry_ConfiguredTTL, TestSessionTokenExpiry_DefaultWhenUnset, TestIssueSession_StampsExpiry, TestAPITokenExpiry, TestCreateApiToken_DefaultExpiryReachesTheTokenAndTheCaller |
| `auth/authenticate-opaque` | Every Authenticate failure (missing header, wrong scheme, empty or unknown token, failed decorator or narrower) answers the same `Unauthenticated` — `Unauthenticated` is for credential problems only. An organization the caller cannot act in is not one: the credential was accepted, so `ORG_NOT_MEMBER` answers `PermissionDenied` (403 on REST, never 401), the same for an organization that does not exist and one the caller is not in, and `ORG_MISMATCH` answers `InvalidArgument`; both reveal only the caller's own membership. The exceptions to the opaque answer: an EXPIRED or REVOKED token presented with its own key (or, for a machine account's bearer token, `device/bot-bearer`, presented at all), which answers `SESSION_EXPIRED` with the instant it ended (`base/expired-token-says-so`, `device/revoked-token-refused-as-expired`) — it reveals only a property of the token the caller holds, and the same for both; and a request proving no key, which answers `DEVICE_PROOF_REQUIRED` before any lookup and so says nothing about the token. `DEVICE_REVOKED` is said only to a request proved by the bound key (`device/proof-required`). An outage is reported as `AUTH_UNAVAILABLE`, not as bad credentials. | TestUnauthenticated_WireStatusIsAlwaysOpaque, TestUnauthenticated_TwoCallsWithDifferentCauses_WireResponseIdentical, TestOrgNotMember_SaysWhatIsWrong, TestOrgNotMember_AnUnknownOrganizationAnswersTheSame, TestScopeDecorators_UnclassifiedFailureStaysOpaque, TestAuthnError_AnOutageIsNotReportedAsBadCredentials, TestAuthnError_NotFoundNeverBecomesOperational |
| `auth/authenticate-no-disabled-check` | Authenticate does not read `disabled_at` or `kind`. A token's validity depends only on the row existing and not being expired. Every path that disables or deletes an account must therefore delete its tokens. See `interaction/user_admin-*`. | n/a: superseded. Since rc.25 both resolvers run the disabled gate on the token's owner (Verified finding 4), so a token of a disabled account is refused even if it survived; `kind` is read only for the machine label. Disabling and erasing revoke the tokens (nothing is deleted). The current behaviour is proved by `TestResolvePrincipal_ADisabledAccountIsRefused`. |

#### Per-address rate limit (REST preset, platform ≥ 1.11)

The gateway limits unauthenticated endpoints per client address (default 30 requests per minute, burst 10). The preset decides what spends a token:

| Endpoint | Mode | Why |
|---|---|---|
| SignIn, ConfirmSignUp, VerifyMfa, ResetPassword, VerifyEmail, CliToken, OAuthCallback, OAuthExchange | `failed_only` | Each checks a guessable secret: wrong guesses spend the limit, correct answers are free. |
| SignUp, RequestPasswordReset, OAuthAuthorizeURL | every request (default) | They do their work, such as sending mail or creating a row, when they **succeed**: a registration from a fresh address never fails, and RequestPasswordReset always answers success. |
| All bearer-authenticated endpoints | not limited by this mechanism | They are attributed to an identity. RequestEmailVerification (mail to the caller's own address) is unbounded per account. |

| ID | MUST hold | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| `auth/public-limit-modes` | The table above is what the preset declares. A project that exposes these RPCs on a transport other than the REST preset must provide its own limit. | none (live only) |

The limit is per client address only as far as the gateway can see the address. Behind an HTTP terminator (a reverse proxy, a load balancer, a server-side app relaying the request), the gateway keys each request by the address of the nearest peer it does not trust as a proxy: with `<PREFIX>_PUBLIC_RATELIMIT_TRUSTED_PROXIES` not naming the terminator, that is the terminator, and every caller shares one bucket — a few wrong passwords a minute from anyone then refuse every sign-in. The README's "Behind a proxy" says what to set. SignIn's guess budget (`sign_in_turnkey/failure-budget`) does not depend on it.
| `auth/in-plugin-guess-budgets` | Independently of the gateway: 5 attempts per MFA challenge (atomic counter); at most `mfa_max_challenges_per_window` challenges per user per window, counted under a row lock, and a new challenge supersedes pending ones; `sign_in_failure_budget` password attempts per account per window, then only from a known device within its own budget (`sign_in_turnkey/failure-budget`); 5 attempts per pending sign-up; the step-up locks for 15 minutes after 5 wrong codes; a TOTP step is accepted once. | TestVerifyMfa_LocksOutAfterMaxAttempts, TestVerifyMfa_ConcurrentGuessesShareOneBudget, TestMfaIssuance_CapRefusesOnceTheWindowIsSpent, TestMfaIssuance_CapHoldsUnderConcurrency, TestMfaIssuance_NewChallengeRetiresThePendingOnes, TestSignUpConfirmation_WrongCodesAreRefusedAndExhaustTheRow, TestStepUp_FiveWrongCodesLockIt, TestTotp_ConcurrentReuseIsAcceptedOnce, TestSignIn_BudgetSpent_AnUnknownDeviceIsRefused |
| `auth/cheap-gates-before-hash` | SignUp runs the allowlist and invite gates **before** any password hash, so a refused registration costs no hashing work. | TestSignUpAllowlist_GatesThePlainSignUp |

#### Events

| ID | MUST hold | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| `auth/events-secret-carriers` | Exactly four events carry a plaintext secret, for a hand-written delivery channel: `PasswordResetRequested.token`, `EmailVerificationRequested.token`, `SignUpConfirmationRequested.code`, `MfaChallengeRequested.code` (event-code path only; blank when TOTP is used). The secret comes from the request and is never persisted; only its hash is stored. | `auth/events-no-credentials` | No event carries a bearer token, a password or password hash, a TOTP seed, a recovery code, an invitation token, or a client secret. | `auth/events-after-commit` | An event is emitted only after its row commits. A rolled-back write (for example a failed pending sign-up transaction, a refused MFA issuance or a rolled-back acceptance) emits nothing, and therefore sends no mail. | none (live only) |
| `auth/events-only-real-accounts` | `PasswordResetRequested` is emitted only for an existing account, so a subscriber never mails a non-account address on the plugin's behalf. | TestRequestPasswordReset_UnknownEmail_NoOp |

#### Secrets at rest

| Secret | Stored as | ID / Proof |
|---|---|---|
| Account password, PendingSignUp password | PHC string from `password_hash_algo` (argon2id default; bcrypt, scrypt, pbkdf2). Verify reads the algorithm and parameters from the stored hash, so changing the knob affects new hashes only. There is no rehash on login (the rotate callback is nil). | `auth/password-hash-algo` — TestSignUpThenSignIn_TheSamePasswordWorks |
| Bearer token, password-reset and verification token, invitation token, CLI code | sha256 hex in a unique column | `auth/high-entropy-secrets-sha256` — TestNewBearerToken, TestInviteToOrg_ReturnsThePlaintextOnceAndStoresTheHash, TestAcceptOrgInvite_ConsumesTheHashNotThePlaintext, TestRequestEmailVerification_StoresHash |
| MFA event code (6 digits), sign-up code (8 base32 characters), AuthClient secret | Password hash (slow), because the space is small or the secret is long-lived | `auth/low-entropy-secrets-slow-hash` — TestRegisterClient_IssuesASecretAndStoresOnlyItsHash, TestVerifyMfa_EventCodePath |
| Recovery codes | SHA-256, single use | TestConfirmTotp_HandsOutTenRecoveryCodesStoredHashed, TestVerifyMfa_ARecoveryCodeSignsInOnce |
| TOTP seed | `CRYPTED_SECRET` (project field keyring). A legacy secretbox seed under `two_factor_secret_key` is moved to the new column the first time it is used. If the legacy key is unusable, the refusal is `TOTP_UNAVAILABLE`, not a sign-in. | `auth/totp-seed-crypted` — TestEnrollTotp_NeedsNoPluginKey, TestLegacySeed_VerifiesAndMoves, TestLegacySeed_KeyGone_Refuses |
| OAuth client secret | `CRYPTED_SECRET`. A legacy PASSWORD-typed `client_secret` is moved on first use and the legacy column is cleared. A provider with no secret is refused as misconfigured. | `auth/oauth-secret-crypted` — TestOAuthProvider_SecretFromEncryptedColumnOrMovedFromLegacy, TestOAuthCallback_EmptyClientSecret_Rejected |

#### Configuration that must fail at boot

| ID | Condition | Enforced | Proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| `auth/boot-password-algo` | `password_hash_algo` is not one of argon2id, bcrypt, scrypt, pbkdf2 (or pbkdf2-sha256) | `RegisterPlugin` | `TestRegisterPlugin_UnknownPasswordHashAlgoFailsBoot` (unknown values, including a wrong case, fail; every supported spelling boots) |
| `auth/boot-new-devices` | **Retired:** `two_factor_scope=new_devices` without `devices` is valid (device identity is base tier) | `ValidateConfig` | `TestValidateConfig_NewDevicesNeedsNoFeature` |
| `auth/boot-api-ttl` | `api_token_default_ttl_hours` greater than 100 years (would overflow the duration type) | `ValidateConfig` | TestAPITokenExpiry_OutOfRange |
| `auth/boot-public-session-ttl` | An explicit `public_session_ttl_seconds` > 0 longer than the session lifetime (`session_token_ttl_seconds`, or its 30-day default): a public-computer session must not outlive a normal one. The manifest default 0 (8 hours, capped at the session lifetime) MUST boot whatever the session lifetime — the bundle fills an unset knob with that default. | `ValidateConfig`. At use the public lifetime is also capped at the session lifetime. | `TestPublicSessionTTL`, `TestPublicSessionTTL_TheManifestDefaultBootsWithShortSessions` |
| `auth/boot-allowlist` | A `sign_up_allowlist` entry that is neither `name@domain.tld` nor `@domain.tld` | `ValidateConfig`. At runtime, a malformed list refuses everyone. | TestParseSignUpAllowlist, TestSignUpAllowlistGate |
| `auth/boot-feature-graph` | `requires` / `conflicts_with` violations, such as tenant_scope with oauth | codegen | — |
| **Not** boot-checked (gaps) | an unknown `two_factor_scope` value (treated as `all_logins`, the stricter setting); an unknown `email_proof_required` (treated as `full`, also stricter); `email_proof_required` ≠ none without `email_verification` (inert); `invite_only=true` without `org_invite` (inert); a malformed `two_factor_secret_key` (fails per user, at use); an empty `oauth_redirect_base` (the redirect URI is derived from the request Host) | — | none |

#### Transactions (one unit of work)

When `DistTx` is nil, as in a hand-built test handler, the calls fall back to sequential ones. A wired bundle always uses the transaction coordinator.

| Flow | Writes held together | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| SignUp | CreateUser (+tenant) · signup roles · open-invite bind · RegisterDevice · IssueToken · RecordSignInEvent | `TestSignInHistory_SignUp` (the history row; the rest sequencing only) |
| SignUp under sc | open-invite bind · CreatePendingSignUp (+tenant) | none |
| ConfirmSignUp | ClaimPendingSignUp · CreateUser · roles · mark confirmed · supersede the address's other pending rows · RegisterDevice · IssueToken · RecordSignInEvent | none |
| SignIn, VerifyMfa, OAuthExchange, CliToken (the session) | IssueToken · RecordSignInEvent (the success row). Device trust after the commit; a refusal's row on its own. | `TestSignInHistory_SignIn_TheSuccessRowIsInTheSessionsTransaction` |
| OAuth create | CreateUser · roles · mark confirmed. **CreateOAuthIdentity is outside this transaction.** | none |
| CreateApiToken / IssueBotToken | IssueApiToken · every AddTokenPermission. Without this, a partly written subset would be read as "no subset", which grants every API-realm permission. | TestCreateApiToken_NarrowingFails_RollsBackTheLiveToken, TestIssueBotToken_NarrowingFails_RollsBackTheLiveToken, TestServiceAccount_TheWritesThatMustNotSeparate |
| CreateBot | CreateBotUser (+tenant) · membership · role | TestCreateBot_GrantFails_RollsBackTheAccountAndMembership |
| AcceptOrgInvite | ConsumeOrgInviteByToken · role-snapshot check · AddOrgMembership · AssignRoleToUser · mark derived | none |
| ResetPassword | ConsumePasswordResetToken · UpdateUserPassword. **Token sweep after commit.** | TestConsumePaths_TheTokenAndItsWriteDoNotSeparate, TestResetPassword_FailureAfterConsume_RollsBack |
| VerifyEmail | consume · MarkEmailVerified | TestConsumePaths_TheTokenAndItsWriteDoNotSeparate |
| ChangePassword | UpdateUserPasswordIfUnchanged · RevokeOtherSessionTokens | none (sequencing only: TestChangePassword_AFailedSignOutFailsTheChange asserts the error, not a rollback) |
| MFA issuance | LockUserForIssuance · budget count · supersede pending · CreateMfaChallenge | TestMfaIssuance_SupersedeAndCreateAreOneSeam, TestMfaIssuance_AFailedSupersedeMintsNothing |
| EnrollTotp / ConfirmTotp / DisableTotp | replace seed / confirm + recovery codes / delete seed + codes | TestEnrollTotp_AFailedCreateDoesNotLeaveTheUserWithoutAFactor, TestEnrollTotp_BothWritesGoThroughOneSeam |
| DisableUsers / membership update and removal | one multi-op statement set (`ops`) | none (live only) |
| EraseUsers (per account) | BeginUserErasure (tokens · devices · device details · disable · password, email proof and device trust) · each staged feature's eraser · SpendPasswordResetTokens · EraseSignInEvents · EraseUser (address and erased_at), in that order | TestEraseUsers_OneTransactionPerAccount (commit per account; a failing eraser rolls back). Without a coordinator: TestEraseUsers_ARetryCompletesAFailedErasureWithoutATransaction |

#### Performance

| ID | MUST hold | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| `auth/lookup-columns-indexed` | Every lookup used on a request path is served by a unique key or an index: `UserToken.token_hash` (and legacy `token`), `UserToken.user_id`, `UserToken.device_id`; `User.email`; `PasswordResetToken.token_hash`; `EmailVerificationToken.token_hash`; `CliAuthCode.code_hash`; `OrgInvite.token_hash` and `(org_id, email)`; `OAuthIdentity(provider_id, external_id)`; `Device(user_id, identifier)`; `UserRecoveryCode(user_id, code_hash)`; `PendingSignUp.email`; `MfaChallenge.user_id`; `OrgMembership(user_id, org_id)`; `Organization.slug`; `Tenant.domain`; `OAuthProvider.name`; `AuthClient(client_id, redirect_uri)`; `SignInEvent(user_id, created_at)` (the person's history), `SignInEvent(address, created_at)` (an erasure's address match, an operator's search) and `SignInEvent.created_at` (the admin list's order). | none (schema; live only) |
| `auth/authenticate-read-only` | Authenticate performs no write per request. The exceptions are the one-time legacy-token upgrade and, under `api_token`, the `last_used_at` write throttled to once a minute per token (`api_token/last-used-throttled`). A refused token costs one more read: the expiry probe after a miss (`base/expired-token-says-so`). It runs a fixed number of reads: token, then grants, then the API subset (ap); one per decorator (tenant; org slug or org list; machine label); one or two per narrower (inside an organization the org narrower reads the realm-wide roles too, for `realm_permissions`). | `TestAuthenticate_TouchesLastUsedAtThrottled`, `TestAuthenticate_LookupOutageIsNotProbedForExpiry` |
| `auth/no-expired-row-sweeper` | Nothing deletes an expired or revoked token: the `UserToken` table is the history of every credential (`device/revocation-is-a-state`; the per-user sweep at mint is retired). Nothing deletes expired `PasswordResetToken`, `EmailVerificationToken`, `MfaChallenge` or `CliAuthCode` rows. Expiry is enforced in the read predicate, but the tables grow without bound. Nor are pending sign-ups (claimed, superseded or expired) or lapsed invitations (closed on re-invite, never deleted): nothing in the auth tables is deleted (`auth/nothing-is-deleted`). | TestInviteToOrg_ClearsALapsedInviteFirst, TestInviteToOrg_ClosesALapsedInvitationAndKeepsIt, TestSignUpConfirmation_AnExpiredRegistrationStaysAndIsRefused |
| `auth/hash-cost-bounded` | The slow hash runs on: SignIn (always, including the dummy verify), SignUp (1, or 2 under sc), ConfirmSignUp (code verify), VerifyMfa (event code), ChangePassword (2), ResetPassword (after a valid link), OAuthExchange when it creates the account, a SignIn that opens an event-code challenge (one more), CliToken with a registered client, and RegisterClient. Every unauthenticated one is behind the per-address limit. | live only: from one client address, drive each public RPC that hashes past the burst with failing requests and assert 429 (the limit is the gateway's, `auth/public-limit-modes`). In-handler ordering is unit-proved by `TestSignUpAllowlist_GatesThePlainSignUp` and `TestChangePassword_HashesBeforeTheTransactionOpens`. |

---

### How to prove it — live-only coverage

A unit test against a fake store cannot prove the invariants below. An example project's end-to-end suite, run against a real database, the real gateway and the event bus, should cover them. The `fakeidp` sandbox covers OAuth.

1. **Conditional updates under concurrency** (fire N parallel requests and assert exactly one wins): ConsumePasswordResetToken, ConsumeEmailVerificationToken, ConsumeMfaChallenge, ConsumeCliAuthCode, ConsumeOrgInviteByToken, BindOpenOrgInvite, ClaimPendingSignUp, ClaimTotpStep, RecordMfaAttempt / RecordPendingSignUpAttempt / RecordStepUpAttempt / CountSignInAttempt / CountDeviceSignInAttempt (shared budget), UpdateUserPasswordIfUnchanged, and the LockUserForIssuance cap (`auth/in-plugin-guess-budgets`).
2. **Database constraints**: concurrent SignUp of one address → exactly one account, the rest `EMAIL_TAKEN`. Concurrent first-user sign-ups → **one** bootstrap administrator (`interaction/rbac-sign_up-first-user-race`). Duplicate `(provider_id, external_id)` on parallel OAuth callbacks.
3. **Transactions roll back on a real store**: inject a failure after the first write for SignUp, ConfirmSignUp, AcceptOrgInvite, CreateBot, CreateApiToken (subset), MFA issuance and ChangePassword; assert that no partial row exists and that no event was emitted.
4. **Revocation completeness**: after DisableUsers, every token class of the account (session, device-bound, CLI, API, bot) gets 401 on its next request, and its row is still there with `revoked_at`, `revoke_reason` and `revoked_by`. After an erasure, the same, plus the table-by-table state of `user_admin/erasure-keeps-every-row`, and a new sign-up with the old address. After ResetPassword, sessions and API tokens get 401. After ChangePassword, other sessions get 401 while the calling session and API tokens still work. IssueBotToken for a disabled bot must be refused (`interaction/user_admin-service_account-disabled-bot-token`).
5. **Expiry in SQL**: a token or code whose `expires_at` has passed is refused by Authenticate, VerifyMfa, ConfirmSignUp, ResetPassword, VerifyEmail, CliToken and AcceptOrgInvite.
6. **Gateway rate limit**: on one client address, with the limit on, `failed_only` endpoints allow unlimited correct answers and answer 429 after the burst of wrong ones. SignUp, RequestPasswordReset and OAuthAuthorizeURL answer 429 after the burst even when every request succeeds (`auth/public-limit-modes`).
7. **Events on the bus**: the four secret carriers arrive only for committed writes and only on the internal channel. No client-visible event stream ever shows them. No event payload contains a bearer token (scan every payload for the issued token string) (`auth/events-*`).
8. **Forwarded headers reach the handler**: Host (tenant resolution under ts), `User-Agent` / `X-Forwarded-For` (device label), the gateway's `x-w17-dpop-jkt` on every mint and Authenticate (device binding: a mint without it and a request whose proof is by another key must be refused end to end), and `W17-Org` header or path (om). Each feature depends on the gateway propagating its header, and the handler-level tests inject it directly.
9. **Cross-feature flows**: the second factor under `new_devices` for one device key, then the same key after an OAuth sign-in (`interaction/oauth-devices-two_factor-trust-laundering`), and after a password change and after a disable and re-enable (`interaction/credential-change-device-trust`); one key signing in to two accounts (`device/trust-per-user-device`); a revoked device's key refused at sign-in and on every request (`device/revoked-refused-for-good`); an API token driving CliAuthorize → CliToken (`interaction/api_token-cli_login-realm-escalation`, must be refused) and a session approving a CLI login whose session then approves the next (`cli_login/no-lifetime-extension`, every link ends with the first session); an org-scoped API token whose permission is granted only by a realm-wide session role (must not survive); an OAuth link into an unverified and into a verified local account.
10. **Secrets at rest on a real row**: legacy-token upgrade (seed a plaintext `token`, authenticate, assert `token_hash` set and `token` NULL); a TOTP seed and an OAuth client secret round-trip through the field keyring and move from their legacy columns.
11. **Feature-combination builds**: each `go_files_combined` pair with and without its partner feature. A table, column or request field named across a feature boundary is caught statically by `check-stage`.
12. **Nothing is deleted, on a real database** (`auth/nothing-is-deleted`): revoke a role grant, remove a membership, delete a role and an organization, remove a permission, withdraw and let lapse an invitation, confirm one of several registrations, disable and re-enrol an authenticator, replace a recovery-code set — and after each, diff the tables: every row still there with its state set, the ACL answer (Authenticate, `GetUserPermissions`) the one the delete gave, and the re-add (same grant, same member, the name or slug, the permission, the address, a new enrolment) accepted. The migration from rc.25 must keep every existing row live (`ended_key` `''`, every state column NULL).


### delivery (all features that mail a credential)

| id | invariant | category | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| `delivery/sealed` | A credential an event carries for a person — a reset or verification token, a sign-up, step-up or second-factor code — MUST travel sealed to `delivery_recipient` (age X25519, standard base64) in the event's `sealed_*` field, with the clear field empty; only the recipient's identity opens it. An event without a credential (a challenge the authenticator answers) carries neither. Every emit of those five events MUST map its `sealed_*` field. | Security | `TestDeliver`, `TestSealedDelivery_PasswordReset`, `TestSealedDelivery_EmailVerification`, `TestSealedDelivery_SignUpConfirmation`, `TestSealedDelivery_SecondFactor`, `TestSealedDelivery_StepUpCode`, `TestSealedDelivery_AnAuthenticatorChallengeCarriesNothing`, `TestSecretEvents_CarryTheSealedField` |
| `delivery/recipient-required` | An activation that stages a feature mailing a credential (`password_reset`, `email_verification`, `sign_up_confirmation`, `two_factor`, `step_up_code`) MUST refuse to boot without `delivery_recipient`, except with `W17_ENV=dev`, where the credential travels in clear as before. A value that is not an age recipient MUST refuse the boot anywhere. | Security | `TestCheckDeliveryRecipient` |
